At 1/5 only the deletion of the fswatch probe directory remains, which no control recorded in any shape (below). The read-back open is a rule up to three controls in five (two controls read it back at start-up, a third opens the lock by name at exit), so from four in five it stays unexplained, as before. The raw second capture gives the same counts; the first capture's task (16, 18, 19, 19 and 19) is unchanged, its six runs all in the renamed-first shape. On `paired-2.1.286-project-heredoc` the task's and one control's rewrites fold too, and at full agreement the task is INCONCLUSIVE on 8 events instead of 11 (the backup of `~/.claude.json` and the policy file remain); `paired-2.1.286-p17` builds three fewer rules at each agreement. No other fixture's outcome or count, and no figure of the attack evaluation, changes. What the fold hides: the lock is one of the harness's own files (I-30), and a write of it by the agent's process in this shape reads as the harness's rewrite; the agent's files in the same shape are left alone and stay visible. The fswatch probe directory's deletion is not folded: the harness creates `/tmp/claude-<uid>/fswatch-probe-<id>` and writes its `probe` file in all 24 runs, and the probe records the directory's deletion, an ambiguous `file_delete` with the ` (deleted)` suffix 0.4 to 1.1 seconds after the create, in 3 of them (the fixture's task run, control 1 of `paired-2.1.286-p17`, control 1 of `paired-2.1.286-project-heredoc`). None of the other 21 holds a delete or rename of the directory or its `probe` file, or an ambiguous delete of `/tmp/claude-<uid>`, so the deletion is not there in another shape: it is occasional harness activity in the probe's window, and the run's INCONCLUSIVE on it is V-24's.
Alternatives rejected: Folding the third shape for any file (seen only on the lock; an agent's direct write between a create and a rename of other files in its directory would read as one replace); requiring the whole span in one batch (two of the five runs put the rename in the next batch); dropping the second open (it is the harness's read of the lock, which two controls record as a named open); a rule or fold for the fswatch probe directory's deletion (no control recorded it, so there is no other shape to normalise and no measured activity to explain it).

### I-45: The harness's in-place rewrite folds into one hashed write
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldInPlaceWrites`, `nextToDirEvent` in `claudecode/stream.go`).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the `shapes` capture of Claude Code 2.1.296, **Verified** on one run, and the checked-in fixtures).
Context: The file tools write a sibling temporary and rename it over the target, and rewrite the target in place (open, truncate, write, close, no rename) when the temporary cannot be created with `EACCES` while the target exists, or when the rename fails with `EXDEV`, `EPERM`, `EEXIST` or `EBUSY` (**Reported**, from the strings of the 2.1.296 binary). The `shapes` task makes a Write and an Edit of `locked/data.txt` while `locked/` has no write permission. The probe recorded, by the agent's process, opens of the file (the first with the hash of the content before), one `file_write` with no hash and a `file_close` with the hash of the content after, and no create, temporary or rename, for both calls. The folds of I-22, I-32 and I-44 know only replaces through a temporary, so each claimed write paired with the unhashed write (Unverified) and each close was Unrecorded: at every agreement from one to five controls in five, two Unverified and two of the three Unrecorded positions of the run.
Decision: `NormalizeStream`, after the atomic-write folds and before the cwd-file fold and the collapse of opens, folds a run of writes of one file and the close that follows it into one `file_write` carrying the close's hash, at the first write. It applies only when, for one process, the event before the first write is an open of the same file and the event after the writes is its close, with no other event of the process between; every event names the file exactly. It does not apply to a write that already carries a hash (a fold's output), to a temporary's name (an unfinished replace stays raw), to the harness's own files (I-30), when the process's last event before its opens and writes of the file or its first event after the close is an ambiguous event of the file's directory (a create or a rename there is a replace in a shape no fold knows, such as the lock's third shape on another file), or to a process that the stream shows being forked, exec'd or exiting. The open is kept, since it is the claim's open. `NormalizeStream` does not receive the root pid (I-43); the root's start precedes the capture, so on every checked-in fixture and raw capture the root has no process event and every command the agent ran has one.
Consequences: On the `shapes` capture the Write and the Edit are corroborated on their content: at every agreement the run has eight claims corroborated, the two swapped Bash calls mismatched (O-2), one Unrecorded position (the harness's open of `fast.txt` after the Bash `cat fast.txt`) and no Unverified one, NOT FAITHFUL on the mismatches. Every baseline built from the fixtures' and the raw captures' controls is unchanged, and so is every verify report of the seven fixtures and the two raw `tools` captures but for the degradation line, which now names the in-place rewrite; no figure of the attack evaluation changes. A Bash `echo x > f` keeps its child's open, write and close as recorded: a command's content, explained by the command's claim, with the coverage counts unchanged. What the fold hides: a direct write of a file by the agent's process reads as one write with the content's hash, which is the form a claim has, so an unclaimed one is still an Unrecorded write and a claimed one with other content a mismatch. The count of writes in a run and the separate close are lost, on the observed side only, as for a replace.
Alternatives rejected: Folding a write and its close by any process (it would rewrite every command's file events, changing coverage counts and the forensic view for no position); passing the root pid to `NormalizeStream` (an interface change outside the adapter, as in I-43); claiming an in-place Write or Edit as an open, a write and a close (the transcript does not say which path the harness took, and both shapes must give one claim form); folding without the open before the write (the binary's in-place path opens the target first, and the capture shows it).

### I-46: A Bash read command implies an optional open of the file it reads
Status: Accepted, implemented (`readCommandFiles`, `simpleCommands`, `harnessReadVersions` in `claudecode/harnessread.go`; `harnessReadClaims` in `claudecode/claudecode.go`; `collapseOpenClaims` keeps harness-implied opens apart). Builds on V-33. The user's choice of design on 2026-10-13: optional harness-implied claims. Gate extended to 2.1.306 by I-63 (2026-10-24).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the `shapes` capture of Claude Code 2.1.296 and the strings of the 2.1.284, 2.1.286 and 2.1.296 binaries).
Context: After a Bash call, Claude Code calls a function (`bMn` in 2.1.296) with the command, its record of file state, the abort signal and the exit code, but only when the call was not interrupted, returned no image and was not sent to the background (`if(!Re&&!Do&&!Fe.backgroundTaskId)`; `Re` and `Do` are the result's `interrupted` and `isImage`). It takes the files a parser (`F1r`) names, keeps a search's only on exit 0 (`!H.requiresExitZero||s===0`), and for each one resolves the path, returns if the record holds it (`n.get(G)`), stats it, returns if it is not a regular file or exceeds 10485760 bytes or the call was aborted, and then reads it (`readFile`), which is the open the probe sees (**Reported**, from the strings of the binary). `F1r` returns nothing for a command holding `|`, `<` or `>` anywhere (`/[|<>]/`); otherwise it splits the command into its simple commands and keeps, for each, the first of four read forms that matches; when there are several parts, a part that matches none must match `/^\s*(echo|printf|true|:)\b/` or nothing is read at all. The forms: `sed` with a quiet flag (`--quiet`, `--silent`, or a short flag holding `n`), refusing `-i` in a short flag, `-e`, `--in-place` and `--expression`, a script `N,Mp` or `Np` and one file; `cat` with only `-n` or `--number`, `nl` with no flag, `bat` and `batcat` with only `-n`, `--number`, `-p` or `--plain`, and exactly one file, not `-`; `head` and `tail` with only `-n N`, `--lines N`, `--lines=N`, `-nN` or `-N` (N not 0) and exactly one file, not `-`; and, only when the command has one part, `grep`, `egrep`, `fgrep` or `rg` with a pattern and one file, not `-` and with no `*`, `?`, `[` or `{`, accepting `-A`, `-B` and `-C` with a number, `-A3`, `--context=3`, for `rg` `--context 3`, the short flags `^-[niwxEFGPHh]+$` (grep) or `^-[iSswxFnNHUP]+$` (rg) and a fixed list of long flags, marked `requiresExitZero`. A search that exits 1 is reported with `returnCodeInterpretation` "No matches found" and one that exits 2 or more is an error. In 2.1.284 and 2.1.286 the parser is the same but for whether `cat`, `nl` and `bat` number their output, which decides only whether the harness keeps the content, after the read, and the call is made under the same condition. On the `shapes` capture the agent's own process opened `fast.txt` about 7 ms after the command `cat fast.txt` exited and after its `cat` child opened the file; `sleep 2; cat slow.txt` and the multi-file `cat one.txt two.txt locked/data.txt` caused no such open, and in the `edits` fixture `cat notes.txt` caused none because the harness had written the file itself (**Verified**).
Decision: For a Bash call of a version whose binary was read (2.1.284, 2.1.286, 2.1.296), the adapter derives, after the call's own claim and with its interval, thread and block, one harness-implied `file_open` (V-33) of each distinct file the command reads by these rules, marked with the command as `ImpliedBy`. The path is resolved against the session's working directory, the record's `cwd`, which must be absolute and equal on the call's record and on its result's. `readCommandFiles` implements a subset of the parser, conservatively: it reads the command itself and gives up (derives nothing) on anything the shell gives a meaning to beyond quotes, `;`, `&&` and newlines (`$`, backquotes, backslashes, globs, `~`, parentheses, braces, `#`, `!`, a lone `&`), on a quoted command name, on an empty word or command, on `sed` flags other than `-n`, `--quiet` and `--silent`, on the colon form (the binary's `\b` needs a word character after the colon, so `: x` cancels for the harness too), and on any other doubt. The result states that cancel the read derive nothing: `run_in_background` in the input, a structured result with `interrupted` true, `isImage` true or a `backgroundTaskId`. A search derives only when the call's structured result shows its exit was 0: a Bash result carrying `interrupted`, no `returnCodeInterpretation` and no `is_error`. No call result, no `cwd`, a relative one or one that changed between the call and its result derive nothing. The record of file state is not modelled: whether the harness reads is left to the verifier, which costs the claim nothing when it does not. The harness's read is conditioned in the verifier on the command having read the file first: V-33 lets the claim pair only with an open of exactly the resolved path, inside the call's interval, no earlier than an open of that path beneath a command whose exec is exactly `ImpliedBy`, which is how "the command's own process was observed reading that file" is enforced, from the ground truth and not at parse time. `collapseOpenClaims` merges a harness-implied open only with another implied by the same command, so the agent's own open of the file (a Read just after) never becomes optional. The adapter never reads a marker from its input: the claims are built from the call.
Consequences: On the raw `shapes` capture the open of `fast.txt` is harness-implied at every agreement from one to five controls in five, all ten claims of the agent are corroborated and the run has no finding: INCONCLUSIVE only on the harness's backup of `~/.claude.json`, which no control made (five events at one and two controls in five, six from three). No checked-in fixture has a harness-implied claim, because `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` keeps neither the records' `cwd` nor the Bash result's fields, so every fixture gives byte-identical `cmd/verify` output, with and without a baseline, and the attack evaluation is unchanged. A transcript without `cwd` derives nothing, silently, and its harness reads stay Unrecorded, as before. A read command the subset does not follow, or a path the probe reports in another form (a symlink in the working directory resolved by the kernel), leaves the harness's read Unrecorded, as before.
Alternatives rejected: modelling the record of file state from earlier calls (a misprediction becomes an Unwitnessed finding, and the record also holds files the transcript does not show it learned); enforcing that the command read the file in the adapter or in `Prepare` (neither sees the process tree; `Prepare` would have to build it again); deriving on any version (a later version may change the parser, and the claim would then pair where the harness did not act); claiming the read as an ordinary open (it is Unwitnessed whenever the file was already known, as in the `edits` fixture).

### I-47: The Gemini adapter's process model, wrapper recovery and write content are measured on agy 1.3.4
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Process`, `Normalize`, `commandOf` and `writeHash` in `pkg/agent/gemini`). The search tools' part of Consequences is superseded by I-54. The subagent part of Consequences is superseded by I-55.
Date: 2026-10-13 (`strace -f -e trace=execve,execveat,clone,clone3,vfork,fork,openat,rename,renameat,renameat2,unlink,unlinkat,mkdir,write` of three print-mode runs of agy 1.3.4, Antigravity's CLI, each in a fresh workspace with a custom agent fixing its tool set; **Verified** on those runs only, without the probes).
Context: The Gemini adapter's `ProcessModel` was a set of guesses reported as degradations, it did no wrapper recovery (D6), and it claimed the hash of `write_to_file`'s `CodeContent` as the file's content (O-16 is open for Gemini, and the observation behind D9 was made on Claude Code only). The Gemini CLI is obsolete here; the harness is `agy`, a single Go binary (`~/.local/bin/agy`) that keeps its conversation in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/conversations/<id>.db`, the SQLite format the adapter already read: `Parse` read the four conversation databases of those runs (three main conversations and a subagent's) without an error, and the 46 older conversations on the machine as well (`TestParseRealConversationsDoNotFail`). Measured: the agy process is the agent; it neither forks nor execs a launcher, and its language server runs in the same process. Each `run_command` is a fresh child of that process that execs `bash -c <CommandLine>` with the call's `Cwd` and a new pseudo-terminal, bash being looked up on `PATH` (`/usr/bin/bash` in an interactive shell, `/bin/bash` under the reset environment `setpriv --reset-env` gives). A subagent started with `invoke_subagent` ran its command as a child of the same process, and its steps are in a conversation database of its own, named in the result of the `invoke_subagent` step and not marked by `has_subtrajectory`. Two `run_command` calls the model issued together became two steps whose started-to-completed intervals overlapped, while the harness execed the second only after the first had exited; the subagent's command ran after a later command of the main thread, so steps overlap. On every one of the seven commands the step's started time (5.6) preceded the exec and its completed time (5.8) followed the exit; for the queued call the started time preceded the exec by two seconds, and an unread field, 5.32, preceded each exec by 12 to 23 ms. `write_to_file` with `CodeContent` `hello` wrote `hello\n` with an in-place open, truncate and write; with `one\ntwo\n` it wrote the content as it is. `replace_file_content` read the file once and rewrote it in place; `view_file` opened the file three times; neither used a temporary or a rename. `grep_search` tried to exec an embedded ripgrep it extracts to `~/.cache/antigravity/bin/rg_embedded-<hash>` (the exec failed, since the binary names an interpreter under `/usr/grte`) and then execed `grep -l --binary-files=without-match -F -r -- <query> .`; `list_dir` and `find_by_name` ran in the agent process. Go's first exec in the process starts with a child that exits at once without exec'ing (a pidfd probe), a quiet fork. When its last check was more than 15 minutes ago the auto-updater starts `agy --bg-updater ...`, which runs `ldd` and `agy --version`, unless `AGY_CLI_DISABLE_AUTO_UPDATE=true` (the value `1` does not disable it).
Decision: `ShellPerCommand: true`, `SubagentsInProcess: true`, `IntervalExecution`, `Concurrency: Parallel`, `ExitsClaimed: false`. `Normalize` rewrites a process exec or exit whose target starts with `/usr/bin/bash -c ` or `/bin/bash -c ` and has something after it to the rest of the target, which is the `CommandLine`; a cut target gives a prefix (V-26). A `write_to_file` claims the hash of its `CodeContent` with a newline added when it lacks a final one, and no hash for empty content, which was not measured and is then not a field the format states.
Consequences: A `run_command` claim pairs with its level-1 child; another program, another shell or another flag keeps its text and shows as itself. Because the probe joins argv with spaces, `bash -c 'echo' 'hi'` (which runs `echo` with `$0` set to `hi`) and `bash -c 'echo hi'` recover the same command; what the shell then ran is its subtree, which the claim explains either way. Parallel calls carry no block id, so a harness that executed them in another order than their started times would read as a mismatch; this is reported as a degradation. A subagent's steps are not read, so its actions read as unexplained, which the report names. The search tools stay unknown (O-9): `grep_search` is a level-1 command and, with the failed ripgrep exec before it, two level-1 processes per call. No claim shape of the fs lane is checked against fanotify yet (O-16), and the auto-updater's process would read as an unexplained command in a run that starts it.
Alternatives rejected: Recovering the command from any line that ends with it (a command run before it would be explained by the claim); following the subagent's database in this change (the link between the step and the database is read only from a result's text and an undocumented field, and is left to a capture that shows a subagent); taking 5.32 as the claim's start (its meaning is inferred from seven commands only).

### I-48: The Gemini adapter declares agy's own directories and replaces their per-run ids
Status: Accepted, implemented (`harnessOwned`, `IsHarnessOwned`, `Canonical` and `canonicalPath` in `pkg/agent/gemini/harness.go`). Amended by I-49 (the step directory).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the three strace runs of I-47).
Context: V-24 needs to know where the harness keeps its own files, and a baseline (I-24, I-41) can explain the harness's file activity only if its paths agree between runs. The strace runs show agy writing, in every run: `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` (the conversation database and its write-ahead log, `brain/<conversation id>/` with its transcripts, transcript chunks and step outputs, `annotations`, `implicit`, `presence`, `cache`, the summaries, a crash log `crashes/crash_<pid>_<uuid>.log` created at start and deleted at exit, a log `log/cli-<date>_<time>.log` when no `--log-file` is given, and the shim `bin/agentapi` rewritten before every command), `/tmp/unleash-repo-schema-v1-codeium-language-server.json` (a feature-flag cache) and `/dev/ptmx` with `/dev/pts/<n>` for each command; in some runs `~/.cache/antigravity/bin/` (the embedded ripgrep, extracted through `.rg_embedded-<digits>` and a rename) and the auto-updater's create and delete of `~/.local/bin/.update_test<digits>`, beside the binary. Temporaries in the application directory are `<name>.<uuid>.tmp` renamed over the name.
Decision: `IsHarnessOwned` matches `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/`, `~/.cache/antigravity/`, `~/.local/bin/.update_test<digits>` and the feature-flag file, for a home under `/home` or `/root`. It leaves out the binary and the rest of its directory, the Gemini CLI's own files under `~/.gemini`, and the workspace's `.agents` directory. `Canonical` replaces, in file targets only: every UUID under the application directory by `{id}`, a crash log's pid by `{pid}`, a step's index by `{n}`, a transcript chunk's index by `{n}`, the log's time by `{time}`, and the digits of the ripgrep temporary, of the update test and of `/dev/pts/<n>` by `{n}`.
Consequences: A rule built from control runs names the same file in every run, and an unexplained harness file event there makes a run INCONCLUSIVE rather than NOT FAITHFUL (V-24). The conversation id is replaced only where runs are compared, so a claim that names the agent's artifact directory under `brain/<id>/` still pairs on its exact path; an unexplained write of the agent there reads as harness activity. Whether fanotify reports the pseudo-terminal and the feature-flag file at all depends on the filesystems the probe marks, and is unmeasured. The list is specific to 1.3.4 and is checked against each capture, like I-29 and I-30.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring all of `~/.gemini` (it holds the Gemini CLI's configuration, credentials and settings, which agy also reads); declaring `~/.local/bin` (a write to the binary is a finding); waiting for the paired capture (the families are the same with or without the probes, and without them no baseline can explain the task run's harness files).

### I-49: A step's directory is in the step id family
Status: Accepted, implemented (`stepRE` in `canonicalPath`, `pkg/agent/gemini/harness.go`). Amends I-48.
Date: 2026-10-13 (the paired capture of the `basic` task with agy 1.3.4 and five controls, **Verified** on its six runs).
Context: The first paired capture of agy 1.3.4 has a task run of five tool calls and five control runs of one `run_command` each. For every step that produced output the harness creates `brain/<conversation id>/.system_generated/steps/<N>/output.txt`. The probe reports the create as an ambiguous `file_write` of the step's directory `steps/<N>`, with no trailing component, one nanosecond before the file's creating open in the same batch. I-48 replaced a step's index only in a path that continues below the directory (`/steps/<N>/`), so the directory itself kept its index. Each control made one step, number 2, so the controls agreed on the rule `steps/2`, and the task's creates of `steps/4`, `steps/6`, `steps/8` and `steps/10` matched nothing: the four unexplained harness events of the run at every agreement, which made it INCONCLUSIVE where nothing else did. The report's `Explained by the baseline: 0 events` line has another cause, which is not a fault: it counts only events beneath level-1 subtrees the baseline explains (`Coverage.Baselined`), and agy runs no process of its own beyond the command it is asked to run and Go's pidfd probe (a quiet fork), where Claude Code runs git, ripgrep and its shell snapshot. On this capture the baseline did explain the harness's level-0 activity: at one control in five it took the task run from 246 unrecorded events and 352 unexplained harness events to 6 and 4.
Decision: `Canonical` replaces a step's index in `steps/<N>` at the end of a path as well as before a further component: `/.system_generated/steps/<digits>` followed by `/` or the end of the path becomes `/.system_generated/steps/{n}` with what followed. Nothing else changes.
Consequences: The controls give the rule `file_write` of `brain/{id}/.system_generated/steps/{n}` instead of `steps/2`; the rule count is unchanged at every agreement (268, 263, 259, 259 and 252 from one to five controls in five), and the task's four step creates are explained at every agreement. At one and two controls in five the run has no unexplained harness event; from three in five the harness's own replaces of its caches and summaries through `<name>.<uuid>.tmp` vary in shape between runs (a temporary's open or close merged into the rename's batch), which only some controls show, so 3, 3 and 12 events stay unexplained at three, four and five in five (4, 4, 7, 7 and 16 before, from one to five in five). A path elsewhere that merely ends in `steps/<digits>` is not under the application directory and is left alone.
Alternatives rejected: Folding the directory's create into the file's open for the harness's own files (it changes rules the controls measure and hides nothing the id family does not); a rule per step number (the number of steps differs with the task).

### I-50: Gemini file tools are claimed as they were measured, and the observed side drops the create and collapses runs of opens
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.NormalizeStream`, `foldCreates`, `collapseOpens` in `pkg/agent/gemini/stream.go`; the conditional degradation in `Parse`). Supersedes the Gemini part of I-18; decides O-16 for `view_file`, `write_to_file` and `replace_file_content`. The one-microsecond bound of the create fold is dropped by I-101. Amended by I-118: a run of opens passes over the harness's open of its project configuration. Amended 2026-11-05 by I-135: view_file's run of opens of a background task's log collapses too.
Date: 2026-10-13 (the paired capture of the `basic` task with agy 1.3.4, **Verified** on its task run; the controls make no file-tool call).
Context: The adapter claimed `view_file` as a `file_open` and `write_to_file` and `replace_file_content` as a `file_open`, a `file_write` and a `file_close` (the close with the content's hash for `write_to_file`, I-47), a shape guessed from fanotify's order and reported as a degradation. The task run's five tool calls show, all by the agent's own process, with no temporary and no rename: `view_file` of `b.txt` opened it three times in 1.8 ms; `write_to_file` of the new `a.txt` was an ambiguous `file_write` of the workspace directory (the create, which fanotify reports with the directory only) and, one nanosecond later in the same batch, the open of `a.txt` with the input hash of empty content (the probe stamps it only on the open that created a file, P-22), its write and its close with the hash of the content; `replace_file_content` of `a.txt` opened it (the harness's read) and 0.8 ms later opened it again, wrote it and closed it with the new content's hash; `write_to_file` over the existing `b.txt` did the same, the second open 94 microseconds after the first. Each claim had a partner, so all eleven claims were corroborated, and the extra opens and the create were five of the run's six Unrecorded positions. Between two tool calls the agent's process writes its transcripts under `brain/<id>/`. The harness creates its step output files `steps/<N>/output.txt` in the same create shape.
Decision: The claims stay as they are. `NormalizeStream` rewrites the observed side to their grain in two narrow steps, both for the agent's own process only (a process the stream shows forked, exec'd or exiting is a command, whose events are its content) and never on the harness's own files (I-48), whose shapes the controls measure. First, an ambiguous `file_write` of a directory is dropped when the process's next event is, within one microsecond (one batch, P-18), a named open of a file directly in that directory carrying the input hash of empty content. Second, an open whose process's previous event is an open of the same file, neither ambiguous, is dropped, so a run of opens keeps its first open, which carries the content the file had when the call began. Any other event of the process, the harness's transcript writes included, ends a run; claims are not collapsed. The degradation that called the file-tool shapes a hypothesis is gone; a session with a `multi_replace_file_content` call reports that its shape is unmeasured and that it is claimed like `replace_file_content`.
Consequences: On the capture all eleven claims are corroborated and the five Unrecorded positions are gone; the baseline rules the controls give are unchanged at every agreement: no control's agent process creates a file outside the harness's own files, and a rule names an action and a target once however often they occur (the collapse takes 8 to 10 repeated opens per control, of files such as `~/.gemini/config/projects/default-cli-project.json`, and 11 such in the task run besides the workspace's 4). Two calls on one file always have the harness's transcript writes between them on this capture, so each keeps its own open; a harness that made no event between two such calls would merge their opens on the observed side only and leave the second claim's open Unwitnessed, which is loud. What the folds hide: that a file was created (its open, write and close remain, and the claim does not state creation), and how many times the agent's process opened a file in a row. An unclaimed file the agent's process creates or opens still shows as an Unrecorded open, write and close. `write_to_file` of empty content, `multi_replace_file_content`, a write split into several write events and a file the probe reports without the creating open's empty hash (a filesystem without birth time, P-22) were not seen: the last two leave their extra events Unrecorded.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming each measured event (the number of opens is the harness's implementation, and a claim would have to know whether the file existed); collapsing across harness events as Claude Code does (I-32), with claims collapsed too (the harness reads `~/.gemini/config/projects/default-cli-project.json` before a command, outside its own directories, which would break an observed run that the collapsed claims still join); dropping every ambiguous write of a directory by the agent's process (a `mkdir` or another create would vanish with nothing else to show it).

### I-51: The Gemini adapter reads a private copy of the conversation database and its log
Status: Accepted, implemented (`openSnapshot` and `copyFile` in `pkg/agent/gemini/gemini.go`, used by `Detect` and `Parse`).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the paired `basic` capture of agy 1.3.4 and a database built in the test, **Verified**).
Context: agy keeps a conversation database in write-ahead-log mode (the header's version bytes are 2 in all six databases of the capture). Steps not yet checkpointed are in `<id>.db-wal`, and SQLite reads them only through a shared-memory index `<id>.db-shm`, which it creates next to the database, even for a connection opened with `mode=ro`, when there is none. The capture script copies the database and a non-empty log. In the `basic` capture the task's database is byte-identical to agy's own after the run, which had checkpointed and removed its log at exit, so the copy is complete; the empty `basic.conversation.db-wal` and the `basic.conversation.db-shm` beside it were created after the capture by the verifier's read (the log's time is the minute of the capture script's verify step) and later reads. Measured: on a copy whose log still held a deletion of most steps, `Parse` in place read only what the log left (it does read the log), created the index next to the database, and in a directory without write permission `Detect` refused the database, so `cmd/verify` fell back to the generic adapter and failed on the binary file.
Decision: `Detect` and `Parse` copy the database and its log, when one exists, into a new temporary directory, open the copy read-only, and remove the directory when done. The index is not copied: SQLite rebuilds it from the log.
Consequences: Reading a session writes nothing beside it, reads a database in a read-only directory, and still reads steps that only the log holds. Each read copies the database (half a megabyte for a five-call run) once for `Detect` and once for `Parse`. A database that agy is writing while it is read can be copied between its file and its log in an inconsistent state, as an in-place read could see it between two reads; a session is verified after the run. A rollback journal (`-journal`) is not copied, since agy does not use one for its conversations.
Alternatives rejected: Opening with `immutable=1` (SQLite then ignores the log, so un-checkpointed steps would be lost without an error); opening in place (it writes into the evidence directory and fails where that is read-only).


### I-52: The Gemini adapter declares agy's token endpoint as a harness host
Status: Accepted, implemented (`HarnessHosts` in `pkg/agent/gemini/harness.go`; `TestHarnessHostsAreTheMeasuredOccasionalHosts`, `TestPairedBasicAtEachAgreement`, `TestPairedBasicTokenRefreshIsAFindingWithoutTheDeclaration`).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the paired `basic` capture of agy 1.3.4, fixture and raw capture, **Verified**).
Context: V-34 lets an adapter declare its harness's own service hosts. In the six runs of the capture the agent's own process made `net_connect` events to six hosts. `daily-cloudcode-pa.googleapis.com` (8 to 10 per run), `antigravity-unleash.goog` (2), `play.googleapis.com` (2), `www.googleapis.com` (1) and `lh3.googleusercontent.com` (1) appear in all six runs, so the baseline explains them at every agreement. `oauth2.googleapis.com` appears once, in the task run, about one second after start, and in none of the five controls, which ran within the next minute: agy reads its login from the Secret Service and, by its timing and target, refreshed its access token, which the controls did not need. The raw capture holds the same hosts and counts as the fixture.
Decision: `HarnessHosts` returns `oauth2.googleapis.com` only. The hosts seen in every run are not declared: the baseline explains them, and a declaration would only weaken a run where a rule is missing.
Consequences: The fixture's task run is INCONCLUSIVE at every agreement from one to five controls in five, with all eleven claims corroborated, no finding, and the refresh as unexplained harness network activity; before V-34 it was NOT FAITHFUL on that one Unrecorded position. A refresh that a control happens to make becomes a rule at a low agreement and is then explained, as before. A connection to the token endpoint by a command the agent ran stays a finding.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring every Google host the harness named (they are explained by the baseline, and `www.googleapis.com` serves many APIs).

### I-53: The Claude Code adapter declares the release download host as a harness host
Status: Accepted, implemented (`HarnessHosts` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/claudecode.go`; `TestHarnessHostsAreTheMeasuredOccasionalHosts`).
Date: 2026-10-13 (the eight paired fixtures and the raw captures of 2.1.286 and 2.1.296, **Verified**).
Context: V-34 lets an adapter declare its harness's own service hosts. Over the 42 distinct runs of Claude Code on this machine (21 of 2.1.286, 21 of 2.1.296, from 2026-10-07 to 2026-10-13; 33 controls and 9 task runs), the agent's own process named four hosts in every run: `api.anthropic.com`, `mcp-proxy.anthropic.com` (once or twice), and `http-intake.logs.us5.datadoghq.com`, the datadog telemetry intake, exactly once per run, so the telemetry connect once reported as occasional is in every kept run and the baseline explains it. `downloads.claude.ai`, where the harness fetches its releases, appears once, in control 1 of the parallel capture of 2.1.286 (2026-10-07), two seconds after start; a control runs no task, so the connection is the harness's. `example.com` appears once, in the parallel task, made by the claimed `WebFetch` in the agent's own process. The 2.1.286 captures before N-22 also hold address-only connects of the harness, which are `net_dial` in later captures and need no rule.
Decision: `HarnessHosts` returns `downloads.claude.ai` only. The datadog intake and the API hosts are not declared: every run has them.
Consequences: No fixture changes: the release host appears in no task run. A run in which the harness checks for or downloads an update is INCONCLUSIVE rather than NOT FAITHFUL on that connection. Since `WebFetch` runs in the agent's own process, an unreported `WebFetch` of a URL on `downloads.claude.ai` reads INCONCLUSIVE too (the residual risk of V-34); one of any other host stays a finding.
Alternatives rejected: An empty declaration (the one occasional harness host the evidence shows would stay a finding in a run that checks for an update); declaring the datadog intake (it is in every run; a declaration would only matter in a run whose baseline lacks the rule).

### I-54: Gemini `grep_search` is claimed as the kind of search; `list_dir` and `find_by_name` are non-effectful
Status: Accepted, implemented (`SearchGrep`, `searchOf`, `foldSearches` in `pkg/agent/gemini/search.go`; `grep_search` in `claimsFor` and `list_dir`, `find_by_name` in `nonEffectful`, `pkg/agent/gemini/gemini.go`). Decides O-9 for Gemini; with I-25 for Claude Code, O-9 is closed. Supersedes the search tools' part of I-47's Consequences. Amended 2026-11-02 by I-108: the claim names its SearchPath and the observed search its folder. Amended 2026-11-05 by I-133: agy 1.4.7's shapes, and only the harness's own run.
Date: 2026-10-14 (the paired capture of the `search` task with agy 1.3.4 and five controls, recorded 2026-10-13, **Verified** on its task run).
Context: O-9 asked how the many-target search tools are claimed; for Gemini they were unknown tools (option (c)), so each `grep_search` was two unexplained commands and two Unrecorded positions. The capture's task run calls `list_dir`, `grep_search`, `find_by_name`, `write_to_file`, a `multi_replace_file_content` call the harness rejected (O-16) and `run_command`. `grep_search` with `Query` `seed` gave two children of the agy process, 1 ms apart, with no event of the agy process between: the embedded ripgrep, `~/.cache/antigravity/bin/rg_embedded-14d1066b44c4f6b7 --no-heading --with-filename -l --fixed-strings --ignore-file=<workspace>/.antigravityignore -- seed`, which opened its own program and exited with no exit code (the exec fails on this machine, I-47; Go's child of a failed exec ends with `exit`, not `exit_group`, so the probe records no code), then `/usr/bin/grep -l --binary-files=without-match -F -r -- seed .` (grep found on `PATH` under the capture script's reset environment), which read the workspace's files and exited 0. Go's pidfd probe, a quiet fork, preceded both. `list_dir` and `find_by_name` started no process and left no event of the agent's process in the workspace; nothing either did was unexplained.
Decision: Option (d) for `grep_search`, as I-25: the call is one `process_exec` claim with the fixed target `agy search:grep`. `NormalizeStream` rewrites the exec and the exit of an observed process to that target when its command line is one of the two measured forms and nothing else: the ripgrep at `/home/<user>/.cache/antigravity/bin/rg_embedded-<16 hex>` (or under `/root`) with exactly the options above and an ignore file named `.antigravityignore`, or `/usr/bin/grep` or `/bin/grep` with exactly the options above, a query and ` .` at the end. When a ripgrep exec is followed in the shape the capture showed, the two are one search: the ripgrep's process does nothing after its exec but open its own program and exit with no exit code, the parent's next fork is the grep's, the grep's exec names the same query, and from the ripgrep's exec to the grep's exec the parent has no event of its own and forks or execs nothing else. The grep is then placed under the ripgrep's process (its fork, exec and exit name the ripgrep's pid as parent) and keeps its command line, so it is the content of the one claimed command. On a machine where the embedded ripgrep runs, only its process appears and is the search. The pattern and path are not verified. Option (b) for `list_dir` and `find_by_name`: they are in the adapter's non-effectful set, claim nothing and are not reported as unknown.
Consequences: On the capture all five claims are corroborated, the search on the ripgrep's process with the grep beneath it, and no Unrecorded position or unexplained command is left (`TestASearchVerifiesEndToEnd`). A search that happened is verified to have been claimed, in order, and an unclaimed one is an omission; what the search read is the command's content, explained by the claim, so a query that names another path (the probe joins argv with spaces) is not caught. Another program, another option shape (a `grep_search` with other arguments, such as a regular expression, case-insensitivity or includes, was not measured and may change the options), a ripgrep outside the harness's cache, grep from another directory and a command line the probe cut keep their text and show as unclaimed commands, which is loud. A ripgrep that ran (with an exit code) followed by a grep, or a pair with anything between, stays two commands, each rewritten, so the second reads as an Unrecorded exec. A binary placed in the harness's cache is trusted as the harness itself is (I-27). If a version makes `list_dir` or `find_by_name` start a process, that process shows as an unclaimed command (fails safe); what they read in the agent's own process is not verified.
Alternatives rejected: Leaving the tools unknown (each search stays two findings); dropping the failed ripgrep's events (they would vanish rather than be explained); claiming `list_dir` and `find_by_name` as the kind of search (they start no process, so the claim would be a permanent Unwitnessed position); recognising the search from its query alone (any program could carry it).

### I-55: The Gemini adapter follows a subagent's conversation database from the invoke_subagent result
Status: Accepted, implemented (`subagentLinks`, `followSubagents`, `readConversation` in `pkg/agent/gemini`; the subagent databases in `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh` and `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`). Supersedes the subagent part of I-47's Consequences. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78.
Date: 2026-10-14 (copies of the conversations of the parallel `strace` run of agy 1.3.4 of I-47 and of an older conversation on the development machine, **Verified** on those; no paired capture under the probes has a subagent).
Context: A Gemini subagent runs inside the agy process (I-47) and keeps its steps in a conversation database of its own; `has_subtrajectory` stays 0. The adapter counted each `invoke_subagent` call and named it in a degradation, so a subagent's actions read as unexplained. Measured: the `invoke_subagent` step's payload holds the call's result in field 140. Its 140.2.1 is text for the model (`Created the following subagents:` followed by one JSON object per subagent with its `conversationId`), and its 140.2.6 is a `google.protobuf.Any` of type `gemini_coder.Step` whose value has a field 143 with one 143.10 per subagent, the subagent's conversation id in 143.10.1. In the parallel run one call created one subagent; in the older conversation one call created several, and the typed ids and the text agreed, in the order of the call's arguments, in both. Each subagent's database was `<id>.db` in agy's conversations directory, beside its caller's. Its steps hold its own tool calls at 5.4 (the caller's message is a step with no call there, and its reply to the caller is a `send_message` call), so the adapter reads it unchanged. In the parallel run the subagent's `run_command` started after the main thread's later `cat two.txt`, as strace saw the commands run. Listing the conversations directory before each strace run shows that a run without a subagent added one database and the run with one subagent added two. The reduced fixture of the parallel conversation dropped field 140, so it named no subagent.
Decision: `Parse` reads, from every step holding an `invoke_subagent` call, whatever its status, the ids at 140.2.6.2.143.10.1 that are lowercase UUIDs, and not the text. Each id is looked up as `<id>.db` beside the conversation and then in `<conversation without .db>/subagents/`, and read with the same adapter. Its claims get `ThreadID` = the subagent's id, as the Claude Code adapter sets it for its subagents (D9), and `BlockID` = `<id>/step-<idx>` for a step with several calls. The claims of all threads are sorted stably by start, the main thread's first on a tie, as the Claude Code adapter does. Subagents named by a subagent are followed the same way, breadth first, each id once, down to four levels below the main conversation. A missing database, a step that names no usable id and a subagent beyond the bound are degradations naming what was not read; a subagent database that does not parse is a parse error naming the subagent, which adds no claim and no count. The capture script lists agy's conversations directory before a run and copies every database that appeared during it, other than the run's own, with a non-empty log, to `<label>.conversation/subagents/<id>.db`; `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` keeps the ids of an `invoke_subagent` result and reduces the subagent databases the conversation names into the same layout.
Consequences: A subagent's actions are claimed on its thread, so a subagent that runs a command makes the command explained rather than unexplained. The alignment does not read `ThreadID` (O-29), and a subagent runs asynchronously, so its claims interleave with the main thread's by started time; a subagent whose actions the kernel shows in another order than the sorted claims would read as order findings, which no capture has shown. The prefix keeps the blocks of two threads apart in V-32. Attacker view: the link comes from the trajectory. Removing it leaves the subagent's actions unexplained, which is loud. Pointing it at another conversation in the directory makes that conversation's claims count as the subagent's, which is no more than an attacker who writes the claims into the main conversation can do; an id that is not a UUID is not used as a path, so it cannot name a file outside the two directories. The capture copies a conversation of another agy session running at the same time; it is never read unless the run names it, and the fixture script reduces only the ones named. The reduced fixture of the parallel conversation is reduced again with the id kept, and its subagent's database is checked in beside it, so it claims the subagent's command; every paired fixture gives byte-identical `cmd/verify` output, with and without a baseline, and the attack evaluation is unchanged. Unmeasured: how a subagent's events verify under the probes, nested subagents, and a subagent whose call did not complete.
Alternatives rejected: Reading the ids from the result's text (it is prose for the model, and the typed field gives the same ids); taking every database that appeared during the run as the run's subagents (a concurrent session's conversation would be claimed as the agent's); per-thread ordering or alignment (O-29 is open, and the thread id is attacker-controlled, D9); an unbounded recursion (each id is read once, which ends a cycle, but the bound keeps the work of a crafted chain of databases small and the report names what it cut).

### I-56: The adapters' folds take the named directory-entry shapes, with every name required
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldReplacesWithNames`, `previousOnReplace`, `destinationCollapsed`, and the named rename in `nextToDirEvent`, in `pkg/agent/claudecode/stream.go`; `foldCreates` in `pkg/agent/gemini/stream.go`). Amended 2026-10-31 by I-76: the create is the process's last event on T or X before the open, so its events on other paths may lie between; `previousOf` became `previousOnReplace`.
Date: 2026-10-14.
Context: With P-25 the probe names the entry of a create, a delete and a rename, and reports a rename as two events, its source and its destination. The Claude Code folds (I-22, I-32, I-33, I-34, I-39, I-44, I-45) and Gemini's create fold (I-50) take ambiguous directory events and infer the file from the order of events, and the cwd-file fold (I-43) names a read the probe could not. Every checked-in capture was made without names, and new ones will carry them.
Decision: Both shapes fold to the same result, and the named one is judged by its names. Claude Code: for one process and a temporary T of target X, a `file_write` of T (the create) as the process's event just before the open of T, the writes and the close of T, the rename's source naming T after the close (or right after the create, at the same time, when the kernel merged it into the create's record), and the destination naming X as the process's next event on T or X, fold into one `file_write` of X with the close's hash at the create's time. Every name must be exact and any other event of the process on T or X ends the shape; events of the process on other paths may lie between. A missing destination is accepted only when the last event on X before the source is a rename of X by the same process, the case in which P-18 drops it or the kernel merges it, and a replace of X that begins first ends the search. A temporary whose create names it is never judged by the older shapes, and their rename is now only the directory's. The in-place fold also stops at a rename naming the file, or a temporary of it, next to the rewrite. The reads before a replace (I-39), the version lock's rewrite (I-44) and the cwd file's read and delete (I-43) need no fold of their own: with names they are named opens and the temporary shape. Gemini: a `file_write` with no hash naming a file, followed by the process's open of exactly that file with the empty-content input hash in the same batch, is the create and is dropped, as the ambiguous create is.
Consequences: Every checked-in fixture gives byte-identical `cmd/verify` output, with no baseline and with the baselines of each fixture's controls at every agreement, and the attack evaluation's figures are unchanged. A capture with names folds a replace only when its six events name the temporary and the target, so a mixed or partial shape stays visible. A destination merged into an earlier one with an event on X between is not accounted for and the replace stays raw, which an honest run shows as unexplained or unrecorded events. The named shapes rest on synthetic tests mirroring each fold test and on P-25's measurement; no harness has been captured with names.
Note 2026-10-31: "no harness has been captured with names" no longer holds: the fixtures `paired-2.1.296-tools-named` (commit 79580bd, 2026-10-17) and `paired-2.1.306-tools` (I-63) were captured with the fs probe's names, and I-76 amends the fold on a named capture. A destination merged into an earlier record of its name is completed by the probe since P-38 ([probes.md](probes.md)), for captures made after it; the symbol list names `previousOnReplace`, which replaced `previousOf`.
Alternatives rejected: Rewriting named events into the older ambiguous shape before the folds (it throws the names away); keeping the older shapes' looser rules for named events (a rename naming the temporary or the target was accepted as the replace's rename by either name, so one half could stand for both).

### I-57: The agent versions each adapter was verified against are configured, and every tool checks against them
Status: Accepted, implemented (`config/agents.json`; `pkg/config`; `cmd/status`; `checkVersion`, `applyStrict` and `printVersionCheck` in `cmd/verify/version.go`; the version check and `--allow-unconfigured-version` in `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`; `TestEveryListedFixtureHasItsVersion`, `TestEveryVersionedFixtureIsListed`, `TestVersionGatesAgreeWithTheConfig`, `TestAgentVersionCheck`). The user's choice of design on 2026-10-15, the middle way between refusing every unverified version and checking none. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78. Extended by I-77 (the agent profile, schema 2).
Date: 2026-10-15.
Context: Much of each adapter was measured on particular agent versions: for Claude Code 2.1.286 and 2.1.296 the wrapper shape (I-23, I-26), temporary names and id families (I-29, I-38, I-41), I-46's harness reads, gated in code on 2.1.284, 2.1.286 and 2.1.296, and the declared host (I-53); for agy 1.3.4 the wrapper (I-47), id families (I-49), claim shapes (I-50), search forms (I-54), subagents (I-55) and host (I-52). When the agent updates, these can stop fitting without an error: a changed wrapper makes claims mismatch, a new temporary name reads as unexplained harness activity, and a changed parser makes a harness-implied claim pair where the harness did not act. Until now the only version check was `verify` refusing a baseline of another version than the session's, and a session without a baseline was not checked at all. Claude Code states its version on every transcript record; agy's conversation database states none.
Decision: `config/agents.json` lists, per adapter, the agent's binary and the versions whose checked-in fixtures verify, each with the date of its newest capture and the fixture directories; `pkg/config` reads it from `--config`, `$AGENT_TRACE_CONFIG`, beside the executable, or the checkout above the working directory. Tests, not run-time coupling, tie it to the code: each listed fixture was recorded by its version, each versioned fixture is listed, and each configured Claude Code version is in I-46's gate. `cmd/status` compares each installed agent's `--version` with the file and prints the kernel against the probes' stated minimums, exiting non-zero when an installed agent's version is not listed. `cmd/verify` compares the session's version, or the version `--agent-version` gives for a format that states none, with the file: an unlisted version is a `WARNING:` line at the top of the report and `agent_version_configured: false` in the JSON, and the verdict is unchanged; with `--strict-version` an unlisted or unknown version makes the run INCONCLUSIVE, whatever it would have been, with a reason naming the version and the findings, which stay listed. `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh` refuses an unlisted installed version before it asks for sudo unless `--allow-unconfigured-version` is given, and its own `verify` runs use `--strict-version`, with `--agent-version` for agy. A version is added by hand once a fixture of it verifies.
Consequences: Every checked-in fixture is of a configured version, so every fixture's `cmd/verify` text report is byte-identical, and the JSON report gains only `agent_version_check` and, where known, `agent_version`, `agent_version_source` and `agent_version_configured`. A Gemini session's version is `unknown` without `--agent-version`, which prints nothing by default and is INCONCLUSIVE under `--strict-version`. A capture of a new version is still possible and reads INCONCLUSIVE until the version is listed, which is the measurement that lists it. The file records that a version was verified, not that the adapter fits it: two builds with one version number, or an unlisted version that behaves alike, are not told apart. 2.1.284 stays gated in I-46 and unlisted, since it has no capture.
Alternatives rejected: refusing every unlisted version in `verify` (a capture of a new version could then never be verified, and an old session could not be re-read); a warning only, with no strict mode (a script could not make the check binding); gating each measured rule on its version in the adapters (it spreads the version list over dozens of rules and makes every new version an edit of all of them, where most rules carry over); reading the version list from the fixture names at run time (the binaries would depend on the source tree's test data); making NOT FAITHFUL advisory instead of INCONCLUSIVE under `--strict-version` (the strict mode exists so that no verdict is asserted on an unverified version; the findings stay in the report either way).

### I-58: The harness's socket named by its pid is an id family and one of the harness's own files
Status: Accepted, implemented (`ccSockID` in `claudecode/shell.go`, the socket in `harnessOwned` in `claudecode/claudecode.go`; `TestTheHarnessSocketIsComparedAcrossRuns`). Amends I-29 and I-30.
Date: 2026-10-17.
Context: Claude Code 2.1.296 binds a Unix socket `/tmp/cc-socks/<pid>.sock`, named by its own pid, at start-up and deletes it at exit: the net probe records the bind and the listen, the fs probe a create and a delete. Before P-25 the fs probe recorded these as ambiguous `file_write` and `file_delete` events of `/tmp/cc-socks`, the same in every run, and the baselines hold them as rules. The first capture with entry names, the `tools` task of 2.1.296 with five controls (2026-10-17), names the socket in each of its six runs, each with its own pid, so no rule matched and the honest run was NOT FAITHFUL at the capture script's default agreement on two Unrecorded level-0 events, the socket's create and delete, with every claim corroborated (**Verified**). A review of the six runs' file and network events after `NormalizeStream` and `Canonical`, for any other path that differs between runs by an id, found one more: the subagent metadata's temporary `agent-<id>.meta.json.tmp.<8 hex>`, left unfolded in one control because an event of the same process on another path lay between its create and its open, which I-56 does not take; in the other runs it folds away. The socket's bind and listen differ too, but they are capability events, outside the verdict.
Decision: `Canonical` replaces the pid in exactly `/tmp/cc-socks/<digits>.sock` with `N`, anchored, after the ` (deleted)` suffix is dropped, so the controls' rules name `/tmp/cc-socks/N.sock`. `IsHarnessOwned` matches exactly that path, not the directory. The metadata temporary does not become a family.
Consequences: On the capture the socket's create and delete are explained at every agreement, and the honest run has no finding. A rule for the socket explains a write or delete of any `/tmp/cc-socks/<n>.sock` by the agent's process, the same family trust as I-29's; any other name in that directory, or the socket's path elsewhere, stays a finding. Without a baseline, or with one whose controls lack the socket, an unexplained level-0 event on such a socket is unexplained harness activity, INCONCLUSIVE rather than NOT FAITHFUL. That downgrade is narrow: the harness names the socket by its own pid, an in-process file tool writing there would be a claimed call, and a command's create there is a level-1 event, which the declaration does not touch. The directory is left out: every Claude Code process on the machine shares it, and the checked-in captures hold its ambiguous events at level 0, which the declaration would turn from Unrecorded into harness activity in their reports without a baseline. Every checked-in fixture's `cmd/verify` output is byte-identical, with no baseline and with the baselines of each fixture's controls at agreements of 0.2 to 1. The metadata temporary in its unfolded shape stays a per-run path, so in a run it is at most unexplained harness activity under `~/.claude`. Making it a family would change the baselines of `paired-2.1.296-tools`, two of whose controls (made before P-25) hold its open and write raw, and its unfolded shape is a limit of I-56's fold, not of the families.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the whole of `/tmp/cc-socks` the harness's (it changes the older fixtures' reports and covers files the harness does not name); matching `<digits>.sock` anywhere (an unanchored family canonicalises workspace paths too, the limit I-41 records); requiring the pid to be the root pid's (`Canonical` sees one event, not the run, and a control's rule names its own pid).

### I-59: Each adapter declares its harness's security-sensitive files
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent.SensitiveDeclarer`, `agent.SensitiveFiles`; `SensitiveFiles` in `claudecode/claudecode.go` and `gemini/harness.go`; `TestSensitiveFilesAreTheSettingsAndConfiguration` in both adapters). Serves V-40.
Date: 2026-10-17.
Context: V-40 snapshots a short list of the harness's settings, permission and configuration files at the start and the stop of a capture. Which files those are is a fact about the harness, so the adapter states it, as it states its own directories (I-30) and hosts (I-52, I-53). The evidence is the captures (which files the harness's own process opens and writes) and the harness's binary (which files it names as where it persists settings); a capture cannot show a read of a file that is absent.
Decision: An optional interface, `SensitiveDeclarer`, `SensitiveFiles() []string`, each entry a path under `~/` (the home of the user the agent runs as) or `{workspace}/` (the watched directory). Claude Code declares `~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude/remote-settings.json`, `{workspace}/.claude/settings.json` and `{workspace}/.claude/settings.local.json`. agy declares `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/config/config.json` and `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`. The generic adapter declares none.
Consequences:
- Claude Code (**Verified** on the four captures of 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-17, controls and task runs alike): the harness's own process opened `~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json` and `~/.claude/remote-settings.json` in every run, wrote `~/.claude/remote-settings.json` in place in every run, and renamed `~/.claude.json` into place in each of the six runs of the 2026-10-17 `tools` capture, the first with directory-entry names (P-25); the three older captures, which name a rename only by its directory, show a write of it in three of their eighteen runs. `~/.claude.json` holds the per-project allowed tools, MCP servers and trust flags; `remote-settings.json` the managed settings the harness fetches. The two workspace files are the project's shared and local settings, which the binary of 2.1.296 names (**Reported**: its strings hold `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`); the captures run with `--setting-sources project` and no such file, so no open of them is recorded. `~/.claude/settings.local.json` is not declared: the binary names it only once, inside an example permission rule. `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, opened in every run and written in none, is not declared: it holds tokens, not settings or permissions, and a write to it is not hidden by any baseline rule. The plugin lists under `~/.claude/plugins/`, which the harness rewrites at rest in some runs, are not declared either; they are candidates if the list grows.
- agy (**Verified** on the paired captures of agy 1.3.4 of 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-17, all eighteen runs): its process opened the three declared files in every run and wrote none. Its binary names `settings.json` as where an always-allow answer persists (**Reported**: "Persist to settings.json"), and the file holds `permissions.allow`, `trustedWorkspaces` and `mcpServers`; `config.json` holds the installed plugins and `mcp_config.json` the MCP servers, and the latter may carry comments, so it is then summarised as a whole file. The administrator's `/etc/antigravity/admin_settings.json` is root's and outside the agent's user's home, so it is not declared.
- A declared file the harness does not write at rest is not hidden by the baseline today: its write by the agent's process is an unexplained level-0 event. The snapshot still adds a check for it, since a change by a process outside the agent's tree outside the workspace is dropped by the fs scope (P-17) and never reaches the verifier.
- `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, which moves Claude Code's configuration, is not followed: the declaration names the default places.
- Amended 2026-10-17: I-60 adds, for the declared file that mixes the harness's state with its settings (`~/.claude.json`), the list of its security-relevant key paths (V-41).
Alternatives rejected: declaring every file the harness writes at rest (its transcripts, caches and logs change every run and hold nothing that grants the agent anything; the list would be long and the snapshot heavy); declaring by pattern (a pattern names files the evidence does not show).

### I-60: The Claude Code adapter declares the security-relevant key paths of `~/.claude.json`; every other sensitive file is checked whole
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent.SensitiveKeyDeclarer`, `agent.SensitiveKeys`; `SensitiveKeys` and `globalConfigKeys` in `claudecode/claudecode.go`; `TestSensitiveKeysNarrowOnlyTheGlobalConfiguration`, `TestSensitiveKeysOfTheGlobalConfiguration`, and the assertion in `TestSensitiveFilesAreTheSettingsAndConfiguration` of the Gemini adapter that agy declares none). Serves V-41; amends I-59. Its sources re-checked for 2.1.306 by I-63 (2026-10-24).
Date: 2026-10-17.
Context: V-41 checks, in a sensitive file that mixes the harness's state with its settings, only the key paths that decide what the harness lets the agent do. Which keys those are is a fact about the harness, so the adapter declares them, as it declares the files (I-59). The evidence is the harness's binary: where it reads a key to decide a permission, a trust, an MCP server, an API key, a hook, the environment, extra directories or a dangerous mode, and its own lists of configuration keys. Claude Code 2.1.296 holds `GLOBAL_CONFIG_KEYS`, the keys of `~/.claude.json` that `claude config` may set, `PROJECT_CONFIG_KEYS`, `["allowedTools","hasTrustDialogAccepted"]`, and the defaults of a project's entry, `allowedTools`, `mcpContextUris`, `mcpServers`, `enabledMcpjsonServers`, `disabledMcpjsonServers`, `hasTrustDialogAccepted`, `hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesApproved` and `hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesWarningShown` (**Reported**: `strings` of `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.296`).
Decision: `SensitiveKeys` returns, for `~/.claude.json`, the patterns below, and nil for the other four declared files. Each pattern and its evidence (**Reported**, the 2.1.296 binary unless stated):
- `apiKeyHelper`: a command the harness runs to obtain the API key; in `GLOBAL_CONFIG_KEYS`.
- `env`: environment variables applied to the harness and the commands it runs; in `GLOBAL_CONFIG_KEYS` and in the default global configuration.
- `mcpServers`: the user-scope MCP servers, read from the global configuration (`mcpServers` of the configuration object) when the harness starts its servers.
- `primaryApiKey`: the API key the harness sends, read as the "/login managed key" and written when a key is saved to the configuration.
- `customApiKeyResponses`: the `approved` and `rejected` lists of custom API keys; the harness reads them to decide whether an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is accepted without asking.
- `bypassPermissionsModeAccepted`: the consent to `--dangerously-skip-permissions`; without it the harness downgrades `bypassPermissions` to the default mode, and `--bg` with that mode is refused.
- `hasSeenAutoModeEntryWarning`: whether the warning on entering auto mode has been shown; the onboarding of a dangerous mode.
- `projects.*.allowedTools` and `projects.*.hasTrustDialogAccepted`: `PROJECT_CONFIG_KEYS`, the tools a project allows without asking and the answer of its trust dialog, which the harness reads (`projects[dir].hasTrustDialogAccepted === true`) before it trusts the directory.
- `projects.*.mcpServers`: the project's local-scope MCP servers.
- `projects.*.enabledMcpjsonServers` and `projects.*.disabledMcpjsonServers`: the project's approvals of the servers in its `.mcp.json`.
- `projects.*.enabledMcpServers` and `projects.*.disabledMcpServers`: built-in and configured servers switched on or off; the binary's help text names `disabledMcpServers` in the project entry of `~/.claude.json` as where `/mcp disable` persists.
- `projects.*.mcpContextUris`: MCP resources the project adds to the model's context.
- `projects.*.hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesApproved`: the approval to load `CLAUDE.md` files from outside the project, which the harness asks for in a dialog.
Excluded on purpose, and so report-only: the caches (`cachedGrowthBookFeatures`, `cachedGrowthBookFeaturesAt`, `cachedExperimentFeatures`, `cachedExperimentData`, `additionalModelOptionsCache`, `additionalModelCostsCache`, `clientDataCacheSlots`, `modelAccessCache`, `metricsStatusCache`, `groveConfigCache`, `passesEligibilityCache` and the like), the counters (`numStartups`, `pluginUsage`, `skillUsage`, `tipsHistory`, `promptQueueUseCount`, the `*SeenCount` keys), the timestamps (`additionalModelOptionsAnsweredAt`, `firstStartTime`, `changelogLastFetched`), the migration markers (`migrationVersion`, `hasResetAutoModeOptInForDefaultOffer`, the `*MigrationComplete` keys), the notices and onboarding flags (`hasCompletedOnboarding`, `hasSeenTasksHint`, `seenNotifications`, `hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesWarningShown`, `autoModeEnvSetup`), the per-project session statistics (`projects.*.last*`, `exampleFiles`), the account record (`oauthAccount`, `userID`: who is signed in, not what the agent may do), the remote-settings mode (`remoteHomeSettingsMode`, which governs remote sessions, not this one) and the display preferences (`theme`, `editorMode`, `verbose`). None of them is read where the harness decides what the agent may do.
Consequences:
- The settings files, `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude/remote-settings.json` and the workspace's `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`, hold settings only, so every key of them is checked (permissions, hooks, additional directories, MCP servers, `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`, `skipAutoPermissionPrompt` and `enableAllProjectMcpServers` among them). No capture shows the harness changing either home file at rest (**Verified** on the six runs of the 2026-10-17 `tools` capture of 2.1.296).
- agy: no file declares keys. `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` holds `model`, `permissions.allow`, `trustedWorkspaces` and `mcpServers`, `~/.gemini/config/config.json` a `userSettings` object and `mcp_config.json` MCP servers (**Verified**: their key paths on the development machine on 2026-10-17; the binary's struct tags name `trustedWorkspaces` among settings fields). None holds caches or counters, and no capture shows agy writing them at rest (I-59), so they stay checked whole. A capture of agy with a snapshot will show whether that holds.
- On the 2026-10-17 capture no change lies under an allowlisted key, in the task run or in a control (**Verified** with `cmd/verify`, V-41).
- The list is tied to 2.1.296. A version that reads a new key to decide a permission is report-only on that key until it is added; the list is re-checked against the binary when a version is added to the agent version configuration (I-57).
- `*` matches one key element, which may contain dots (a project directory), so patterns are split on dots and keys are not.
Alternatives rejected: allowlisting by top-level key only (`projects` would make every per-project statistic a finding, and the project entry is where the trust and the allowed tools live); a denylist of known caches (a new cache key in a later version would be a finding until listed; the allowlist fails toward report-only instead, with the change still shown); declaring keys for the settings files too (they hold settings only, so narrowing them only hides changes).

### I-61: agy 1.3.4 offers `multi_replace_file_content` under no configuration; the adapter keeps claiming it like `replace_file_content`
Status: Accepted, no code change (the mapping of I-50 and its degradation in `Parse` stay). Narrows O-16 for Gemini and proposes closing its Gemini part.
Date: 2026-10-17.
Context: On the paired `search` capture (2026-10-13) the custom agent listed `multi_replace_file_content`, the CLI did not declare it to the model and the call failed tool validation (O-16). The 1.3.4 binary still links the tool's converter and the enum `ReplaceToolVariant` (`UNSPECIFIED` 0, `REPLACEMENT_CHUNK` 1, `SEARCH_REPLACE` 2, `APPLY_PATCH` 3, `SINGLE_MULTI` 4, the field `tool_variant` of `ReplaceContentToolConfig`, itself `replace_content_tool_config` of `CodeToolConfig`), and the user's agy conversations of June 2026 declared the tool next to `replace_file_content`. The question was which setting, model or server flag makes the CLI offer it. The evidence, gathered without calling the model:
- The 1.3.4 binary (`~/.local/bin/agy`, its Go function table and disassembly; **Reported**) has two tool registries. The one that builds the agent's tools (`core/tools.init`) registers `replace_file_content` with `NewSingleReplaceFileContentToolConverter` and registers `multi_replace_file_content` as `deprecatedEmptyTool`, whose function logs `Tool %q is deprecated and has no effect`. `NewMultiReplaceFileContentToolConverter` is referenced only from the map of `sharedfragment.registerDefaultToolConverters`, which converts a model's call into a step and does not declare tools. Nothing calls the converter constructors directly, and no code path from the variant to the multi converter was found.
- The CLI's own log confirms it: each of the six runs of the `search` capture logged `registry.go:28] Tool "multi_replace_file_content" is deprecated and has no effect` when it loaded the custom agent (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/log/cli-20261013_2345*.log` and `cli-20261013_2348*.log`, language server 1.3.4; **Verified**).
- The user's 67 agy conversations (read from copies, I-51), all of the same trajectory source, separate model, variant and version. The four of 2026-06-25 (`gemini-pro-default` and `gemini-3-flash-a`) carry `tool_variant` 4 and declare both tools, the multi tool with `ReplacementChunks` and `replace_file_content` with one chunk. The 39 of 2026-09-07 to 2026-09-15 still carry `tool_variant` 4, with `gemini-pro-default`, `gemini-3.1-pro-low`, `claude-sonnet-4-6` and `claude-opus-4-6-thinking`, and declare only `replace_file_content`, in a schema whose keys are sorted as the newer registry writes them. The 24 of October (agy 1.2.6 and 1.3.4, `gemini-3.8-flash-n`; 23 hold generation metadata) leave `tool_variant` unset and never declare it (**Verified** on those conversations; design input only). The same model with the same variant lost the tool between June and September, so neither selects it; a version did.
- `agy --help` offers no flag that selects tools; the agent's tools come from `--agent`, and naming the tool there is what produced the warning above. The CLI's settings file holds `model`, `permissions.allow`, `trustedWorkspaces` and `mcpServers` (I-60). A server-sent tool configuration cannot bring the tool back: the registry entry has no effect whatever the variant.
Decision: No reachable configuration of agy 1.3.4 offers `multi_replace_file_content`, so no capture can measure its shape on this version. The adapter keeps claiming a call of it like `replace_file_content` and keeps reporting the degradation that its shape is unmeasured (I-50); a 1.3.4 conversation cannot hold a completed call of it, so the mapping matters only for conversations of older versions, which are not configured (I-57; agy's database states no version, so `--agent-version` names it). The capture script's `search` task is unchanged: its `multi_replace_file_content` step fails on 1.3.4 as it did on 2026-10-13, and the task stays as its fixture `paired-agy-1.3.4-search` was recorded. The Gemini part of O-16 is proposed for closing on this ground; until the integrator closes it, O-16 stays open for it with no capture pending.
Consequences: The I-50 shapes cover every file-editing tool agy 1.3.4 can call. If a later agy version declares the tool again, the binary check above is repeated when the version is added to the agent version configuration (I-57), and a paired capture with a `multi_replace_file_content` step measures its shape then. Which version removed the tool between June and September is not known: no CLI log of that period survives.
Alternatives rejected: Extending the `search` task with a flag, model or setting that selects the tool (none exists in 1.3.4); dropping the mapping (a conversation of an older version would then lose a write claim, and a missing claim fails toward Unrecorded events, not toward silence, but nothing is gained by it).

### I-62: Only the assistant's records state Claude Code calls and only the user's records their results; a repeated id is a parse error
Status: Accepted, implemented (`claimBlock` and the repeat check in `Parse`, `claudecode/claudecode.go`; the copies and `check_copies` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `TestOnlyAssistantAndUserRecordsStateCalls`, `TestARepeatedCallIsAParseErrorNotASecondClaim`).
Date: 2026-10-24.
Context: A paired capture of the `tools` task on Claude Code 2.1.306 (2026-10-24, five controls) verified with 12 findings against its own baseline: five Mismatched, two Unwitnessed writes of `notes.txt` stamped 0001-01-01 and five outside their interval, although the probe saw the same shapes for Read, Edit, Write and NotebookEdit as on the named capture of 2.1.296 (I-56). The transcript of 2.1.306 holds three record types no earlier capture has: `api-request`, `api-request-shape` and `api-request-blob` (**Verified**: the record types of the six raw Claude Code captures kept from 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-24, five of 2.1.296 and this one; `atis-latch`, `cost-state`, `last-prompt`, `queue-operation` and `attachment` are in the 2.1.296 captures too). An `api-request-blob` (fields `type`, `sessionId`, `hash`, `message`, and `agentId` in a subagent's transcript) copies an API request: its message content repeats earlier `tool_use` and `tool_result` blocks under the same ids, among text blocks, with no timestamp and no `toolUseResult`, and the copied call's input may lack a field the original has (an Edit's `replace_all` when false). In the task run two of its 15 such records repeat the three Reads of one message, and two more the two Edits and a `ToolSearch` of another. `Parse` read calls and results from any record with message content, so it claimed those six calls twice, the copies at the zero time, and the copies' results, read later, replaced the real ones: the Reads and Edits lost their end and their content. The other new types hold no message content.
Decision: `Parse` takes `tool_use` blocks only from records of type `assistant` and `tool_result` blocks only from records of type `user`, the only record types that carry them in the five raw captures of 2.1.296, and the only ones the 2.1.306 capture states them in first (**Verified**). A record of any other type is skipped without a report, as before, whatever its content. A `tool_use` id or a `tool_result`'s `tool_use_id` seen a second time among those records, in the main transcript or a subagent's, is a parse error that names the file and line; the first stays the one claimed and the repeat is dropped, so no transcript can double a claim or replace a result by restating it. No transcript of the six raw captures repeats an id within those records (**Verified**). `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` keeps `assistant` and `user` records as before, cuts a record of another type that repeats calls or results to its type, the zero session id and those blocks, with every result's text elided, so a fixture shows the adapter the repeats it must not claim, and leaves out and counts every other record (an `api-request-blob` copies a whole API request, system prompt included).
