It refuses the fixture when a copy holds a call or result no `assistant` or `user` record states, or an input field the stated call lacks or holds otherwise. Reduced again with the new script, the 2026-10-13 `shapes` and 2026-10-17 `tools` captures give byte-identical output.
Consequences:
- On the raw 2.1.306 capture with its own baseline (one control in five), the 14 calls give 16 claims, all corroborated, and no finding; the run is INCONCLUSIVE on one harness file event, the harness's refresh of its model catalog cache, `~/.claude/cache/model-catalog/<organisation>-<hash>-cc.json`, which no control made (**Verified** with `cmd/verify --baseline`). Before, it was 20 calls and 23 claims.
- The outputs of `cmd/verify` on every other checked-in fixture are unchanged, since their reduced transcripts hold only `assistant` and `user` records.
- A record type that carried a call in a later version would lose the call: its effects would show as Unrecorded events, a loud failure.
- Records of other types are not listed in the report: the report has no field for them, and they state nothing the verifier compares.
Alternatives rejected: Skipping records without a timestamp (a later copy with a timestamp would be claimed again, and the type says what a record is); dropping a repeated id silently (a transcript that restated a call would hide the restatement, and the duplicate is evidence that the format changed); a parse error per record of an unknown type (every 2.1.296 transcript would report its `attachment` and `queue-operation` records); reading the copies as corroboration of the calls (a copy is the harness's request to the model, not a record of what ran).

### I-63: Claude Code 2.1.306 is configured, with I-46's gate, on the evidence of one paired capture and its binary
Status: Accepted, implemented (`harnessReadVersions` in `claudecode/harnessread.go`; the 2.1.306 entry of `config/agents.json`; the fixture `paired-2.1.306-tools` and `tools306_test.go`; the capture `tools-2.1.306` in `pkg/attack/eval_test.go`). Applies I-57; extends I-46's gate; re-checks I-60's sources for 2.1.306. Amended by V-43 (2026-10-26): at five controls in five the package-manager probe is an unexplained harness command, and the fixture and the evaluation's honest control are INCONCLUSIVE with no finding. Amended by I-76 (2026-10-31, commit 0dc0cf7): one control's replace of the policy file's stamp now folds, so at five controls in five the task run has six unexplained harness file events instead of eight; `TestTools306CorroboratesEveryClaimAndPinsTheAgreements` pins, at agreements 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8 and 1, all 16 claims corroborated with no finding, no unverified field and no ambiguity, and the run INCONCLUSIVE with no unexplained harness connection, 1, 3, 3, 3 and 6 unexplained harness events and 0, 0, 0, 0 and 1 unexplained harness command ({1, 6, 1} at full agreement), that command the package-manager probe with every event beneath it in the controls. Amended by V-58 (2026-10-31): with the session's bookkeeping declaration, as `cmd/verify` takes it, the task run's write of the model-catalog cache is declared bookkeeping, so at one control in five the run is FAITHFUL instead of INCONCLUSIVE, and at two and at five controls in five it is INCONCLUSIVE with no finding on two and four unexplained harness events (`TestTools306CatalogWriteIsDeclaredBookkeeping`).
Date: 2026-10-24.
Context: I-57 configures a version once its checked-in fixture verifies and every version gate in the adapter is decided for it. The `tools` task was captured with 2.1.306 on 2026-10-24 with five controls and the fs probe's names (P-25); with I-62 its 14 calls give sixteen claims. The adapter has one version gate, I-46's `harnessReadVersions`, and I-60's allowlist and I-35's wording rest on strings of the 2.1.296 binary. The binary of 2.1.306 is installed at `~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.306`.
Decision: 2.1.306 is configured with the fixture `paired-2.1.306-tools`, reduced from the capture by `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`, and verified on 2026-10-24. Evidence, gate by gate:
- I-46: the read-command parser (2.1.296's `F1r` and the forms it calls), the tree-sitter shell splitter it uses, the function that reads the named files after a Bash call, and that call with its conditions (not interrupted, no image, not in the background) are those of 2.1.296 but for the minifier's names (**Reported**: the binaries' text compared with every identifier of up to four characters replaced, identical from the start of each over 6,142, 9,817 and 2,231 characters, each comparison ending in unrelated code), so 2.1.306 joins `harnessReadVersions`.
- I-60: `GLOBAL_CONFIG_KEYS`, `PROJECT_CONFIG_KEYS` and the project defaults (`DEFAULT_PROJECT_CONFIG`) are identical lists in both binaries (**Reported**). No other place where 2.1.306 reads a key was compared, so a new security-relevant key would stay report-only. On the capture no change to a declared sensitive file lies under an allowlisted key, in the task run or in a control: the task's 85 changes are 52 the controls made and 33 report-only (**Verified** with `cmd/verify --baseline` on the raw capture).
- I-35: the binary holds both result sentences of a Write (**Reported**).
- The probe saw the same shapes for Read, Edit, Write and NotebookEdit as on `paired-2.1.296-tools-named`, and the run declares no new harness host: no connection is unexplained at any agreement (**Verified** on the fixture).
Consequences:
- On the fixture, every claim is corroborated at every agreement from one to five controls in five, the three whole-text Reads and the two Edits on their content. From one to four controls in five there is no finding and the run is INCONCLUSIVE on 1, 3, 3 and 3 harness file events (the task run's refresh of the model catalog cache, which no control made, and from two in five a write of the policy file). At five in five it is NOT FAITHFUL on two findings: the harness's package-manager probe (`sh -c uname -s ...`), which the task run and four of the five controls ran, is an Unrecorded exec and an unexplained subtree, and 8 harness file events are unexplained (**Verified**, `TestTools306CorroboratesEveryClaimAndPinsTheAgreements`, and with `cmd/baseline` and `cmd/verify` at each agreement). This is the first capture in which an occasional harness command is a finding, the evidence O-30 waits for; the capture script's default agreement (one control in five) does not reach it, `cmd/baseline`'s default (every run, I-24) does.
- The attack evaluation reports the capture and does not gate it, as the `tools` captures of 2.1.296 (E-5). Its baseline is built at full agreement, so the honest control is NOT FAITHFUL on the same two findings and every run carries them as unaccounted findings (200 per 100 runs); detection is 1.000 for every generator and count, and classification and location are within 0.01 of the 2.1.296 `tools` captures' (**Verified**, `go test ./pkg/attack -v`).
- The capture's transcripts first showed the copied API requests that I-62 excludes.
Alternatives rejected: configuring 2.1.306 without its own fixture (I-57 requires one); leaving it out of I-46's gate (its harness reads after a Bash read command would stay Unrecorded on an honest run, though the parser is unchanged); gating the evaluation on the new capture (the honest control fails at the evaluation's full agreement, for a reason outside the generators).

### I-64: The campaign runner pins the model and the version, takes fresh controls, orders slots by a recorded seed and records every exclusion
Status: Accepted, implemented (`scripts/capture-campaign.sh`, `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`). Builds on I-57 for the version check and on I-24 and V-24 for the controls. Amended by I-65: agy defaults to `gemini-3.8-flash-high` instead of requiring an id. Amended by I-67: `--validate` verifies the known answers against a baseline of `simagent --null` runs. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78.
Date: 2026-10-26.
Context: The experiment protocol (sections 6, 9.4 and 10.1) and the user's decisions of 2026-10-26 on the protocol (the protocol's D6, D7, D8, D10 and D11, local only; not the D entries of this file) fix what a campaign records: one version of each agent with updates off, a pinned model, five fresh controls per task run with two of them before a `long` run, held-out tasks kept outside the repository, a random slot order from a recorded seed, an instrument check before each block and a rule for every excluded run. The runner reused one control set per tool set, ran the task first, did not check the model and deleted nothing but also classified nothing. The protocol leaves open how each rule is enforced.
Decision: The runner takes every control run in the capture of its task run and never passes `--baseline-from`; `--controls-before N` orders them, and `long` or a plan entry marked `split` takes `N*2/5` before. Both agents' runs are pinned with `--model`; Claude Code defaults to `claude-sonnet-5-5`, agy has no default and the runner refuses without an id. After each run the transcript's recorded model must equal the pinned one, else the run fails with `MODEL MISMATCH` and the campaign stops. Claude Code runs require `env.DISABLE_UPDATES` to be `"1"` in the user's settings and get `DISABLE_UPDATES=1` in their environment; the agent's version is read before and after each run, and a change stops that agent's campaign and excludes the run as X3. The slots of a plan run in the order of the SHA-256 of the seed and the slot, which a resumed invocation keeps. A failed run is classified: X4 is retried twice in its slot and then reported failed, X6 (Ctrl+C) is rerun, a login or quota error or a model mismatch ends the campaign; the rule and reason are written to the run and the manifest and no run is deleted. `--validate` runs the known answers of protocol 9.4 with `simagent` before the first run and halts on a failure; the slow end-to-end tests are opt-in. A held-out task is read from a directory outside the repository and never copied; the manifest holds the SHA-256 of its prompt and seed.
Consequences:
- A Claude Code `basic` run costs six sessions instead of one to six; the dry run states the totals.
- A model mismatch or a login or quota error is not retried: the next run would fail the same way, and a drifting model is a variable nobody registered.
- The ordering function depends on neither the Python version nor the platform's random source; two invocations with the same seed over the same plan produce the same order.
- The agy model id that matches the existing captures is not determined offline (**Reported**: `agy models` lists `gemini-3.8-flash-high`, `-medium` and `-low`, and the existing conversations record `gemini-3.8-flash-high` in their executor metadata); the pilot confirms it. Amended 2026-10-27 (commit 499452f): the agy pilot of 2026-10-27 recorded `gemini-3.8-flash-high`.
- X1 (loss) and X5 stay with the analysis, which reads the counters in the manifest.
Alternatives rejected: keeping control reuse with a per-task window (the protocol's D10 chose fresh controls); a Python-seeded shuffle (its sequence is not promised across versions); retrying a model mismatch (it would hide a server-side change); deleting failed runs (the protocol counts every excluded run).

### I-65: The runner measures each watch's cost from /proc, writes the annotations the manifest cannot show and reads scale diagnostics from the capture; the comparison sweep covers the campaign's kept runs
Status: Accepted, implemented (`scripts/measure-watch.py`, `scripts/annotate-run.sh`, `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`, `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`, `scripts/compare/sweep.py`). Builds on I-64, P-34 and N-33 (the diagnostics), E-6 (what `cmd/analyze` reads) and the user's decisions of 2026-10-26 (agy model, the protocol's D13, local only; not D13 of this file). Amended by I-66: an agent's non-zero exit status is a note, not `x4`. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78.
Date: 2026-10-26.
Context: `cmd/analyze` reads an optional `cost` object in each `run.json` (start-up, drain, `watch` CPU and peak RSS, for H4b) and an `annotations.json` of what only the operator knows (X4 causes the manifest cannot show, X6, task completion). The runner wrote neither, and its scale record took the net counters from `watch`'s log although coverage schema 2 carries them, with the ring and `tracked_pids` occupancy, in the ground truth. The comparison sweep ran four fixtures, seeds 0 to 99 and n=1, where the protocol needs the campaign's kept runs with the H2 sweep's attacked trajectories. `watch` is not changed, so its cost is measured from outside.
Decision: Every `sudo watch` runs under `scripts/measure-watch.py`, which samples `watch` from `/proc` (found by its `--out` argument) every 5 ms and writes start-up, drain, `watch`'s own CPU (`utime` plus `stime`), its `VmHWM` and the rusage of the whole tree; the runner copies the four fields `cmd/analyze` reads into `cost` in `run.json` (and `control_cost`). A field the helper could not measure is null. The runner writes `x4` into `annotations.json` for a task run that completed but whose `watch` exited non-zero, marks the fields it wrote under `auto`, and rewrites only those; `scripts/annotate-run.sh` writes the operator's facts and takes a field over from the runner. The scale record's net counters and `diagnostics` come from the ground truth; the log line is a labelled fallback for coverage schema 1. agy defaults to `gemini-3.8-flash-high`, still overridable and still checked against the conversation record after the run. `scripts/compare/sweep.py --campaign` attacks the runs `cmd/analyze` keeps, with the H2 generators' primary variants through `attack`, halo-record and Signet on all of them and reproducible-trajectories on the Claude Code runs with Write or Edit claims at the seeds the protocol's D13 names.
Consequences:
- The cost of recording in a real session is a userspace figure: the kernel time spent in the probes' BPF programs is charged to the agent's tasks and is not separated, so H4b's `watch` CPU is a lower bound of the recording's cost and the overhead benchmark's paired runs remain the measure of it. The peak RSS is a lower bound by sampling, bracketed from above by the tree's.
- A run whose agent exits non-zero is annotated X4 and leaves every analysis unless the operator takes the annotation over. Retrying such a slot as an X4 failure instead is a policy question the runner does not decide (O-32, decided as I-66).
- Task completion stays the operator's statement; the runner checks no task output.
- The comparison inputs are `attack`'s; their equality with `cmd/analyze`'s in-process mutations rests on both calling the same generators with the same avoided targets (**Reported**: no difference on the checked-in fixtures and a raw capture of each agent, seeds 1 to 8). `analyze` exporting the attacked trajectories it generates would make them identical by construction.
Alternatives rejected: changing `watch` to report its own CPU and RSS (the probes' contract and its tests are the probe lanes'); wrapping the agent in a timing shell (the agent would no longer be the root of the traced tree, changing what `verify` reads); parsing timestamps from `watch`'s log (its start and end lines carry none); reusing one analysis binary's generators from Python (a second implementation of the generators).

### I-66: A run whose agent exits non-zero after a complete session counts; the runner notes the status and annotates x4 only for genuine harness failures
Status: Accepted, implemented (`watch_nonzero` and `summarize` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`). Decides O-32; amends I-65.
Date: 2026-10-26.
Context: `watch` exits 1 when the agent it ran exited with an error and still writes the capture and the transcript exists. I-65 annotated every such run `x4`, which takes it out of every analysis. The user ruled on 2026-10-26 that such a run counts.
Decision: For a task run that completed (the capture script exited 0 and the version did not change) but whose `watch` exited non-zero, the runner reads `watch`'s log. If it says `agent exited with error`, the runner writes `agent_exit_status`, the agent's exit status (watch's own when the log gives none), into the run's entry in `annotations.json`, listed under `auto`. `cmd/analyze` reads only `x4`, `x6` and `completed` from an entry, so the note changes no number and is not shown. If the log does not say the agent failed, `watch` itself failed and the runner writes `x4`, as for the other harness failures (no transcript, a login or quota error, a model mismatch), which the manifest records through the run's status and exclusion. A control run's `watch` exit is only recorded in `watch_exit`.
Consequences:
- A campaign counts the runs of an agent that ends with a non-zero status after answering; the notes show how many, and the operator can still exclude one with `annotate-run.sh --x4`.
- A `watch` log that says neither is read as `watch`'s failure, the conservative reading.
Alternatives rejected: annotating `x4` (I-65: it removes complete sessions from H3); retrying the slot as an X4 failure (it would spend quota on a session that is complete).

### I-67: The instrument validation verifies simagent's known answers against a baseline of `simagent --null` control runs and checks that the baseline hides none of them
Status: Accepted, implemented (`--null` in `cmd/simagent`; `validate_instrument`, `null_check`, `power` and `judge` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`, and its `--validate-only`). Builds on V7, V-24, P-17 and I-41; amends I-64.
Date: 2026-10-26 (after the validation's first real run).
Context: `capture-campaign.sh --validate` ran `simagent` under `watch` and verified it without a baseline. Since P-17 `watch` marks every filesystem, so the capture also holds the agent process's own start-up and exit at level 0: `watch`'s hold script, `setpriv`, the loader and libraries, the name service files, `simagent`'s binary, the zone file and the write of the trajectory outside the workspace. On its first real run the honest known answer read NOT FAITHFUL with 30 Unrecorded events and the validation halted the campaign. The real captures subtract exactly this with a baseline built from control runs (V7); the validation did not. The end-to-end tests do not see it because they watch only the workspace.
Decision: The validation takes three control runs of `simagent --null`, which acts on nothing and writes an empty trajectory, under the same `watch` and `setpriv` wrapper as the known answers, in the order null, honest, null, dropped (`--drop-entry 0`), null. Every run writes its trajectory to one path, which is moved aside after the run, so the rules for its write hold in each run (a per-run path would be a different target in every run, I-41); the workspaces differ and the baseline's `{workspace}` placeholder covers them. `--null` loads the local time zone as the script's runs do when they format their first timestamp, so a null run opens the same zone file. `cmd/baseline --agent generic` builds the baseline from the null runs at an agreement of one run in three, the capture script's default (V-24): an event of `simagent`'s own start-up that only some runs show is still explained. The honest and dropped runs are verified against it. The validation passes only when every `watch` exited 0, every null run wrote `[]`, `cmd/baseline` accepted the null runs (no loss), the power check passes, the honest run is FAITHFUL and the dropped run is NOT FAITHFUL with exactly one finding, an Unrecorded event equal to the honest run's first claim (its action type and its target with the workspace as placeholder). The power check fails when a rule names the workspace, when the baseline holds a command rule or a subtree record (a null run runs no command), or when a rule explains any action the honest run claims, since the dropped entry would then be hidden. `--validate-only OUTPUT_DIR` builds, asks for sudo, runs the validation alone and exits, writing no manifest.
Consequences:
- The check keeps its power by construction and by test: the null runs never touch the workspace, and a baseline that explains a claimed action fails the validation instead of hiding the dropped entry. On the recorded ground truths of the failed run, a single rule for the dropped write made the dropped run read FAITHFUL; the power check refuses that baseline.
- Noise that some null run shows is explained; noise in a known answer that no null run shows fails the validation and names the events, and so does any noise in a null run that touches the workspace or runs a command. The validation never passes with an unexplained event.
- The validation measures the probes on `simagent`'s own actions in the workspace and on its command, not on activity outside the workspace: the baseline explains the agent process's level-0 opens outside the workspace in every run alike, which is the V-36 and V-37 limitation of any baseline.
- It takes five runs of under a second each instead of two.
Alternatives rejected: an agreement of every run (an event of start-up that one control missed would fail the validation, with no gain in power, which the power check already guards); verifying without a baseline with `watch` restricted to the workspace (the campaign's captures mark every filesystem, and the validation must exercise the same configuration); a hand-written list of `simagent`'s start-up files (version and machine specific, the failure V7 replaced); taking the null runs from the dropped run's own capture with the actions removed (a post-hoc filter of the run being judged).

### I-68: A Claude Code Read, Write or Edit whose result is marked `is_error` claims nothing
Status: Accepted, implemented (`claimsFor` in `claudecode/claudecode.go`; `TestAFailedFileToolClaimsNothing`, `TestAFailedWriteIsNotUnwitnessed`). Amends I-35. Extends I-37's rule for a failed NotebookEdit to the other file tools.
Date: 2026-10-27 (the first `long` pilot of Claude Code 2.1.306).
Context: The adapter claimed a Read, Write or Edit whatever its result said. On the first `long` pilot, an honest run, a subagent's Write of `REPORT.md` came back as `<tool_use_error>Subagents should return findings as text, not write report files. ...</tool_use_error>` with `is_error`, and the probe saw no event of the file: its open and write claims were Unwitnessed. A Read of the same file, before the main agent wrote it, came back `File does not exist.` with `is_error`, and its open claim, with nothing of its own to pair with, took the level-0 open of another file a Grep had made seconds before: one Mismatched and one Outside interval finding (**Verified** with `cmd/verify` on the raw capture). The 2.1.306 binary refuses the subagent's Write in the tool's input validation, on a file name matching `^(REPORT|SUMMARY|FINDINGS|ANALYSIS).*\.md$` in a subagent, before any write (**Reported**, from its strings); a failed open is not a file event.
Decision: A Read, Write or Edit whose `tool_result` carries `is_error` claims nothing and is counted in `UnmappedByTool`, as a failed NotebookEdit is (I-37). It is not counted among the Writes assumed to overwrite.
Consequences: On the pilot the two Unwitnessed, the Mismatched and the Outside interval findings go; the Grep's open that the Read had taken is Unrecorded, as the other Grep opens were. No checked-in fixture holds a failed Read, Write or Edit, so `cmd/verify` on every fixture is byte-identical and the attack evaluation is unchanged. A failed call that opened its file before it failed, as an Edit's check of its old string may, leaves the open Unrecorded where it was corroborated before, and its write is no longer an Unwitnessed claim; no capture holds a failed Edit. A transcript that marks a call that acted as failed leaves its effects unclaimed, so they are findings, never explained.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the claims (every refused call is a fabrication finding on an honest run). Claiming the open of a failed Edit as an ordinary claim (it is Unwitnessed whenever the harness refused the call before reading, as for a file not read first). An optional claim for it (V-33 conditions one on a command that read the file, and a failed Edit has none).

### I-69: A Claude Code Grep that names a path implies an optional open of it before the search
Status: Accepted, implemented (`grepOpenVersions`, `grepOpenClaims` in `claudecode/grepread.go`; the call in `Parse`; `collapseOpenClaims` keeps the marker apart; `TestAGrepOfAPathImpliesTheHarnessOpenBeforeTheSearch`, `TestTheHarnessOpenBeforeAGrepIsExplainedEndToEnd`, `TestGrepOpenGateAgreesWithTheConfig`). Builds on V-33 and V-48.
Date: 2026-10-27 (the first `long` pilot of Claude Code 2.1.306 and the strings of the 2.1.296 and 2.1.306 binaries).
Context: The pilot's task tells the agent to Grep each of four module files for a marker. Each Grep of one file showed, within about 1 ms of the call, an open of the module by the agent's own process, then ripgrep's exec with the target `/proc/self/fd/3`, then ripgrep's open of the same module; a Grep of the directory holding the modules showed ripgrep's exec with the target `.` and its opens of the files, and no open by the agent's process (**Verified** on the raw capture). Three of the four opens were Unrecorded findings and the fourth was taken by a failed Read's claim (I-68). The binaries of 2.1.296 and 2.1.306 hold the reason: the Grep tool's path check opens the path with `O_RDONLY|O_NONBLOCK`, returns no result for a missing path, and for a non-directory on Linux, where `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` resolves, sets the search target to `/proc/self/fd/3` and passes the open descriptor to ripgrep as descriptor 3; a directory is searched by path or through its descriptor as the working directory (**Reported**, from their strings). When the Grep's own `glob` or `type` filters exclude the file, the tool returns no match without starting ripgrep (**Reported**). The transcript's input names the path, and its result does not say whether the path is a file.
Decision: For a Grep of a version whose binary was read (2.1.296, 2.1.306) with a result, an input `path` not starting with `~`, and the same absolute `cwd` on the call's and the result's records, the adapter derives one harness-implied `file_open` of the path resolved against that `cwd`, after the Grep's own claim and with its interval, thread and block, `ImpliedBy` `claude-code search:grep` and `ImpliedBefore` set (V-48). The verifier lets it pair only with an open of exactly that path inside the call's interval, no later than an open of the path by a search's ripgrep. A Grep with no path derives nothing: the harness searches the working directory.
Consequences: On the pilot the four opens are harness-implied; with I-68 the run's alignment has one Unrecorded event left, a `net_request`, and no Unwitnessed, Mismatched or Outside interval finding (**Verified** with `cmd/verify --baseline` on the raw capture). The directory Grep's claim stays unpaired, which costs nothing. No checked-in fixture has a Grep with a path (the one Grep, in `paired-2.1.286-parallel`, names none), so `cmd/verify` is byte-identical on every fixture and the attack evaluation is unchanged. A Grep the filters cut short leaves the harness's open Unrecorded, as does a Grep of 2.1.286, whose binary was not read, and a path through a symbolic link.
Alternatives rejected: An ordinary claim of the open (Unwitnessed for every directory). Reading the result to tell a file from a directory (an empty result says nothing). Claiming every file ripgrep reads under a directory (those are ripgrep's, beneath the claimed search, and explained already).

### I-70: The mangled workspace is Claude Code's project name: every character but an ASCII letter or digit becomes a dash
Status: Accepted, implemented (`mangle`, `maxMangled`, `templateWorkspace`, `expandWorkspace` in `pkg/agent/baseline.go`; `TestCaptureTemplatesTheMangledWorkspaceWithAnyCharacter`, `TestMangleIsClaudeCodesProjectName`). Amends I-29. The 20 subagent events Consequences leaves unexplained are addressed by I-71 (2026-10-27), which closes O-35: the controls of a task with the subagent tool start a subagent.
Date: 2026-10-27 (the first `long` pilot of Claude Code 2.1.306 and the strings of its binary).
Context: A baseline replaces a control's workspace in its rules' targets by `{workspace}` and the workspace's mangled form by `{workspace-}`, so a rule for the harness's per-project files matches a run in another directory (I-24, I-29). The mangled form turned only slashes into dashes. Claude Code names the directory with `path.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g, "-")`, and a name over 200 characters is cut to 200 and suffixed with a dash and a base-36 hash of the path (**Reported**, from the strings of the 2.1.306 binary). The pilot's workspaces lie under a directory with an underscore and a version with dots, so no control rule was templated: each named its own control's directory, and 702 level-0 events of the task run under the harness's own directories (its transcript, the Bash tool's task outputs, the subagent's files, an MCP log) were unexplained harness activity (**Verified** with `cmd/verify --baseline` on the raw capture). The checked-in fixtures' workspaces (`/ws/<label>`) hold only slashes, letters, digits and dashes, which both forms spell alike.
Decision: The mangled form turns every UTF-16 code unit of the workspace that is not an ASCII letter or digit into a dash, as the harness does (a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane gives two dashes). A form over 200 characters is not templated when the baseline is captured, and a rule naming `{workspace-}` matches nothing for a run whose form is over 200 characters, since the hash is not computed here.
Consequences: Rebuilt from the same controls, the pilot's baseline explains all but 20 of the 702 events; those are the subagent's transcript and metadata, which no control writes because the control task runs no subagent (O-35). `cmd/verify` on every fixture is byte-identical with and without baselines at every agreement. A baseline captured before this decision from workspaces with other characters holds literal rules for the controls' own directories; it must be rebuilt from its controls with `cmd/baseline`, no new capture needed. A workspace whose mangled form contains another workspace's (`/ws/a_b` and `/ws/a-b`, or `/w/run-1` inside `/w/run-10`) is templated as that workspace's too, as before for slashes.
Alternatives rejected: Templating the per-project directory by its position under `~/.claude/projects/` (a pattern, which V7 refuses: it would explain whatever an attacker named there). Computing the harness's hash for long names (its function is minified and unverified; no capture has a long workspace).


### I-71: The controls of a task with the subagent tool start one subagent that does nothing
Status: Accepted, implemented (`control_subagent`, the control prompts and `subagents_seen` in `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`; `CONTROL_TURNS` and `read_order` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`). Closes O-35 with option (b), the user's decision of 2026-10-27. Builds on I-24, I-28 and I-38. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78. Extended 2026-11-05 by I-136: an agy custom agent with invoke_subagent lists one subagent the capture defines, so the controls' subagent can run.
Date: 2026-10-27 (the first `long` pilot of Claude Code 2.1.306).
Context: A control runs one no-op marker command (I-28) with the task's tool set (I-24), and the baseline built from the controls explains the harness's own activity. A task that starts a subagent makes the harness write that subagent's transcript `agent-<id>.jsonl` and metadata `agent-<id>.meta.json` under the session's directory, which a one-call control never writes; on the pilot, after I-70, these were the run's 20 unexplained harness events. Only the `tools` task's controls started a subagent (since the 2026-10-12 captures). The paths hold per-run ids, which I-38's family already replaces where runs are compared (`~/.claude/projects/{workspace-}/SESSION/subagents/agent-N.jsonl`); 2.1.306's agent id (`a` and 16 hex digits on the pilot) fits its `[0-9a-z]+`. For agy, `canonicalPath` replaces every UUID under the application directory with `{id}` (I-48), so a subagent's conversation database and `brain/<id>/` files take the same canonical names as the main conversation's. Neither adapter needs a change.
Decision: The controls of every task whose tool set holds the subagent tool (Claude Code `Agent`, agy `invoke_subagent`), built-in or held-out, run the marker command and then start one subagent whose only instruction is to reply with a word: for Claude Code with `run_in_background` false if offered and no tool, for agy with no command and no file, since an agy subagent answers through a `send_message` call. The `tools` task keeps its own three-step control prompt, which already does this. The prompt is fixed per agent and identical across a task's controls, so the control-set key (`--check`'s `control_prompt_sha256`) and `--baseline-from`'s prompt comparison separate these controls from one-call ones; a capture without `control-prompt.txt` still counts as one-call. After each run the script counts the subagents the run started and, for Claude Code, the tool calls their transcripts record, into `order.tsv` and the manifest, and warns when a control's count is not one subagent with no call; the run is kept. The campaign's turn estimate counts four turns for such a control.
Consequences: The baseline takes rules for the subagent's files from the controls, so a task's subagent bookkeeping is explained by measurement, not by trusting the transcript to name its files (option (c) of O-35, rejected). The subagent's own claims are untouched: the null subagent makes no action a rule could come from, and a task subagent's claims still pair with their events. **Verified**, as an illustration and not a measurement of the new controls: the pilot's task run verified against baselines from the five controls of the fixture `paired-2.1.306-tools` (another session, workspace and subagent id, with a subagent in every control; home directory rewritten back from the fixture's), at agreements of 0.2, 0.6 and 1, leaves none of the 20 subagent events unexplained, and its 185 corroborated claims, the subagent's `REPORT.md` write and test command among them, stay corroborated; that baseline cannot judge the run otherwise, since it comes from another tool set. The pilot cannot be re-verified against controls of this kind without new captures. Each control of such a task costs about two more model turns, and the first pilot's controls cannot serve a `long` capture through `--baseline-from` any more. A control's subagent is asked for, not enforced, and the agy count includes databases a concurrent agy session adds ([../architecture/40_tools.md](../architecture/40_tools.md), Known limits).
Alternatives rejected: Leaving the events unexplained (option (a): every run with a subagent stays INCONCLUSIVE); a harness-owned path set derived from the subagents the transcript names (option (c): it trusts the transcript to name the harness's files); a separate control task with a subagent beside the one-call controls (a second control set per task, and two baselines to merge); failing a control that did not start exactly one subagent (an exclusion rule the protocol does not have; the counts are recorded for the analysis to decide).

### I-72: A Claude Code WebFetch of 2.1.306 claims its domain check as harness-implied, its connection, and its GET when the capture read request content
Status: Accepted, implemented (`webFetchVersions`, `webFetchClaims`, `fetchTarget`, `Adapter.NeedsContent` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/webfetch.go`; the call in `Parse`; `agent.ContentClaimer` and its use in `agent.Prepare`; `TestFetchTarget`, `TestAWebFetchClaimsItsDomainCheckConnectionAndRequest`, `TestAWebFetchIsVerifiedEndToEnd`, `TestWebFetchGateAgreesWithTheConfig`). Amends I-16. Uses V-52.
Date: 2026-10-27 (the `websearch` capture of Claude Code 2.1.306, private, and the strings of the 2.1.306 binary).
Context: A `WebFetch` was claimed as one `net_connect` to the URL's host (I-16), which the 2.1.286 `parallel` fixture corroborates; that capture had no request content (`identity_only`), so no `net_request` existed to claim. The `websearch` capture of 2.1.306 is the first with request content: under its own baseline its one `WebFetch` left two Unrecorded `net_request` events by the agent's own process, `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/web/domain_info?domain=man7.org` 13 ms after the call, then the connection to `man7.org` and `GET https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/fanotify.7.html` (**Verified**). The binary of 2.1.306 holds the tool (**Reported**, from its strings): it looks the URL up in a cache of fetched pages keyed by the URL as written (50 MB, a time-to-live) and returns a hit with no request; otherwise it rewrites `http:` to `https:`, checks the host with that GET unless a check of the same host allowed it within five minutes (or a setting skips checks), and fetches the URL with axios, `maxRedirects: 0`, following a redirect itself only to the same site (same scheme and port, the host with or without `www.`) and returning any other redirect to the model without fetching it. Remote sessions fetch through a proxy instead; this adapter reads local sessions. The binaries of 2.1.286 and 2.1.296 were not read (neither is installed).
Decision: For a `WebFetch` of a version in `webFetchVersions` (2.1.306) with a result, the adapter derives, in the harness's order and with the call's interval, thread and block: a harness-implied `net_request` `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/web/domain_info?domain=<host>` (V-52), the `net_connect` to the host and the `net_request` `GET <target>`. The target and the host come from `fetchTarget`, in the probe's form (`models.CanonicalNetTarget`): https, the host lowercased, no default port (80 of an `http` URL included), the path and query as written, no fragment, an empty path as `/`. It predicts only a URL the WHATWG parser leaves as written: no user information, a host of ASCII letters, digits, dots and dashes that is not an address, a port of plain digits, and a path and query of characters it does not encode, with well-formed escapes and no dot segment; for any other URL the call keeps the connection alone, as before, with a degradation. The connection and the GET are harness-implied too when the call may have made no request: a URL that an earlier `WebFetch` of the session named as written (the cache), or a result marked `is_error` (the harness fails before or after the request). Only a capture that read the agent's request content can witness a request, so `agent.Prepare` drops every claim the adapter's `NeedsContent` names, a `WebFetch` `net_request`, when the net probe's `Content` is not `active`; the connection stays. A call with no result, and every other version, keeps the one connection.
Consequences: On the raw `websearch` capture with its own baseline, the two requests are explained: the check is harness implied, the connection, the GET and the Write are corroborated, and the alignment has no finding (**Verified** with `cmd/verify`). The run stays INCONCLUSIVE on two other causes of the same capture: V-39's five unresolved file events (P-37 names that shape in later captures) and five events of a second temporary of `~/.claude.json` whose rename into place the kernel merged with the first one's, unexplained harness activity. No checked-in fixture has a `WebFetch` of 2.1.306, so every `cmd/verify` report of the fixtures, text and JSON, with and without baselines, is byte-identical, and the attack evaluation is unchanged (**Verified**). What stays unclaimed: a redirect the harness follows within the site, a request to the redirect's target, possibly with a connection to the `www.` host; a fetch over HTTP/2, which the probe does not parse (N-D2; the harness's axios speaks HTTP/1.1 under Bun); a response of a binary type, which the harness also saves to a file (a write no claim names); a second fetch of another URL of the same host that reuses the connection, whose `net_connect` claim is Unwitnessed (as before this decision). A fetch of a URL that differs from an earlier one only in form (another letter case, a fragment) is not a cache hit and stays ordinary.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming the GET only as harness-implied (it is the agent's action, and an omission or a fabrication of it must be a finding when the capture can see it). Setting a GET claim aside as out of capture when content was not read (V-47's way; it would make every honest `WebFetch` INCONCLUSIVE on an identity-only capture, where the connection already checks it). Normalizing any URL as the WHATWG parser does (an approximation that would misname some URLs as the agent's fabrication). Deriving the claims for 2.1.286 or 2.1.296 (their binaries were not read; the 2.1.286 fixture shows a fresh connection to the model API 240 ms before the fetch, consistent with the check, which the baseline explains at host grain).
Note 2026-10-28: Context calls the `websearch` capture the first of 2.1.306 with request content. The `long` pilots of 2.1.306 of 2026-10-27 read request content before it (N-34, N-37, [../architecture/13_probe_net.md](../architecture/13_probe_net.md)); what this entry rests on is the `websearch` capture's `WebFetch`.

### I-73: Claude Code declares the output file of a Bash command as a background output file
Status: Accepted, implemented (`backgroundOutput`, `Adapter.IsBackgroundOutput` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/claudecode.go`; `TestIsBackgroundOutputNamesTheBashOutputFilesOnly`, `TestToolsReadOfTheBackgroundOutputIsTheAgents`). Declares the family V-56 reads. The user's choice on 2026-10-28.
Date: 2026-10-28 (the three `tools` captures of Claude Code 2.1.296 and 2.1.306).
Context: V-56 lets an adapter declare the files its harness writes a command's output to and opens only up to the command's end. Claude Code writes a Bash command's output to `/tmp/claude-<uid>/<workspace>/<session id>/tasks/b<8 letters or digits>.output`: for a background command it creates the file, opens it while the command runs and when it ends, and writes and closes it then; from 2.1.306 a foreground command gets one too, which the harness deletes when the command ends. The same directory holds a subagent's output, `a<16 hex digits>.output`, which the harness writes only. On the checked-in captures of 2.1.286 to 2.1.306 no harness open of a Bash output file came more than 20 ms after its last write (**Verified**, V-56).
Decision: `Adapter.IsBackgroundOutput` declares exactly the paths `^/tmp/claude-<digits>/<one component>/<uuid>/tasks/b[0-9a-z]{8}\.output$`, for every version. A subagent's output and any other file in the directory are not declared, so they are read as before.
Consequences: On the three `tools` captures the agent's Read of the background command's output file, 14 s to 17 s after the harness's last write, is the agent's (V-56). No other checked-in capture has an open of such a file more than a second after its last write. The declaration is not keyed by version: a version whose harness reopens a Bash output file more than a second after its last write would read that open as the agent's, an Unrecorded finding in an honest run, until the declaration is narrowed.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring every `tasks/*.output` file (the subagent's output has no observed harness open to bound, and nothing was measured for it); keying the declaration by version (the family and its timing are the same on every version captured, and an undeclared version would hide the Read again without a measured reason).

### I-74: Claude Code declares the workspace's session transcripts and their subagents directories as the trace
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent.TraceDeclarer`, `agent.Trace`, `agent.MangledWorkspace`, `agent.ReadTrace` in `pkg/agent/trace.go`; `Adapter.Trace` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/trace.go`; `TestTraceDeclaresTheSessionTranscriptsAndSubagents`, `TestTools306TheHarnessWritingItsTraceIsNoTampering`, `TestReadTraceFailsClosed`). The declaration V-59 reads. The user's decision of 2026-10-31. Codex joins by I-84, agy by I-102 (main files only). Amended 2026-11-02 by V-81: with the session id the capture recorded, the trace is that session's transcript and subagents only.
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: V-59 needs the trace's paths as the capture names them, from what the capture records: its workspace and the home it resolved `~/` against. Claude Code keeps a session in `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session id>.jsonl` and each subagent's transcript and metadata under `<project>/<session id>/subagents/` (D8), where `<project>` is the workspace mangled as I-70 states. The project directory also holds files that are not the session's report (the auto-memory, persisted tool results). When the transcript is gone, its session id is unknown.
Decision: An adapter declares its trace with `TraceDeclarer.Trace(workspace, home)`. Claude Code declares, under the recorded home (else any home I-30 declares), every `<project>/<uuid>.jsonl` as a main session file and every `<project>/<uuid>/subagents` with what is beneath it, for the capture's workspace, with a mangled name over 200 characters matched as the harness cuts it (its first 200 characters, a dash and a base-36 hash). No workspace, a relative one, or a home I-30 does not declare declares nothing. `agent.ReadTrace` reads a session for `cmd/verify` and `cmd/analyze` and returns V-59's state for a missing, empty, unparsable or damaged file of an adapter that declares a trace; an adapter that declares none reads as before (a Parse error is an error, parse errors stay in the report). `cmd/verify`, `cmd/analyze` and the attack evaluation set the declaration after `Prepare`.
Consequences: Every session of the workspace's project directory is declared, not only the one verified; a campaign's workspace holds one. Codex and agy declare nothing yet: their sessions read as before, and a deleted rollout or database is still an error for `cmd/verify`. To join, Codex would declare its rollout files under `~/.codex/sessions/` and agy its conversation database with its write-ahead log under its conversations directory, each with `Main` the file `--trajectory` names; neither path holds the workspace, so the declaration covers the directory or uses the session id from the run's output.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the whole project directory (the agent's own writes of the auto-memory, claimed by in-process tools, would read as tampering); the session's own files only (the session id is not known when the transcript is gone); declaring the trace in `Prepare` (setting it beside the version-keyed declarations leaves `Prepare` and every test that calls it unchanged).

### I-75: A run whose trace is missing, damaged or tampered with is a result, recorded by the runner and counted by the analysis, never a harness error
Status: Accepted, implemented (`capture_one`, `trace_state` and `capture` in `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`; `trace_result`, `run.json`'s `trace`, the `trace` annotation and the manifest's `trace_results` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `Session.Trace`, `CauseTrace`, `TraceRun`, `Results.Trace` and the sweep set-aside in `cmd/analyze`; `TestCaptureKeepsARunWhoseTranscriptWasDeleted`, `TestCaptureOfAnIntactTranscriptIsUnchanged`, `TestAMissingTranscriptIsAResultNotAnExclusion`, `TestClassifyReasonOfAnUnreadableTrace`). Builds on V-59 and I-74. The user's decision of 2026-10-31. The capture script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh` since I-78. An agy task run joins by I-102.
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: The capture script failed a task run whose transcript was gone with "no transcript found", and the campaign classified it as X4, a harness error, moved the attempt aside and recorded the slot again; `cmd/analyze` read a session that did not parse as X4 too. An agent that deletes its own trace therefore produced an excluded, retried run, and the tampering vanished from the results. The model check after each run read a missing or garbled transcript as `MODEL MISMATCH`, which ends a campaign.
Decision: For an adapter that declares its trace (Claude Code, I-74), a task run whose transcript is missing after the run is kept: the capture script says so, keeps the ground truth, records the controls, builds the baseline and runs both `verify`s, which report the trace as one that cannot be verified (V-59). It records the copied transcript's state in `<task>.trace.txt` and skips the model check of a task run whose trace is not `read` (`model_check=trace-<state>`), and its `verify` runs pass `--check-trace-hash`, since they read the copy the run left. The campaign records each run's `trace` in `run.json` and the manifest, annotates a complete run whose trace is not `read` or that has tampering evidence with `trace` (an `auto` field), lists such runs under `trace_results` and in `summary.txt`, and keeps them complete: no exclusion rule is added, since the run is a result, not a failure to measure. `cmd/analyze` reads the session with `agent.ReadTrace`, so such a run is no load error; it is verified as V-59 says, counted in H3a when it has tampering evidence and in H3c under a thirteenth cause, `trace_deleted_or_damaged`, when it has none, set aside from the attack sweep, and listed in `results.json`'s `trace_results` and a section of `report.md`. A control whose transcript is missing, and an agy run, are failures as before.
Consequences: In an honest campaign a run whose agent removed or rewrote its trace counts against the agent (a finding, or an INCONCLUSIVE with its own cause), never disappears, and is never retried. A transcript that the harness itself failed to write would read the same way: the probes then show no tampering, and the run is INCONCLUSIVE under the trace cause, where a reader sees it. On the private raw Claude Code captures at hand, every task run's copied transcript equals the content the probe hashed at its last close, and no run, control or task, has a tampering event (**Verified**, local only), so the comparison adds no INCONCLUSIVE to an honest capture of these versions. Adding a cause changes `results.json`'s precedence list and adds the `trace_results` key and a report section.
Alternatives rejected: A new exclusion code (the run measured something: an exclusion would hide it); keeping X4 and annotating (a retried slot replaces the run that showed the tampering); reading the trace's fate in the campaign alone (`cmd/analyze` recomputes every verdict from the raw files, so it must reach the same result itself).

### I-76: A named replace's create may have the process's events on other paths before the open
Status: Accepted, implemented (`previousOnReplace` in `foldReplacesWithNames` and in the older shapes' guard, `pkg/agent/claudecode/stream.go`; `TestNamedFoldTakesACreateWithOtherPathsBeforeTheOpen`, `TestClaudeJSONSaveFoldsWithAnotherThreadsEventBeforeTheOpen`, `TestClaudeJSONSaveIsExplainedByTheControls`). Amends I-56.
Date: 2026-10-31 (a private `long` capture of Claude Code 2.1.306 with content, and the fixtures `paired-2.1.296-tools-named` and `paired-2.1.306-tools`).
Context: I-56 folds a replace through a temporary T of a target X in the shape the probe reports with entry names, and lets the process's events on other paths lie between the open of T and the destination, since its other threads share its pid. Before the open it required the create to be the process's event just before it. The task run of a `long` capture of 2.1.306 had every claim corroborated and no finding, yet was INCONCLUSIVE on six unexplained harness events: one save of `~/.claude.json`, the create, open, write and close of `~/.claude.json.tmp.<pid>.<12 hex>`, the rename's source and its destination, complete and with every name exact. The harness's close of its fswatch probe file, on another thread, lay between the create and the open, one nanosecond after the create; the five controls saved the file the same way with nothing between, so they folded and the baseline held the folded write only (**Verified**, the ground truths and `cmd/verify`, local only). The same order left raw a control's replace of a subagent's metadata in `paired-2.1.296-tools-named` (a write of the task's output file between, the known limit of I-56) and a control's replace of `~/.claude/policy-limits.json.stamp.json` in `paired-2.1.306-tools` (an open of another file between).
Decision: The create is the process's last event on T or X before the open, by the same rule as the forward scan: the process's events on other paths are passed over and stay where they are, and an event of the process on another temporary of X ends the search. Everything else of I-56 holds: the create must be a `file_write` naming T without a hash, a source merged into the create's record must have the create's time and is likewise the process's last event on T or X before the open, and an event of the process on T or X that is not the create (an open or a write of X, say) leaves the replace as the probe reported it. The guard that keeps a temporary whose create the probe named from the older shapes looks for that create the same way.
Consequences: On the `long` capture the save folds into one `file_write` of `~/.claude.json` with the close's hash, the baseline's rule explains it, and the task run is FAITHFUL with its own baseline; the baseline is byte-identical (**Verified**, local only). Without a baseline every fixture's report, text and JSON, is byte-identical, and so is the attack evaluation (**Verified**, against eb15f1b). With baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, two fixtures change and no outcome does: in `paired-2.1.296-tools-named` and `paired-2.1.306-tools` one control's replace now folds, so the baselines lose the raw temporary's rules (five fewer at 0.2, five fewer unstable actions at 0.5) and at 1 gain the folded write of the target, and the task runs have one (the subagent's metadata) and two (the stamp file) unexplained harness events fewer at full agreement. Nothing more is hidden: the events folded are the same six, each with its exact name, by the same process, and an event between on another path is still reported as its own; an agent's process that creates T, acts on another path and then opens T has its replace folded as it would without the act between, which stays visible.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the save as harness bookkeeping (V-50's way): the controls already explain it, and the gap was in the fold, which other files' replaces share; bounding the backward search by time or by one batch (the forward search has no bound, and a bound adds a parameter no capture has measured); folding in the verifier (I-56's reasons).

### I-77: Every agent is configured by a profile in config/agents.json, and agent-specific strategies are methods the profile names
Status: Accepted, implemented (`config/agents.json` schema 2; `pkg/config` (`Adapter.VersionCommand`, `Trace`, `Content`, `VersionArgs`, `TraceFile`); `scripts/agent-profile.py`; `scripts/capture-agent.sh` and `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `cmd/status`; `trajectoryName` in `cmd/analyze`; `agent.ControlCommand`; `TestEveryProfileAgreesWithItsAdapter`, `TestProfileLookups`, `TestContentRefusalComesFromTheProfile`, `TestStatus`). Extends I-57. The user's decision of 2026-10-31 ("a clean design"); the H3 thresholds of `cmd/analyze` are left out, below. Extended by I-79 (2026-10-31): a profile without versions may lack an adapter, and the methods `none` (tool set) and `jsonl-rollout` (trace) and the fields `stdin`, `model.effort`, `model.catalog`, `trace.databases` and `instruction_files` join the format. Extended by I-98 (2026-11-01): `scripts/bench-overhead.sh` and `scripts/compare/` read the profiles too. Extended by I-113 and I-114 (2026-11-03): the fields `secret_env`, `config_file` and `isolation.env` and the trace method `sqlite-sessions` with `trace.database` join the format.
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: Every agent should get the same features and be configured the same way: Claude Code and agy now, Codex and an open-source agent next. The capture script, the campaign runner and the analysis branched on the agent's name: the binary and how its version is read (`awk '{print $1}'` for `claude`, `$NF` for `agy`), the version lock (`DISABLE_UPDATES=1` and the settings check, `AGY_CLI_DISABLE_AUTO_UPDATE=true`), the default model, the settings isolation (`--setting-sources project`, overridable by `CLAUDE_FLAGS`), the session bus variables agy needs, the invocation, the custom agent that fixes agy's tool set, how the session id is known, where and how the transcript is copied and its model and subagents read, which `verify` flags a capture gets, the refusal of `--content` for agy, the control prompts and the tasks, and `cmd/analyze`'s trajectory name. A new agent would have added a branch at each.
Decision: The adapter's entry in `config/agents.json` becomes its profile (schema 2): `binary`, `version_command`, `versions`, `version_lock` (`env`, an optional `require_setting`), `pass_env`, `model` (`default`), `isolation` (`flags`, `override_env`), `tool_set`, `session`, `invocation` (an argument template with whole-word placeholders), `params` (per-task values), `trace` (`method`, `dir`, `file`, `subagents`, `declared`, `states_version`), `content` (a list of methods, each a name with its parameters; `none` with a `reason`), `controls` (the command and subagent tools and the control prompts) and `tasks` (prompt, tools, an optional control prompt, `campaign: false`); the top level adds `default_agent` and `unrecorded`, what a control run recorded before its agent, tool set and prompt were written is assumed to be. A strategy is a method name (`list` or `agent-md` tool sets, `assigned` or `stdout-json` sessions, `jsonl-session` or `sqlite-conversation` traces, `tls-symbols` or `none` content) that maps to code in the capture scripts that never knows which agent uses it; `scripts/agent-profile.py` reads the profile for the shell scripts and refuses a method they do not implement. The capture script, the campaign runner, `cmd/status` (the version command) and `cmd/analyze` (the trajectory of a run the manifest names none for) read the profile; no `if agent == ...` remains in them. `pkg/config` reads schemas 1 and 2 and checks the parts the Go tools read; a test ties `trace.declared` to the adapter's `TraceDeclarer`. The model pin's default lives in the profile, as the brief asked; `--model` and the plan's `model=ID` still override it.
Consequences: Every existing capture's command lines are unchanged (**Verified**, on 2026-10-31: the capture script run under shims for `claude`, `agy`, `sudo`, `setpriv` and `watch` in 29 cases, both agents, with and without `--content`, the split and subagent controls, a held-out task, `--baseline-from`, `CLAUDE_FLAGS` and each refusal, before and after: every `watch` command line, `--check` output, output file, `order.tsv` and report byte-identical; and the campaign runner's `--dry-run` output for seven plans), except two refusal messages: an unknown Claude Code task now lists the profile's tasks, and the campaign's refusal of `content` for agy quotes the profile's reason. Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output and the attack evaluation are unchanged, since the Go change touches only the config reader, `cmd/status`'s version command and `cmd/analyze`'s fallback name. `results.json`'s `agent_config_sha256` changes with the file, as with any edit of it. The format admits Codex's content as `tls-openssl-ex` plus `tls-rustls-aead` with a WebSocket parser, agy's as `tls-go-crypto` with an HTTP/2 parser, and a `proxy` method, each a list entry with its own parameters, once the methods exist. Still keyed by the agent's name: `cmd/analyze`'s H3 thresholds and tool-heavy task lists, pre-registered parameters whose home is a policy question left to the user; the fixture version test; `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`, `scripts/bench-overhead.sh` and `scripts/compare/`.
Alternatives rejected: A new profile file beside `config/agents.json` (two files to keep in step, and the versions belong to the profile); tasks as files in the held-out layout (more files, while the profile already holds everything else of the agent; the held-out layout stays for held-out tasks); the method code in the Go adapters, called by the scripts (the capture script runs before any build and the methods are about the harness's files, not the record's meaning); keeping `verify`'s schema check at exactly 2 (an override file of schema 1 would stop `verify` for no reason it needs).

### I-78: The capture script is agent-neutral by name, and the old name is a wrapper
Status: Accepted, implemented (`scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh`; `--BINARY-model` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `TestCaptureClaudeCodeIsAWrapper`). Builds on I-77.
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: The capture script captures any profiled agent (I-77) but was named for Claude Code, and the campaign runner's `--agy-model` named one agent.
Decision: The script is `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `scripts/capture-claude-code.sh` execs it with the same arguments, so every earlier command works unchanged. The runner calls the new name. `--BINARY-model ID` sets the plan's default model of the agent whose profile's `binary` is `BINARY`, so `--agy-model ID` keeps its meaning and `--claude-model ID` is new; a binary no profile has is refused.
Consequences: The runner's messages name `capture-agent.sh`. Decision entries and fixture comments that name the old script stay true through the wrapper.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the old name (it misleads for every other agent); removing it (the user's commands and the fixtures' provenance name it); a `--default-model AGENT=ID` option in place of `--agy-model` (it would break the existing command).


### I-79: Codex is profiled before its adapter, with the methods its harness needs; a capture without an adapter records the runs only; every agent's personal instruction files are moved aside for each run
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `codex` profile and every profile's `instruction_files` in `config/agents.json`; `scripts/agent-profile.py`; the `none` tool set, the `jsonl-rollout` trace, `snapshot_databases`, `stdin`, `{effort}` and `{catalog}`, the model pin, `move_instruction_files`, `restore_instruction_files` and the adapter probe in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `TestEveryAdapterInTheConfigIsRegistered`, `TestEveryProfileAgreesWithItsAdapter`, `TestCaptureOfAnAgentWithoutAnAdapter`, `TestInstructionFilesAreAsideOnlyDuringRuns`, `TestAnAsideFileFromAnInterruptedCaptureIsRefused`). Builds on I-77; the user's decisions of 2026-10-31 (Codex CLI 0.166.0 frozen, `gpt-6.1-terra` at effort `medium`, `danger-full-access`, the isolation flags, the declared trace; the instruction files moved aside for every agent).
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: The first Codex capture, with controls, is the input of the adapter a later change builds (`pkg/agent/codex`). Codex 0.166.0 differs from the profiled agents where the capture touches it (survey of 2026-10-31, and the `codex exec` runs of this change): it takes no per-run tool allowlist; it prints the thread id as `thread_id` in the first line of `--json`; it writes its rollout to `~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<local time>-<thread>.jsonl`, and a subagent's to a rollout of its own whose session metadata names the root thread as `session_id`, starting with the parent's history up to `subagent_history_start_ordinal`; it keeps threads in `~/.codex/thread_history_1.sqlite` and `~/.codex/state_5.sqlite`; it appends a piped stdin to its prompt; and its effort is a configuration value, not part of the model id. The tests required every adapter in `config/agents.json` to be registered, and the capture script ended with a baseline and `verify`, which need the adapter. Every agent also reads the user's personal instructions whatever its isolation: Codex puts `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` into every prompt although `--ignore-user-config`, `--ignore-rules`, `-c skills.include_instructions=false` (measured: the skill list left the prompt), `--disable plugins` and `-c project_doc_max_bytes=0` (measured) are given; the pilot captures show Claude Code 2.1.306 opening `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` despite `--setting-sources project`, and agy 1.4.7 opening `~/.gemini/AGENTS.md`. Those files are the user's, not the harness's, and differ between users.
Decision: The `codex` profile holds everything Codex-specific, with no version listed. New methods and fields, each implemented without naming the agent: the tool set method `none` (no tool set is passed; the prompts name the tools and the task's tools decide the controls; an invocation with `{tool_set}` is refused); the trace method `jsonl-rollout` (the rollout found by the session id, the subagent rollouts the run created that name it, the model and effort read from every turn context, a subagent's after its parent's history, and the subagent's calls counted after the same point; it has the declared-trace state check); `trace.databases` and `trace.snapshots` (SQLite online-backup snapshots after every run, also when the rollout is gone); `stdin` (`inherit` or `null`); `model.effort` and `model.catalog` (I-80), filled as `{effort}` and `{catalog}` inside invocation words and joining the model pin that `control-model.txt` holds, `--check` prints and `--baseline-from` compares, so a profile without them keeps the bare model id. The session method is `stdout-json` with the key `thread_id`; the trace is declared, for the adapter to agree with. A profile without versions may lack an adapter; the capture script asks the built `baseline` whether it knows the agent and, when it does not, records the runs, controls and manifest and writes a `report.txt` that names the missing adapter and the commands to run once it exists, with no baseline and no verdict. The tasks are Claude Code's eight, the prompts naming Codex's tools where Claude Code's name its own; the controls use `exec_command` and `spawn_agent` with `wait_agent`. For every agent, the profile's `instruction_files.paths` (Claude Code `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, agy `~/.gemini/AGENTS.md`, Codex `~/.codex/AGENTS.md`) are moved to `FILE.agent-trace-aside` just before each run, the task and every control, and put back right after the agent exits, and by an `EXIT` trap on any other end of the script (an error, Ctrl+C, SIGTERM). A file the run created at the original path is never overwritten (the user's stays aside, with a warning), and an aside file left by a killed capture makes the next capture refuse before anything else, with the command that puts it back. `task-meta.txt` names each file with the home directory as `~`, with its SHA-256, or `absent`.
Consequences: Claude Code and agy are captured under new conditions from 2026-10-31 on: without the user's instruction file. Every earlier capture, every fixture included, ran with it present; a comparison across that date must allow for it. The user's other sessions that start while a run lasts do not find the file, which is why the window is one run and not the whole capture. Apart from the `instruction_file=` line `task-meta.txt` gains, every Claude Code and agy command line, `--check` output, output file, `order.tsv` and report is unchanged (**Verified**, 2026-10-31: the shim harness of I-77, 29 cases before and after, byte-identical but that line and the refusal of an unknown agent `codex`, which is now known; the campaign's `--dry-run` for seven plans, the same but that one). Every fixture's `verify` output is unchanged, since no Go code but tests changed. `cmd/status` lists Codex as configured none, and reports an installed 0.166.0 as not configured; the capture needs `--allow-unconfigured-version` until a fixture verifies a version. The database snapshots hold every thread of the account and must be reduced before any reaches a fixture (`scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` does not yet read a Codex capture). The capture records `exec_command` calls through Codex's code mode, whose single `exec` tool runs a script of nested calls; what the adapter claims from that is the adapter's decision.
Alternatives rejected: An empty `versions` entry that fakes a fixture (the version test would read it as verified); passing feature flags per task as a tool set (they remove whole tools and change the prompt, they do not choose a set); finding subagent rollouts by the start time and the workspace (the session metadata names the session); copying the databases with `cp` (a database open in write-ahead-log mode is not consistent on disk); failing the capture without an adapter (the capture is what the adapter is built from); recording the instruction file's hash and leaving it in place (the user chose clean runs); a private `CODEX_HOME` with a copy of the login (it handles credentials, and covers Codex only); moving the files aside for the whole capture (the user's other sessions would miss them for its whole length).

### I-80: Codex reads a model catalog pinned by hash, kept private outside the repository, in place of the server's per-model settings
Status: Accepted, implemented (`scripts/extract-model-catalog.py`; `model.catalog` of the `codex` profile (`path`, `sha256`, `override_env`, `cache`); `-c model_catalog_json={catalog}` in its invocation; the hash check, the catalog's hash in the model pin and in `task-meta.txt` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `TestCaptureRefusesAMissingOrChangedCatalog`). The user's decisions of 2026-10-31: pin the server's per-model settings if a local copy works, and keep the copy out of the repository.
Date: 2026-10-31.
Context: Codex 0.166.0 receives per model, from the server, the settings that shape its harness (for `gpt-6.1-terra`: `tool_mode` `code_mode_only`, `shell_type` `unified_exec`, `apply_patch_tool_type` `freeform`, `multi_agent_version` `v2`, the context window, and the instruction templates) and caches them in `~/.codex/models_cache.json`. A server change would change the harness under a frozen version. The binary has a configuration key `model_catalog_json`, a path to a JSON file with a non-empty `models` list. The entry is mostly OpenAI's instruction text (about 63 kB of 66 kB), which the repository must not redistribute.
Decision: The catalog is `{"models": [entry]}` with the cache's `gpt-6.1-terra` entry alone, written by `scripts/extract-model-catalog.py CACHE MODEL OUT` deterministically (the entry's keys in the cache's order, two-space indentation, ASCII escapes, a final newline) to a private path, by default `~/.config/agent-trace/codex-catalog-0.166.0.json`, `$AGENT_TRACE_CODEX_CATALOG` overriding it. The profile pins its SHA-256, `d1a0ac453cf7c4fac6b715373cd6dcf5f0824e6e47e54cb5b10dcdd6e9d2231f`, the entry Codex 0.166.0 cached on 2026-10-31 (fetched 20:41 UTC); the script reproduces that hash from the cache byte for byte. The capture refuses, before it asks for sudo, a catalog that is missing or has another hash, and prints the extraction command; a hash that still differs after extraction means the server's settings changed, a decision for the user. Every run passes the catalog's absolute path as `-c model_catalog_json=`. That Codex reads it was measured: a copy whose `effective_context_window_percent` was 94 instead of 95 made the rollout's context window 470000 instead of 475000; the exact copy gave 475000, `gpt-6.1-terra` and effort `medium` in every turn context, and no fallback-metadata warning. The fallback (recording the cache's entry in the manifest) is therefore not needed.
Consequences: A change of the server's settings no longer reaches the captures; a capture of another model needs its own catalog and pin, or Codex runs it on fallback metadata. No tracked file holds the instruction text; a machine whose cache no longer holds the pinned entry cannot capture until the pin is decided again. Whether the server still sends `/models` and whether `models_cache.json` changes during a run were not measured.
Alternatives rejected: The catalog in the repository (it redistributes OpenAI's instructions); only the four named settings (Codex would run the model without its instructions, which is not what the server sends); recording the cache's entry per capture (it documents a change without preventing it).

### I-81: An adapter may declare its harness's launcher; the capture is then rooted at the harness process
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent.Launcher`, `agent.Rooted` in `pkg/agent/launcher.go`, applied by `agent.Prepare` and `agent.Capture`; `TestRootedRootsTheCaptureAtTheHarness`, `TestRootedFailsClosed`, `TestPrepareAndCaptureRootAtTheHarness`). The Codex adapter declares one (`pkg/agent/codex`, a later change). Codex's declaration: I-82.
Date: 2026-11-01 (the first Codex capture, `codex-0.166.0-basic-20261101-0040`).
Context: The capture script runs the profile's `binary` as found on `PATH`, and `watch` roots the process tree at what it starts (`setpriv` execs it in place). For Claude Code and agy that is the harness itself. Codex's `codex` on `PATH` is the npm package's `bin/codex.js`, a Node script that spawns the native binary (`.../@openai/codex-linux-x64/vendor/<target>/bin/codex`) as its child and waits for it. In every run of the first capture the root (pid of `node`) reads its libraries, its script and the package's metadata, makes NSS socket connects (from `setpriv --init-groups`), forks one child and does nothing else; the child execs the native binary, and every effect of the session, apply_patch's file writes included, is the child's (**Verified**, the capture's six ground truths). Rooted at the launcher, the harness is a level-1 command whose whole activity is the content of one unclaimed command: no file claim can pair, and every run is NOT FAITHFUL.
Decision: An adapter may implement `agent.Launcher`, `HarnessProcess(g)`, naming the harness process when the capture's root is its launcher in the shape the adapter measured. `agent.Rooted` then roots the capture at that process, for `Prepare` (the run verified) and `Capture` (each control) alike, and drops the records of the launch that a harness exec'd in place leaves out of the capture: the fork that created the process, its execs before any other event of it, and its exits. It does so only when the named process is the root's only child and the root's own events are reads (`file_open`, `file_read`) and local socket connects; otherwise the capture is left as it is. The launcher's events stay in the capture, outside the tree.
Consequences: The harness's in-process tools are level-0 events and its commands level 1, as for the other agents. The launcher's reads and socket connects are reported as outside events, which change no outcome; a launcher that writes, connects to a host or starts a second process is not rerooted, so the run reads as if no launcher were declared and is not FAITHFUL. A harness that exec'd another program in place after its start keeps that exec, as a level-0 event no claim names. `cmd/verify` prints and writes the capture's root pid, not the harness's.
Alternatives rejected: Running the native binary directly from the profile (a capture-script and profile change, which leaves the first capture unverifiable and ties the profile to the package's install layout); treating the launcher's single child as the agent for every adapter (a harness that forks a helper first would be misrooted silently); rerooting in `NormalizeStream` (it does not receive the root pid).

### I-82: The Codex adapter claims the commands and file changes the rollout's items record, in the order of the cells that made them
Status: Accepted, implemented (`pkg/agent/codex`: `Parse`, `readRollout`, `itemClaims`, `changeClaims` in `rollout.go`; `recoverCommand` in `shell.go`; `NormalizeStream` in `stream.go`; `Adapter.HarnessProcess`; `TestParseClaimsEachEffectWithItsCellsInterval`, `TestParseCountsWhatItDoesNotClaim`, `TestParseFollowsSubagentRollouts`, `TestRecoverCommandReadsTheMeasuredWrapper`, `TestRecoverCommandRefusesAnythingElse`, `TestNormalizeStreamFoldsTheMeasuredShapes`, `TestPairedCorroboratesEveryClaimAtEveryAgreement`). Uses I-81. Extended by I-87 (2026-11-01): an update claims its content when the session recorded the file's whole content before it. Extended by I-94 (2026-11-01): a command that outlived its cell takes the cell that started it. Amended by I-99 (2026-11-01): the create of an added file is folded at any distance in time from its creating open.
Date: 2026-11-01 (the first capture, `codex-0.166.0-basic-20261101-0040`, and the survey of 0.166.0 of 2026-10-31).
Context: Codex 0.166.0 runs `gpt-6.1-terra` in code mode (I-80): the model calls one tool, `exec`, whose JavaScript calls the nested tools (`tools.exec_command`, `tools.apply_patch`), and a few direct tools (`wait`, the collaboration tools). The rollout records each model call (`custom_tool_call` or `function_call`, with its output record), and what the nested tools did as `event_msg` `item_completed` records: a `CommandExecution` with the argv `["/bin/bash", "-lc", <command>]`, its status and exit code, a `FileChange` with, per file, `add` and the whole content or `update` and a unified diff, an `Extension` (`web.search`, run on the model API's side), a `SubAgentActivity` naming a subagent's thread. An item names its thread and turn, not the cell; its record and its `completed_at_ms` fall inside the window of the cell that made it, between the cell's record and its output's (**Verified**: all five items of the task run). On the host (**Verified**, the capture's ground truths): the npm launcher starts the native binary (I-81); each command is a child of the harness whose first exec is the binary's process setup, `/proc/self/exe --codex-run-as-process-setup <fd> <pid> pipe '' <cwd> /bin/bash /bin/bash -c <script>`, which then execs `/bin/bash -c <script>` and, by the script's last line `exec '/bin/bash' -c '<command>'`, `/bin/bash -c <command>` in the same process (no login shell, whatever the record says); the script's other lines restore the shell snapshot's environment in a fixed set of shapes. apply_patch writes in the harness's own process, in place: an add is the create, the open with the hash of empty content, a write and the close with the content's hash; an update is three opens, a write and the close, with network events of the harness's other threads between the opens. A subagent's rollout starts by replaying its parent's history up to `subagent_history_start_ordinal`, and its items name its own thread.
Decision: A `CommandExecution` with an exit code claims a `process_exec` of its command, read from the argv only in the recorded form; another form is a parse error, one with no exit code is counted. A completed `FileChange` claims, per file in the record's order, an `add` as a `file_open` and a `file_write` with the `output_hash` of the content, an `update` as a `file_open` and a `file_write` with no hash (`Expresses` says the format cannot state it), a `delete` as a `file_delete` (with a degradation, unmeasured), and a move as nothing (counted, with a degradation). Each item takes the interval of the one cell of its turn whose window holds its completion (start the cell's record, end its output's, `IntervalDecision`), and the items of one cell share a `BlockID`; an item that no single cell holds is a point at its completion, with a degradation.
