Exits are not claimed. `Extension` items and the known direct tools are counted as non-effectful; an unknown item type or direct tool is listed in `UnknownTools`. Subagent rollouts in `<rollout without .jsonl>/subagents` (where the capture copies them) whose metadata names the session are read from their history start, with `ThreadID` their thread; an item of another thread in a rollout is a parse error. `Normalize` recovers the command from the setup's or the restoring shell's line only when every line before the exec line is one of the measured shapes and the rest is one quoted word (a cut word as a prefix when the event is truncated, V-26). `NormalizeStream` drops the failed attempts of a PATH search (an exec followed within a millisecond by the process's next event, an exec of the same name and arguments from another absolute directory), the create of a file the harness creates, the repeated opens of a run (only a file event of the process ends one) and folds the open, writes and close of an in-place rewrite into the open and one write with the close's hash; not for a command's processes or the harness's own files.
Consequences: On the first capture all eight claims are corroborated, the add on its content, at every agreement (I-86). An update's content is not checked. Interval checks rest on the cell's window; a cell that runs several tools at once, or one yielded and resumed with `wait`, has not been captured. The `pty` setup form, other shells, and a command line cut inside the restoring script are not recovered and read as mismatches.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming the cell's JavaScript (it is a program, not an effect: the items say what ran); reading an update's final content from earlier records (it needs the file's content before, which the session states only sometimes); claiming exit codes (a parallel cell's exits would be ordered by completion against claims ordered by cell; unmeasured).

### I-83: Codex's own activity: the per-run ids, its directory, its bookkeeping databases and its one occasional command
Status: Accepted, implemented (`canonicalIDs`, `harnessOwned`, `HarnessBookkeeping`, `HarnessCommands`, `HarnessHosts` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestCanonicalReplacesThePerRunIDs`, `TestDeclarations`, `TestPairedCorroboratesEveryClaimAtEveryAgreement`). The housekeeping policy of Claude Code (V-43, V-50, V-34) applied to Codex, as the user asked. Extended by I-88 (2026-11-01): controls that apply a patch measure `git remote -v`; and by I-89: the model traffic is declared housekeeping. Extended by I-95 (2026-11-01): the SQLite maintenance lock's open and close are declared bookkeeping.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: In every run of the capture the harness process runs `lsb_release -a` and `getconf LONG_BIT` three times each, the shell snapshot command (`/bin/bash -lc` with the snapshot script) and its check (`/bin/bash -c set -e; . "<home>/.codex/shell_snapshots/<thread>.tmp-<n>"`), and `codex-code-mode-host` when the first cell runs; it writes its rollout, its databases (`state_5`, `thread_history_1`, `logs_2`, `goals_1`, `queue_1`, `memories_1`, each with `-wal` and `-shm`), its thread lock, its shell snapshot, its app caches through `.tmp<6>` temporaries and its arg0 directory `~/.codex/tmp/arg0-linux/codex-arg0<6>` of helper links; it connects to `chatgpt.com` and `ab.chatgpt.com`. Which database writes and deletes a run shows varies with when the checkpoint falls (between one and five of six runs per file). Only the task run, after its last item, ran `git -c safe.bareRepository=explicit -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false remote -v`; in the survey it followed every turn that applied a patch (two runs) and no other turn (three runs). It also reads three rollouts of the account's older threads at start-up.
Decision: `Canonical` replaces the rollout's date and thread, the shell snapshot's thread and stamp, the arg0 directory's id, the thread lock's id and the app caches' temporaries with fixed tokens. `IsHarnessOwned` is `~/.codex/`. `HarnessBookkeeping` declares, for 0.166.0, the writes, closes and deletes of the six databases and their `-wal` and `-shm` files. `HarnessCommands` declares the `git remote -v` line as the PATH search ends it (`/usr/bin/git ...`), since `NormalizeStream` drops the failed attempts that name the per-run arg0 directory. `HarnessHosts` declares none: both hosts are in every run, which the baseline explains. `HarnessHousekeeping` declares nothing: no Codex capture read request content.
Consequences: The capture's harness file events are explained at every agreement. A run that applies a patch runs `git remote -v`, which no control of the current control task does, so such a run is INCONCLUSIVE on an unexplained harness command (V-43) and never FAITHFUL; how to explain a command a tool call implies is a policy question (Known limits of the adapters' page). The declared command's line names `/usr/bin/git`, so a machine whose PATH finds git elsewhere reads it as an unrecorded command.
Alternatives rejected: A harness host for `ab.chatgpt.com` (it is in every run); declaring the app caches or the snapshot as bookkeeping (every run writes them, so the baseline explains them).

### I-84: Codex declares its rollouts and its two thread databases as the trace
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.Trace` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestDeclarations`, `TestPairedACommandDeletingTheRolloutIsTampering`). The user's choice of the trace for the fail-closed rule (V-59). Amended 2026-11-02 by I-106: the databases' `-shm` is not covered, and their `-wal` and `-journal` are transient. Amended 2026-11-02 by I-109: once the session's thread is known, only its rollout and its subagents' are declared.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The user chose the trace: the main rollout, the subagent rollouts, `~/.codex/thread_history_1.sqlite` and `~/.codex/state_5.sqlite`; a change by anything but the harness is tampering, a read is not. A rollout's name holds its date and thread, not the workspace.
Decision: `Main` is every `<home>/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<time>-<uuid>.jsonl`; `Covers` adds the two databases with their `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files. The home is the capture's, else any home I-30 declares.
Consequences: A command that writes, renames or deletes any rollout of the account or either database is tampering. On the capture the rollout's last write is not a hashed close, so `--check-trace-hash` compares nothing (it says so).
Alternatives rejected: The main rollout only (a subagent's rollout is part of the session, and its name is unknown when it is gone).

### I-85: Codex's security-sensitive files are its configuration layers and hooks, from the strings of its binary
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.SensitiveFiles` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestDeclarations`). Applies I-59.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The 0.166.0 binary names `config.toml` under `CODEX_HOME`, `managed_config.toml`, `requirements.toml`, `hooks.json` and a project's `.codex/config.toml` (**Reported**, the survey's strings). The capture runs with `--ignore-user-config`, and its snapshots hold Claude Code's and agy's files only, since the adapter did not exist when it was taken.
Decision: `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.codex/managed_config.toml`, `~/.codex/requirements.toml`, `~/.codex/hooks.json` and `{workspace}/.codex/config.toml`, each checked whole.
Consequences: `watch` without `--agent` now snapshots these too. The first capture's verdict lists them as not named by the snapshot (a limitation, no reason). `~/.codex/auth.json`, which a token refresh writes, is credentials, not settings, and is not declared, as Claude Code's credential file is not.

### I-86: Codex 0.166.0 is configured on the first capture; the fixture keeps the rollout's calls and items and no database
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `versions` of the `codex` profile in `config/agents.json`; `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` for Codex; `pkg/agent/codex/testdata/paired-codex-0.166.0-basic`; `checkCodexFixture` in `pkg/config/fixtures_test.go`; `TestPairedCaptureLostOnlyTheStartupCleanup`, `TestPairedTaskClaimsEveryEffectOnce`, `TestPairedCatchesAlteredTrajectories`).
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The capture's rollouts hold the account's user and account ids, OpenAI's instructions and the model's reasoning; its database snapshots every thread of the account; its ground truths the user's home, three older threads' rollout names and the ids naming the user's synced skill directories.
Decision: The fixture keeps, of each rollout, the records and fields the adapter reads (I-82), with the session's paths rewritten, and no database; the ground truths are rewritten as for the other agents, and the other threads' rollouts, the skill directories' ids and the app caches' key become placeholders. The script refuses to write a fixture that still holds one of them, the home, the user name or an address. Codex 0.166.0 is listed with the fixture, verified 2026-11-01.
Consequences: The capture verifies with every claim corroborated, INCONCLUSIVE on its loss and on the harness's `git remote -v` (I-83); a listed version's fixtures verify as their tests pin them, not necessarily FAITHFUL (Claude Code's `paired-2.1.286-project-heredoc` is INCONCLUSIVE too).

### I-87: A Codex update claims its content when the session already recorded the file's whole content before it
Status: Accepted, implemented (`reconstruct`, `applyDiff`, `wholeFileRead` in `pkg/agent/codex/content.go`, called from `readRollout`; `Adapter.Expresses`; `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` (`cat_path`, the kept output and diff); `TestApplyDiffAppliesOnlyAnExactDiff`, `TestWholeFileReadIsOnlyACompleteCat`, `TestAnUpdateClaimsTheContentItsDiffGivesTheRecordedContent`, `TestAnUpdateMustStartAfterItsSourceCompleted`, `TestPairedTaskClaimsEveryEffectOnce`, `TestPairedCatchesAlteredTrajectories`). The user's decision (b) of 2026-11-01. Extends I-82.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: Codex records an apply_patch update as a unified diff only (I-82), so its write claimed no content and a substituted update of the right file was not caught. The session's record sometimes already holds the file's whole content before the update: an add of the file in the same session, an earlier update whose content is known, or a command `cat <path>` whose item records Codex's own parse of it (`parsed_cmd`, one `read` of that path) and its whole output (`aggregated_output`). Codex 0.166.0 marks a cut output with "tokens truncated", "chars truncated", "... command output truncated for persistence ..." or "[omitted ... items ...]" (**Reported**, the strings of its binary). On the first capture both updates have such a source: a.txt its add, b.txt the `cat b.txt` before it (**Verified**, the raw rollout).
Decision: Walking the rollout's items in record order, the adapter keeps the content of a file from an add, a whole-file read, or an update whose content was known; a delete or a move forgets it. A whole-file read is a completed `CommandExecution` with exit code 0 that is exactly `cat <word>` (one unquoted path word, no option, no `..`), parsed by Codex as one read of that word, run in a recorded working directory when the word is relative, with an output that is valid UTF-8 and holds none of the cut markers nor the replacement character. An update applies its diff to the known content only when the diff is hunks alone, in order and apart, every context and removed line equal to the file's line at its place, every header's counts equal to its lines and its new start equal to where it lands (with `\ No newline at end of file` handled); when the source completed strictly before the update started (their recorded milliseconds); and when no item that may write any file may have run between them: a command that is not a whole-file read, a failed change or an unknown item is ruled out only when it completed before the source's cell started or the cell it is joined to started after the update started. The update's write then claims the SHA-256 of the result, and `Expresses` says an update states an output hash exactly when it claims one. Nothing is claimed in a session with a subagent, in a rollout with a `wait` call or an unknown direct tool, or in a subagent's rollout. Every other update keeps its open and write without a hash. The fixture script keeps a whole-file read's working directory, parsed form and output for a file of the task's workspace, and an update's diff there, and refuses one the rewriting would change.
Consequences: On the first capture both updates claim their content and are corroborated on it; a substituted update is a mismatch. The report says how many updates claim their content and how many do not. A read or a reconstruction that is wrong makes an honest update a mismatch, never a pass: the hash is compared with what the probe hashed. A command that outlives its cell (a yielded `exec_command`), joined by its completion to a later cell, is not seen as running between the source and the update; a file it wrote there would read as a mismatch. A `cat` whose output a pseudo-terminal rewrote (CRLF) would too; the capture's setup is `pipe`. Reads other than a bare `cat` (`sed -n`, `head`, a quoted path) give no content.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming an update's content only after an add (the brief's decision includes whole-file reads); trusting any `parsed_cmd` read (it covers `sed -n` and `head`, which read part of a file); applying a diff with fuzz or offsets (a guess); a separate tool name for a hashed update (the verifier reads the hash itself).

### I-88: A profile may name an edit tool; its controls then also create a scratch file with it, excluded from the baseline
Status: Accepted, implemented (`controls.edit_tool` read by `scripts/agent-profile.py` and `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `{file}` in the control prompts; `agent.ControlFile`, `agent.ControlFileTarget`; `CaptureOptions.Exclude` matched against the templated target in `agent.Capture`; `BuildBaseline` in `cmd/analyze`; `apply_patch` in the `codex` profile; `TestCaptureExcludesTheControlsOwnEditInEachWorkspace`, `TestCaptureOfAnAgentWithoutAnAdapter`, `TestCaptureOfAnIntactTranscriptIsUnchanged`, `TestTheControlEditIsCheckedInTheProfile`). The user's decision (a) of 2026-11-01. Extends I-28 and I-71.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: Codex runs `git -c ... remote -v` after every turn that applied a patch (I-83). The controls edited nothing, so no baseline held the command and no honest Codex run with a patch could be FAITHFUL. The user chose that the controls also apply a patch, so the baseline learns the command from evidence, as a generic step any agent can enable, with Claude Code's and agy's controls unchanged. In one unprivileged trial of the new control prompt under `strace` (2026-11-01, outside the repository), Codex ran the marker command, added the file, and then ran `/usr/bin/git -c safe.bareRepository=explicit -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false remote -v`.
Decision: A profile's `controls` may name an `edit_tool`. Every control prompt of such a profile (`prompt`, `prompt_subagent` and each task's `control_prompt`) must name `{file}`, and no prompt of a profile without one may; `agent-profile.py` refuses otherwise. The capture script fills `{file}` with `agent-trace-control.txt` (`agent.ControlFile`), refuses a task whose tool set lacks the edit tool, and passes `--exclude '{workspace}/agent-trace-control.txt'` to `baseline` beside the control command. An exclusion is matched against a target as normalized and as templated with the run's workspace, so it names the file in every control's own workspace: the edit is the control agent's action and becomes neither a rule nor unstable, while what the harness did because of it is learned. `cmd/analyze` excludes the file for every agent, which changes no rule when the controls never name it. Codex's profile names `apply_patch`; its controls run the marker command, then add the file containing `ok` (and, for a task with the subagent tool or the tools task, the rest of their steps as before).
Consequences: A Codex capture taken from now on has controls that run `git remote -v`, so the command is a baseline rule with the controls' subtree, and an honest run that applies a patch can be FAITHFUL. Controls taken before (the first capture's) have no edit, and `--baseline-from` refuses them for a new task run, since the control prompt differs. A run's own event on `{workspace}/agent-trace-control.txt` is never explained by the baseline. The edit adds an `apply_patch` add to every control, whose other harness activity (an add's create, open, write and close of the file, all on the excluded file) is excluded with it; anything else the harness does around an edit becomes a rule, which is the intent. Claude Code's and agy's command lines, prompts and baselines are unchanged.
Alternatives rejected: A harness-implied command claim for `git remote -v` (V-33 extended to commands; the user chose evidence from the controls); declaring the command explained without controls (it would explain whatever the command did); a second control prompt beside the first (the step is part of the control task, which a profile writes in its own words); the file's name in the profile (it is a fixed marker like the control command, which `cmd/analyze` needs without the profile).

### I-89: Codex's model traffic over its WebSocket is declared harness housekeeping
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.HarnessHousekeeping` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestHousekeepingIsTheModelTrafficOnly`, `TestPairedModelTrafficIsHousekeeping`). Applies V-54. Requested on 2026-11-01 for the content capture of Codex.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: With content capture (the `tls-rustls-aead` method and the net probe's WebSocket records, built in parallel and not yet merged), Codex's model traffic appears as a `net_request` `GET https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses` without a body (the WebSocket upgrade, answered 101) and a `net_request` `WS https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses` per client message, with the message's hash, by the harness process. No Codex capture with content exists yet in this repository.
Decision: For 0.166.0 the adapter declares as housekeeping exactly those two targets: the GET without a body and the WS message with one. Another method, path, host or query, a GET with a body and a message without one are not declared; the verifier applies a declaration to level-0 events only (V-54).
Consequences: A level-0 model request no claim names is explained by the declaration, listed under declared harness housekeeping, whether or not the controls were captured with content. Requests of a command (level 1 and below) are not covered. Until a capture with content exists, the declaration rests on the record shapes of that content capture and on synthetic tests.
Alternatives rejected: An origin declaration for `chatgpt.com` (V-57 admits only body-less GETs, and the messages have bodies); leaving the traffic to the baseline alone (it explains it only when the controls were captured with content too).

### I-90: The capture script queries the agent's version in a throwaway home
Status: Accepted, implemented (the version query of `scripts/capture-agent.sh` and `agent_version` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `TestTheVersionQueryRunsInAThrowawayHome`).
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The first Codex 0.166.0 capture with content (2026-11-01) was INCONCLUSIVE with the baseline for one reason: six level-0 events of the task run in `~/.codex/tmp/arg0-linux/.tmp<id>/` (an open, a close and four deletes) that nothing explained; the first Codex capture had lost the same six events (V-39). Each Codex start-up creates its own `codex-arg0<id>` directory there, removes it at exit and, first, removes every directory there whose lock is free, renaming each to `.tmp<id>` before deleting it (**Verified** by tracing a start-up). `codex --version` exits without its clean-up and leaves its directory (**Verified**). The capture script ran that query once before the first run, outside `watch`, so the first run, the task's unless `--controls-before` is given, inherited a directory to remove and no control did; the baseline could not learn it, and templating the random name would not help.
Decision: The capture script and the campaign runner run the version query with `HOME` set to a fresh temporary directory, removed right after, for every agent. The version printed does not depend on the home (**Verified** for `claude`, `agy` and `codex`).
Consequences: Codex's task and controls start from the same state of `~/.codex/tmp/arg0-linux`, so the capture of 2026-11-01 would have shown no unexplained harness activity, and the first run no longer loses events to a directory removed before the probe read them. A directory left by a Codex run killed before its clean-up, inside or outside a capture, still reaches the next run the same way; no declaration covers it. An agent whose version query needs its real home (none of the three) would fail the query, which the script reports as printing nothing.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the clean-up as harness bookkeeping (it would set aside deletes the controls never make, for a cause the capture itself created); templating the `.tmp<id>` name in the baseline (no control removes a stale directory, so there is no rule to template); running the query before every control as well (it would add to every run an effect the agent's own start-up does not make).

### I-91: The Codex adapter reads its model requests from the rollout's items and token usage records
Status: Accepted, implemented (`requestReader` in `pkg/agent/codex/requests.go`, the `token_usage_record` case of `readRollout` and the merge in `Parse`; `TestParseReadsTheModelRequests`, `TestParseModelRequestsEdges`). Applies P-43. Amended 2026-11-01 by V-70: a subagent's replayed history enters its first request. Amended 2026-11-01 by V-69: on a capture with the WebSocket decoded, every request of the task's thread was checked; the first, recorded as continuing none, went out continuing an unrecorded startup warm-up, and with the warm-up set aside all were corroborated item for item. Amended 2026-11-01 by I-97: an `agent_message` is sent, and a tool output is digested without its empty `input_text` parts.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: P-43 checks each model request an adapter reads from its record against the captured request continuing the same response. Codex's rollout (0.166.0, **Verified** on the content capture of 2026-11-01) records the items it sends as `response_item` records (an `additional_tools` record, developer and user messages, tool outputs) among the model's own (reasoning, calls, assistant messages), and after each model call a `token_usage_record` naming the response's id; it records no `previous_response_id`.
Decision: Per thread, the items whose type ends in `_output`, the messages of role user, developer or system and the `additional_tools` record recorded since the previous `token_usage_record` are one request; the next `token_usage_record` names the response it got, and the previous one the response it continues (none for the thread's first). A subagent's replayed records before its history start ordinal are skipped, as for claims. Each item is digested whole by `tlsparse.ResponsesItemDigest`. The method is `tls-rustls-aead`.
Consequences: On the content capture of 2026-11-01 the adapter reads six requests, the first with seven items and each later one with one tool output; none could be checked, since that capture recorded no WebSocket message (N-43). Whether the wire carries the developer messages and `additional_tools` as input items, and whether a subagent's first request continues a response, are what a recapture measures. The fixture script keeps none of these records: the items hold the harness's instructions, the user's prompt and the tools' outputs, which no tracked file may hold (how a fixture could keep the check is a question for the user).
Alternatives rejected: Reading the requests from the `token_count` events (they name no response); taking every response item since the previous response (the model's own items are in the response the request continues, not in the request).

### I-97: The Codex adapter reads a request's items as Codex sends them: agent messages included, empty text parts of a tool output left out
Status: Accepted, implemented (`requestReader.item` and `withoutEmptyText` in `pkg/agent/codex/requests.go`; `TestParseModelRequestsAsCodexSendsThem`). Amends I-91.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: On the Codex 0.166.0 long-content capture of 2026-11-01 and the content capture of the same day, both with the WebSocket decoded (**Verified**), the model request check (P-43, I-91) found two kinds of difference by recomputing digests from the rollout. First, a tool output whose recorded `output` list ends in a part `{"type":"input_text","text":""}` went out without that part: the captured digest equals the recorded item's digest with the part removed, for every such output of both captures and none kept on the wire. Second, a request after a `wait_agent` call carried, after its tool output, an item of type `agent_message` (the subagent's report to its parent, with author and recipient), equal to the rollout's `agent_message` record; a subagent's first request carries its own `agent_message` (the parent's task) the same way. I-91 read neither.
Decision: An `agent_message` record is an item the harness sends, like a user message. A tool output (a type ending in `_output`) is digested with every part of its `output` list that is exactly `{"type":"input_text","text":""}` (those two members, the text empty) removed; any other part, an empty part of another type or with another member included, is kept, and an output that is not a list is digested as recorded. The probe's digest (`tlsparse.ResponsesItemHash`) is unchanged.
Consequences: On the long-content capture the task's requests that differed by an empty part or an agent message are corroborated, and so are the controls of the content capture that each differed by one empty part. An empty text part carries no text, so the request and the record hold the same text in two forms; what the check gives up is telling apart a request that carried an empty part from one that did not, which no attack needs. Nothing else in a tool output is rewritten: a truncated or reformatted output still differs.
Alternatives rejected: Dropping empty parts in `tlsparse.ResponsesItemHash` for every harness (it would change the probe's digest for all captures to fit one harness's serializer); dropping empty parts of another type, or from another member such as a message's content (no capture shows it; each widening needs its own evidence). Every empty part the captures show is the last of its list; the rule drops one at any place in the list, since its place changes no text.

### I-98: The overhead benchmark and the comparison tools read the agent profiles
Status: Accepted, implemented (`fixtures_of`, `fixture_runs` and `cmd_plan` in the helper `scripts/bench-overhead.sh` writes, and its `--dry-run`; `profiles`, `trace_method` and `fixture_paths` in `scripts/compare/common.py`; `RT_TRACE` and the `--agents` default in `scripts/compare/sweep.py`; `TestBenchmarkDryRunListsEveryProfiledFixture`, `TestComparisonFixturesComeFromTheProfiles`). Extends I-77.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: I-77 left `scripts/bench-overhead.sh` and `scripts/compare/` naming agents. The benchmark found its fixtures by globbing the Claude Code and agy adapter directories, chose the session file's suffix by agent and passed `--agent-version` to `verify` for agy only, so Codex's paired fixture was never timed. The comparison tools took the gated captures from the Claude Code adapter's directory with the agent written in, defaulted `--agents` to Claude Code and agy, and ran reproducible-trajectories only when the run's agent was named `claude-code`. The user's rule is that every agent is configured and handled the same way, its behaviour in its profile or adapter.
Decision: The benchmark's fixture part times every fixture the profiles list (`versions[].fixtures`), per agent in file order and by directory name within it, with the agent and version from the profile, the session file from `trace.file` with the fixture's label, and `--agent-version` passed exactly when `trace.states_version` is false; a listed fixture that is not paired (control captures, exactly one other ground truth, the session file) is left out, and `--dry-run` names it with the reason. The comparison finds each gated capture through the profile that lists its directory, which gives its path, agent and session file; `--agents` defaults to every profiled agent; reproducible-trajectories applies to a run whose profile's trace method is `jsonl-session`, the transcript format the tool reads, rather than to an agent by name.
Consequences: For Claude Code and agy every command the fixture part runs is unchanged, and the outputs of an unprivileged run (`--unprivileged-only --reps 1 --workloads simagent`) are byte-identical to those of the script before the change once the checkout path and the capture timestamps are normalized (**Verified**, 2026-11-01, against a `git archive` of the parent commit); Codex's fixture is added. The comparison's four subjects are unchanged, and `--plan-only` gives identical output on three campaigns (**Verified**, 2026-11-01). A campaign's `run.json` now records Codex among the default agents. The note on a run reproducible-trajectories cannot read keeps its text, which names the format the tool reads. Names that belong to the compared tools stay: Signet's `claude-code` plugin, halo-record's and Signet's Claude Code hook event format, and the Write and Edit tool names reproducible-trajectories replays.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the directory glob with a third adapter entry (the next agent would need another edit, against I-77); reading the profiles through `scripts/agent-profile.py` (its interface serves the shell capture scripts, and the benchmark's helper is written out of the script, away from the repository's import path); gating reproducible-trajectories by a new profile field (the trace method already says which transcript format a run has).

### I-94: A Codex command that outlived the cell that started it takes that cell, until its own completion
Status: Accepted, implemented (`startedIn` and the join in `readRollout`, `pkg/agent/codex/rollout.go`, also used as `reconstruct`'s cell start; `pkg/agent/codex/testdata/paired-codex-0.166.0-tools`; `TestParseClaimsAnOutlivingCommandFromTheCellThatStartedIt`, `TestAnUpdateClaimsTheContentItsDiffGivesTheRecordedContent`, `TestToolsBackgroundCommandIsClaimedFromItsStart`). Extends I-82.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: On the `tools` task of the campaign `codex-parity-1` (**Verified**, its rollout and ground truth) a cell ran `exec_command` of `sleep 3; echo background-done | tee bg.txt` with a 500 ms yield; the cell's output record (19:14:39.858Z) returned a session id, and the command's item, recorded 19:14:42.213Z, states `started_at_ms` 19:14:39.355Z, inside that cell's window (from 19:14:39.176Z), and its completion between two later cells. A later cell's `write_stdin` on the session read its output. I-82 joins an item by its completion only, so the command was a point at 19:14:42.213Z, three seconds after its exec (19:14:39.200Z): with the baseline the run was NOT FAITHFUL on an Unwitnessed claim, an Unrecorded exec and the command's 68 events as an unexplained subtree. A completion inside a later cell's window would have given the command that cell's interval, which does not hold its exec either. The same join placed such a command in the later cell for I-87's check, so it was not seen as running between a content source and an update.
Decision: A `CommandExecution` item whose `started_at_ms` lies in the window of exactly one cell of its turn, and whose completion comes after that cell's output record, is joined to that cell: its claim starts at the cell's start (D13) and ends at the item's completion, and it is a member of that cell's block when the cell has other effects. I-87's check takes that cell's start for it too. The report counts such commands in a degradation. Any other item, and a command whose start no single cell's window holds or whose completion lies inside the starting cell's window, is joined as before; no other interval is widened.
Consequences: The `tools` run verifies FAITHFUL with its baseline, all fifteen claims corroborated, where it was NOT FAITHFUL (**Verified**, `cmd/verify --baseline` on the raw capture, and the reduced fixture at one and at five controls in five). Every existing fixture's output is unchanged. The interval check of such a command is looser: an exec of it anywhere from the cell's start to the command's completion is inside its interval, where the cell's window alone was shorter; the claim still names one command and alignment still pairs it with one exec. Codex records the start after the exec (155 ms on this capture), so a command whose recorded start falls after its cell's output record is not recognised and stays a point.
Alternatives rejected: Widening every item's interval from its start (only a yielded command can outlive its cell; a file change completes inside it); requiring the session id in the cell's output (the output is what the model's script printed, which it may leave out); taking the later cell's end as the end (the item's completion is the command's own).

### I-95: Codex's SQLite maintenance lock is declared harness bookkeeping, its open and close only
Status: Accepted, implemented (`maintenanceLock` and `HarnessBookkeeping` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestDeclarations`, `TestToolsMaintenanceLockIsDeclaredBookkeeping`). Applies V-50 as the user chose it for Claude Code's version lock; extends I-83.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The `long` run of the campaign `codex-parity-1` (3.6 minutes) verified INCONCLUSIVE with its baseline for one reason: two level-0 events under `~/.codex` that no rule explained, a `file_open` of `~/.codex/.sqlite-maintenance.lock` 60.07 s after the harness process started and its `file_close` at the harness's exit 2.6 minutes later, with the hash of empty content and no write between (**Verified**, the raw ground truth). Both are the native harness's own (the process the launcher started, I-81), and no other process touched the path. The harness opened `logs_2.sqlite` and its `-wal` within a millisecond of the open, and closed its databases beside the close. The strings of the 0.166.0 binary name `.sqlite-maintenance.lock` between `PRAGMA freelist_count` and `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)`, `DELETE FROM logs WHERE ts < ?` and `state/src/runtime/logs_maintenance.rs` (**Reported**): it is the lock of the harness's periodic maintenance of its logs database. No control of the run, each 11 to 14 s long, and no other Codex capture on this machine, none longer than 21 s, shows it. It has the version lock's traits that V-50 rests on: an exact path under the harness's directory, touched by the harness process only, holding nothing the agent could use.
Decision: Codex's `HarnessBookkeeping` declares, for 0.166.0, `file_open` and `file_close` of `<home>/.codex/.sqlite-maintenance.lock`, with V-50's scope and outcome: level 0 only, an unambiguous and untruncated path no claim names, after the baseline, explained and listed under `Declared harness bookkeeping`. A write, delete, rename or create of the lock, another file and another version are not declared.
Consequences: The `long` run verifies FAITHFUL with its baseline (**Verified**, `cmd/verify --baseline` on the raw capture); every existing fixture's output and the attack evaluation are unchanged. Attacker view, as V-50's: the agent's in-process tool (`apply_patch` runs in the harness process) could open and close the lock unclaimed and be explained, which reveals nothing, since no content is written without a write event, and a write stays unexplained harness activity; the same events from a command are that command's subtree. A home where the lock does not exist yet, or a longer run with a second pass, may show events not declared, which stay V-24's.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring `~/.codex` files by pattern (V-50: exact files); also declaring a write of the lock (not observed); leaving it to the baseline (controls shorter than a minute never reach the first pass, and a control as long as the task changes the campaign's cost for one file).

### I-96: The fixture script replaces server response ids with placeholders, one mapping per fixture
Status: Accepted, implemented (`RESPONSE_ID`, the placeholder pass and the leak check in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `pkg/agent/codex/testdata/paired-codex-0.166.0-tools` regenerated). The user's decision (b) of 2026-11-01. Extends I-86. Amended by I-100: the server's item ids are replaced too, and `RESPONSE_ID` is now `SERVER_ID`.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: `paired-codex-0.166.0-tools` is the first fixture made from a content capture. Its ground truths keep each model request's `previous_response_id` and the response ids V-69's warm-ups name: 60 distinct OpenAI response ids (`resp_` and 50 hex digits), server-side ids of the account's responses. They are not account ids, so the script's check let them through, but they identify the account's conversations on the server.
Decision: After every other rewrite, the script replaces each match of `resp_` followed by 32 or more lowercase hex digits, in every text file it wrote (ground truths, rollouts, transcripts), by `resp_1`, `resp_2`, ... in order of first appearance, walking the files in path order, with one mapping across the fixture, so a request still names the response it continues and two files naming one response agree. A kept content, output or diff holding such an id is refused (its hash is claimed), and an id left in any written file, a file that is not UTF-8 included, fails the private-string check.
Consequences: Regenerated, the tools fixture holds no response id (`grep -E 'resp_[0-9a-f]{20,}'` finds none), and its `cmd/verify` text output with and without baselines at agreements 0.2 and 1, its baselines and the codex and config package tests are identical to before the swap; the JSON output differs only in the ids themselves. No other fixture held one. A WS request's `request_hash`, computed by the probe over the message as sent, covers the original id and is kept as captured; nothing compares it with a claim, since Codex states no request hash. Server item ids the rollout keeps (`rs_`, `msg_`) are not replaced.
Alternatives rejected: Dropping the fields (the model request check of a fixture whose rollout keeps its requests would lose the link between requests); a per-file mapping (a request in one file and its response in another would no longer agree).

### I-100: The fixture script replaces the model server's item ids too, never a call id
Status: Accepted, implemented (`SERVER_ID`, `placeholder` and the leak check in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `paired-codex-0.166.0-basic` and `paired-codex-0.166.0-tools` regenerated). The user's decision of 2026-11-01. Amends I-96.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: I-96 left the server's item ids in the Codex fixtures: a completed `Reasoning` item keeps its `rs_` id and an `AgentMessage` its `msg_` id, the prefix and 50 hex digits, one `msg_` in the basic fixture and three `msg_` and one `rs_` in the tools fixture. Like a response id they name the account's content on the server. A survey of every rollout and ground truth of the Codex captures (2026-11-01) found five prefixes followed by 50 hex digits, `resp_`, `rs_`, `msg_`, `ctc_` (a code-mode cell's item) and `fc_` (a direct tool call's item), and `call_` followed by 32. The other prefixed ids (`msg_`, `ctco_`, `fco_`, `amsg_`, `at_` and `retained_` followed by a UUID) are the client's, in UUID form.
Decision: The placeholder pass of I-96 takes `resp_`, `rs_`, `msg_`, `ctc_` and `fc_`, each followed by 32 or more lowercase hex digits, and gives each id the prefix and a number counted per prefix in order of first appearance (`rs_1`, `msg_1`, ...), one mapping across the fixture; `resp_` keeps its numbering. A call id is never replaced: the adapter joins a cell to its output and names a block by it, and the model request check compares the call ids of a request's items. A kept content, output or diff holding any of these ids is refused, and one left in a written file fails the private-string check.
Consequences: Regenerated, the two Codex fixtures differ from before only in those five ids, and hold no id of the five prefixes (`grep -E '\b(resp|rs|msg|ctc|fc)_[0-9a-f]{20,}'` finds none); `ctc_` and `fc_` reach no fixture today, since the script keeps no response item's id. The `cmd/verify` text and JSON output of every paired fixture, with no baseline and baselines at 0.2, 0.5 and 1, their baselines and the attacks' output are identical to before. The ids published before stay in the repository's history. A message id of another provider in the same form, all lowercase hex, would be replaced too; Anthropic's are not hex.
Alternatives rejected: Dropping the item ids (the adapter does not read them, but a fixture should keep the rollout's shape); replacing call ids too (they feed the adapter's joins and the request check's comparison).

### I-99: The Codex create fold takes the creating open at any distance in time
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldCreates` in `pkg/agent/codex/stream.go`; `pkg/agent/codex/testdata/paired-codex-0.166.0-tools-2`, `TestTools2SubagentAddSplitAcrossBatchesIsFolded`, `TestNormalizeStreamFoldsTheMeasuredShapes`). Amends I-82's reading of an add. Extended to the Gemini adapter by I-101.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The `tools` run of the campaign `codex-parity-2` was NOT FAITHFUL on one Unrecorded `file_write` of the file the subagent added with `apply_patch`, every claim corroborated. The subagent's rollout, its claims and their join to its cell were as in `codex-parity-1`, where the run is FAITHFUL; only the kernel record differed. The fs probe stamps each batch it reads with one clock reading plus a nanosecond per record (P-18). In `codex-parity-1` the create and the creating open of the file were one nanosecond apart, one batch; in `codex-parity-2` they were 11 microseconds apart, two consecutive reads (**Verified** on both ground truths), and `foldCreates` dropped a create only within a microsecond of its open. Where the reads split a burst of records depends on load, which P-18 rules out as a cause of a finding.
Decision: A create of a file by the harness's process (a `file_write` naming it, with no hash, not on the harness's own files) is dropped when the process's next file event is the open of that file, not ambiguous, with the hash of empty content, however far apart in time. The probe gives that hash only to the first open of a file born during the capture (P-22), so that open created the file, and the create states nothing it and the following write and close do not.
Consequences: The `codex-parity-2` `tools` run is FAITHFUL with its baseline, as its reduced fixture shows at one and at five controls in five; every other paired fixture's `cmd/verify` output, baselines and attack output are identical, and so is every other Codex capture's. A create followed by anything else of its process first (another file's event, an open without the empty hash) is kept, as before. The Gemini adapter's create fold, which shares the bound, is unchanged (Known limits of [30_agent_adapters.md](../architecture/30_agent_adapters.md#known-limits)).
Alternatives rejected: A wider time bound (any bound fails under enough load, and would be a measurement of this machine); folding in the probe (the create is the probe's record of a real directory change, and other adapters read it).

### I-101: The Gemini create fold takes the creating open at any distance in time
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldCreates` in `pkg/agent/gemini/stream.go`; `TestCreateFoldTakesTheCreatingOpenInALaterBatch`, `TestNormalizeStreamIsNarrow`, `TestNamedCreateFoldIsNarrow`). Applies I-99 to agy; amends the time bound of I-50 and of the Gemini part of I-56.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: I-50 dropped a create of a file by the agent's process only when the creating open followed within one microsecond, one batch of the fs probe (P-18), and I-56 kept that bound for the named shape. I-99 found the bound wrong for Codex: where the probe's reads split a burst of records depends on load. The private pilot capture of agy 1.4.7 (the `long` task, 2026-10-27) shows the same for agy: the `write_to_file` of `REPORT.md` is a create and, about 25 microseconds later in the next read, the open with the empty-content hash, its write and its close, and the create is the run's one Unrecorded `file_write` of a workspace file (**Verified**, local only).
Decision: As I-99 states for Codex: the create (an ambiguous `file_write` of a directory before P-25, a `file_write` naming the file since, with no hash, not on the harness's own files) is dropped when the process's next file event is the named open of a file directly in that directory (before P-25) or of that same file (since), with the input hash of empty content, however far apart in time. Only file events count, so another thread's connection between the two does not keep the create.
Consequences: Every paired fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and `--json`, with no baseline and with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, is byte-identical, and so is the attack evaluation (`go test ./pkg/attack`); on the private agy captures of 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-17 the output is identical too, and on the 1.4.7 pilot the Unrecorded `REPORT.md` create is gone, with and without the baseline (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only). A create whose process's next file event is anything else is kept, as before.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the bound for agy until a fixture shows the split (the pilot shows it, and the reason I-99 gives holds for every adapter).

### I-102: agy declares its conversation databases as main session files, and no path as covered yet
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.Trace` in `pkg/agent/gemini/trace.go`; `trace.declared` of the `gemini` profile; the trace-method dispatch of `trace_state` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh` and `STATE_CHECKED` in `scripts/agent-profile.py`; `TestTraceDeclaresTheConversationDatabases`, `TestReadTraceFailsClosed`, `TestPairedTheDeclarationChangesNoVerdict` in `pkg/agent/gemini`, `TestCaptureOfAgyKeepsARunWhoseDatabaseIsGoneOrDamaged`). Applies the user's fail-closed rule (V-59, I-74, I-75) to agy; which events are tampering is O-37. Amended 2026-11-01 by V-75: the databases with their `-wal` and `-journal` are covered, a close by a process that wrote nothing is a read; and by V-76: the `-wal` and `-journal` are transient. Amended 2026-11-02 by V-77 and I-105: a database that states its conversation id declares that conversation's database only.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: The user ruled that a missing or damaged trace reads "cannot verify: trace deleted or damaged", never a harness error, that reading the trace is not tampering, and asked for agy's trace to be declared as Claude Code's and Codex's are. agy keeps a session in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/conversations/<uuid>.db`, a SQLite database in write-ahead-log mode with its `-wal`, `-shm` and, at creation, `-journal`, and each subagent in a database of its own beside it (I-55); `brain/<uuid>/.system_generated/` holds transcripts and step outputs of the same conversation. On every agy capture at hand, fixtures and private raw captures, every write-class event on these paths in the controls and the honest task runs is the agent process's own, except in the 1.4.7 pilot, where the agent's `run_command` read every conversation database of the account, its own included, with Python's `sqlite3`: such a read opens the database for writing and leaves a close of the database and of its `-wal` and `-shm`, and, for a database no other process holds open, a create, writes and a delete of its `-wal` and `-shm` (**Verified**, local only). Every write-class event on a covered path is tampering under V-59, so covering these files would read a plain read as tampering, against the user's rule.
Decision: The Gemini adapter declares, under the capture's home (else any home I-30 declares), every `conversations/<uuid>.db` as a main session file and covers nothing. `agent.ReadTrace` therefore reads a missing, empty, unparsable or damaged database as V-59's unreadable trace, `Verdict.Trace` lists the events the capture saw on the databases, and nothing is read as tampering until O-37 is decided. The profile's `trace.declared` is true, so the capture script keeps a task run whose database is gone and passes `--check-trace-hash`; its state check reads a conversation database by a copy with its write-ahead log (missing, empty, damaged when the copy does not open, fails `quick_check` or has no step, else read).
Consequences: Every paired fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and `--json`, with no baseline and with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and the attack evaluation are unchanged (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only). With `--check-trace-hash`, the copy of the conversation database on each agy capture at hand (1.3.4 and the 1.4.7 pilot) equals the probe's hash of the database's last close at the harness's exit, and each verdict is the one without the flag (**Verified**, local only). A Gemini parse error (a call missing the argument the adapter maps) now makes the run INCONCLUSIVE as a damaged trace. A command that writes, renames or deletes a conversation database is not a finding until O-37 is decided; `cmd/verify` says that the adapter declares no path whose change is tampering.
Alternatives rejected: Covering the databases now (a read by a stock SQLite client would be tampering, which the user ruled out); declaring nothing until O-37 (a deleted database would stay a harness error, which the user ruled out).

### I-104: Claude Code's named replace takes its create at any distance in time; the pre-P-25 version-lock shape keeps its one-batch bound
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldReplacesWithNames` and `foldNamedLockRewrite` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/stream.go`, unchanged in code; `TestNamedFoldTakesACreateReadInAnEarlierBatch`). Applies I-99 and I-101 to Claude Code.
Date: 2026-11-01.
Context: I-99 and I-101 dropped the one-microsecond bound between a create and its creating open in the Codex and agy create folds: where the fs probe's reads split a burst of records depends on load (P-18). Claude Code has no fold of a lone create. Its named replace (I-56, I-76) takes the create as the process's last event on the temporary or the target before the open, with no bound in time, so a create read in an earlier batch already folds. One fold still bounds a create: the version lock's rewrite read between its create and its rename (I-44), a shape of captures made before P-25, whose create is an ambiguous `file_write` of the locks directory. On the checked-in fixtures that open names the lock and carries, in half of the runs, the lock's previous hash rather than the hash of empty content, so I-99's evidence for a create is not there (**Verified**, the fixtures' ground truths).
Decision: The named replace keeps taking its create at any distance, now covered by a test with the create and the open in one batch, 11 microseconds apart and a second apart. `foldNamedLockRewrite` keeps the bound: no capture made since P-25 holds its ambiguous shape, every checked-in capture that does folds within the bound, and without it the create would rest on the order of the process's events alone.
Consequences: No code path changes; every Claude Code fixture's `cmd/verify` output is byte-identical. A capture made before P-25 whose version-lock create was read in an earlier batch than its open would leave that rewrite raw, unexplained harness activity in the harness's own files; none is known.
Alternatives rejected: Dropping the lock shape's bound without the empty-content evidence (a weaker rule than I-99's, for captures that no longer arise); requiring the empty-content hash on the lock's open (three of the six checked-in rewrites in that shape would stop folding).

### I-105: agy's declaration takes the session's id from the conversation database the reader is given
Status: Accepted, implemented (`SessionTraceDeclarer` and the `session` argument of `Trace` in `pkg/agent/trace.go`; `Report.TraceSession` in `pkg/agent/agent.go`; `statedID` in `pkg/agent/gemini/gemini.go`; `Adapter.SessionTrace` in `pkg/agent/gemini/trace.go`; the calls in `cmd/verify` and `cmd/analyze`; `TestParseStatesTheConversationID`, `TestSessionTraceDeclaresTheSessionsOwnDatabaseOnly`, `TestAnotherConversationsDatabaseIsNotTheSessionsTrace`). Implements V-77. Amended 2026-11-02 by V-80: `SessionTrace` and `agent.Trace` take the session as an `agent.TraceSession`, with the subagent ids its record names. Amended 2026-11-02 by I-110: the id the capture recorded, passed as `verify --session-id`, decides in place of the stated one, and the fixtures keep the `cascade_id`.
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: V-77 declares the session's own conversation database, so the declaration needs the conversation's id. `Trace(workspace, home)` receives neither the session file nor its id. The capture script takes the id from agy's JSON output (`conversation_id`), copies `<id>.db` and its `-wal` as `<task>.conversation.db`, and does not keep the id in a file `verify` reads. agy's database states the id itself: its `trajectory_meta` table holds one row whose `cascade_id` is the conversation id, on 1.3.4 and on 1.4.7; on every agy capture at hand the stated id equals the printed one (**Verified**, local only). The checked-in fixtures keep the `steps` table only (`scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`, I-51).
Decision: `Parse` reads the main database's `trajectory_meta` and gives its one `cascade_id` as `Report.TraceSession` when it is a conversation id, and nothing when the table is absent, empty, holds more than one row, or holds another form. An adapter whose trace is one session's files implements `SessionTraceDeclarer` (`SessionTrace(workspace, home, session)`); `agent.Trace` takes a `session` argument, which `cmd/verify`, `cmd/analyze` and the attack evaluation fill from `Report.TraceSession`, and calls `SessionTrace` when it is set, `Trace` otherwise. The Gemini adapter's `SessionTrace` declares `<home>/.gemini/antigravity-cli/conversations/<id>.db` with its `-wal` and `-journal`; its `Trace`, for a database that states no id, keeps I-102's every database.
Consequences: Every paired fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and `--json`, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation; the fixtures state no id. On the private agy captures the JSON `parse` gains `trace_session`; on the 1.3.4 captures nothing else changes, and on the 1.4.7 pilot the tampering on other conversations' databases goes (V-77) (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only). The id is the trace's own statement: a command that rewrites it moves the declaration, and nothing compares it with the id agy printed (a known limit). A fixture made with a script that kept `trajectory_meta` would get the session's declaration.
Alternatives rejected: Passing the printed id to `verify` from the capture script as a flag (a change to `cmd/verify`'s interface and to every capture's command line, and a reader of an existing capture would have to supply it); inferring the session's database from the capture as the one the harness process wrote (the declaration would rest on the probes' attribution, and a subagent's database is written by the same process); naming the copy after the id (every capture's file names would change).

### I-106: Codex covers its thread databases as agy covers its conversation database: no -shm, the -wal and -journal transient
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.Trace` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestDeclarations`). The user's earlier choice that every agent follows the same rule, applied to V-75 and V-76. Amends I-84. Amended 2026-11-02 by I-109: the rollouts are narrowed to the session's own.
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: V-75 left Codex covering the `-shm` of `thread_history_1.sqlite` and `state_5.sqlite` (I-84), so a stock SQLite reader of them writes a covered file and reads as tampering, and said that whether Codex follows agy was the Codex adapter's to decide. The user ruled that the same rule holds for every agent. The `-shm` holds no row: every reader rebuilds it from the log and writes it through a shared mapping. A reader of a write-ahead-log database no other process holds open creates its `-wal` and deletes it at its close, as on agy.
Decision: The Codex declaration covers the two databases with their `-wal` and `-journal` and not their `-shm`, and declares the `-wal` and `-journal` transient (V-76). Its scope is unchanged: every rollout under `~/.codex/sessions` as a main session file and the two databases.
Consequences: Every Codex fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and `--json`, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation; on the private Codex captures at hand the output is identical too, since no process but the harness touched the databases there (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only). A stock SQLite reader of the thread databases is no longer tampering; a write, truncation, rename or delete of a database, its `-wal` or its `-journal` by a command still is. A change to the `-shm` alone is never seen, as on agy.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the `-shm` covered (every honest reader would be tampering, which the user ruled out for agy and for every agent).

### I-109: Codex's trace is the session's rollout, its subagents' rollouts and the two thread databases
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Report.TraceSession` and `Report.TraceSubagents` in `Adapter.Parse`, `pkg/agent/codex/rollout.go`; `Adapter.SessionTrace` in `pkg/agent/codex/harness.go`; `TestSessionTraceDeclaresTheSessionsRollouts`, `TestParseStatesTheThreadAndItsSubagents`, `TestPairedAnotherSessionsRolloutIsNotTheTrace`). The user's decision of 2026-11-02, which follows from option (c) of V-77: the trace is the session's own files. Amends I-84 and I-106.
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: I-84 declared every rollout under `~/.codex/sessions` as a main session file, since a rollout's name holds its date and thread, not the workspace, and a subagent's rollout is part of the session. A rollout is named `rollout-<time>-<thread id>.jsonl`, its first record states its thread id (`session_meta.id`), and the main rollout's `SubAgentActivity` items name the threads of the subagents it started; a subagent's rollout states the session's thread as its `session_id`. On the Codex captures at hand the harness opens other threads' rollouts at start-up, and a subagent's rollout name holds the thread id the parent's `agent_thread_id` names (**Verified**, the fixtures' ground truths and rollouts).
Decision: `Parse` gives the main rollout's thread id as `Report.TraceSession` and, as `Report.TraceSubagents`, every thread the session's rollouts start and every subagent rollout it reads, sorted, each of the thread-id form. The Codex adapter implements `SessionTraceDeclarer`: `SessionTrace` declares the session's rollout `rollout-<time>-<id>.jsonl` under `~/.codex/sessions` as the main session file, covers its subagents' rollouts (not main files, as Claude Code's subagents directory, I-74), and covers the two thread databases as I-106 does. Without a session id (`Trace`), every rollout stays declared as before.
Consequences:
- A command that writes, renames or deletes the session's rollout, a subagent's or either thread database is tampering, as before; one that changes another session's rollout is an ordinary file event, explained by its claim or a finding.
- Every paired fixture's `cmd/verify` text output, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation. On the three Codex fixtures the JSON `parse` gains `trace_session`, and on the two `tools` fixtures `trace_subagents`; with `--check-trace-hash` the JSON `trace.files`, the declared main files the capture observed, lists the session's rollout only, no longer the other threads' rollouts the harness opened at start-up nor the subagent's rollout, which is covered but not a main file. Nothing else changes, and the same holds on the seven private Codex task runs at hand (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
- The subagent ids are read from the trace: when the session's rollout is gone, the subagents' rollouts are not covered.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping every rollout (the user narrowed the scope to the session's own files); covering only the rollouts the capture copied (the declaration would rest on what the capture script found, not on the session's record).

### I-110: The session id the capture recorded decides the trace; the fixtures keep agy's cascade_id
Status: Accepted, implemented (`--session-id` and the `session id:` line in `cmd/verify/main.go`; `<label>.session-id.txt` and the `verify` runs in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; the `trajectory_meta` kept by `reduce_conversation` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; the regenerated `paired-agy-1.3.4-basic` and `paired-agy-1.3.4-search` databases; `TestSessionIDFromTheCaptureDecidesTheTrace`, `TestCaptureOfAgyKeepsARunWhoseDatabaseIsGoneOrDamaged`, `TestParseStatesTheConversationID`). The user's decision of 2026-11-02, which follows from option (c) of V-77. Amends I-105, whose rejected alternative it takes up. Amended by I-127 (the manifest and `cmd/analyze` read the recorded id).
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: I-105 reads agy's session id from inside the trace, the `cascade_id` of the conversation database, so a claimed command that rewrites it moves the declaration to another conversation, and nothing compared it with the id agy printed. The capture script knows every run's id outside the trace: the one it assigns (Claude Code) or the one the agent prints (`conversation_id` for agy, `thread_id` for Codex). The checked-in agy fixtures kept the `steps` table only, so they stated no id and exercised I-102's every-database scope, not the session's. On every private agy and Codex capture at hand the printed id equals the stated one (**Verified**, local only).
Decision: `cmd/verify` takes `--session-id ID`, generic for every agent. When given, it replaces the id the record states in the session passed to `agent.Trace` (`agent.SessionOf`), so for an adapter whose trace is one session's files (agy, Codex) it decides the declaration, also when the session file is gone; the subagents still come from the record. The report prints a `session id:` line after the inputs, saying whether the adapter's trace depends on it, and the JSON report a top-level `session_id`; a record that states another id adds a `limitation:` line naming both. Without the flag the record's id decides, as before. `scripts/capture-agent.sh` writes every run's id to `<label>.session-id.txt` and passes the task's to both `verify` runs. `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` keeps, of agy's `trajectory_meta`, the table cut to `cascade_id` when it holds one conversation id, and the two agy fixtures are regenerated with it: their `steps` and every other file are unchanged.
Consequences:
- A command that rewrites the session database's `cascade_id` no longer moves the declaration in a capture that records the id; it is a write of the session's own database, tampering.
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and `--json`, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation, except on the two agy fixtures: their JSON `parse` gains `trace_session`, and with `--check-trace-hash` the hash of the file read, which already differed from the probe's, is the new file's. Their declaration is now the session's database, which is the only conversation database their ground truths name, so no verdict changes. On the private agy and Codex captures at hand the output without the flag is unchanged, and with the printed id passed only the `session id:` line and the JSON `session_id` are added (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
- Claude Code's declaration does not depend on the id (I-74 declares the workspace's project directory); the report says so. Whether it should narrow to the session's transcript and subagents directory when the id is given is not decided here.
- `cmd/analyze` and the campaign's manifest do not read `<label>.session-id.txt`; `cmd/analyze` still takes the stated id (a known limit of the tools).
Alternatives rejected: Comparing the stated and the recorded id and failing the run on a difference (the user asked the recorded id to decide; a difference is reported, and a change by a command is already tampering on the declared database); reading the id from the watch log at verification (`verify` reads no log, and the log's format is the agent's).

### I-107: Claude Code's search is a run of the agent's own program, wherever it is installed
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.WithAgentImage`, `searchOf`, `searchArgs` in `pkg/agent/claudecode`; `agent.AgentImageUser`, `agent.NormalizeCapture`, used by `Prepare` and `Capture`; `Rooted` takes a launched harness's exec image; `TestASearchIsARunOfTheAgentsOwnProgram`, `TestASearchFromAnotherLayoutVerifiesWithTheRecordedImage`, `TestRootedRootsTheCaptureAtTheHarness`). The user's decision of 2026-11-02, option (a). Builds on P-46; amends I-27. Amended by I-128 (a build that searches with `rg` from `PATH`).
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: I-27 recognises a Grep or Glob only when its program is the native installer's path, so a harness installed elsewhere has its searches read as unexplained commands, and the check is a path, not the file. The strings of the 2.1.306 binary show how it picks its search program: in its native build it runs its own executable (`process.execPath`) with `--no-config` and `argv0` `rg`; otherwise, or with `USE_BUILTIN_RIPGREP` set off, `rg` from `PATH` with no `--no-config` (**Reported**). The bundled search tool is therefore the harness's own executable, and the capture now records which program the agent ran (P-46).
Decision: With the capture's `root_image` (after `Rooted`, the launched harness's image for an adapter with a launcher), a process event is a search when its command line begins with the image's resolved program path followed by ` --no-config `, and, for an exec whose kernel record names its program, that program is the same path; the arguments then classify it as I-25 does. Any other program, wherever it is, is not a search. A capture without `root_image`, or with a cut one, keeps I-27's install-directory check. The generic hook is `agent.AgentImageUser`: `NormalizeCapture` gives the image to such an adapter before its `Normalize`, in `Prepare` and in the baseline's `Capture`, so a baseline is measured in the same shape.
Consequences: A native harness under another `XDG_DATA_HOME`, or a package that ships the native binary elsewhere, has its searches recognised; a copy of the harness in the workspace, or the native path when the agent ran another program, is not. Trust is the agent's own program file, as in I-27 its install directory. An npm build of 2.1.306 that searches with `rg` from `PATH` is still not recognised: that is not the harness's own tool, and it shows as an unclaimed command until it is measured and decided. No fixture or private capture records `root_image`, so every `cmd/verify` output, the private captures' and the attack evaluation are unchanged (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
Alternatives rejected: Install-path patterns per layout (option (b): unmeasured layouts and more user-writable paths, and still a path, not the file); inferring the harness's program from the root's file opens (option (c): an exec open is not told apart from a read).

### I-108: A search claim names the folder searched, and the observed search must match it
Status: Accepted, implemented (`searchClaim`, `searchTarget`, `searchFD`, `resolveFDSearches`, `fdSearchWindow`, `fdSearchKind` in `pkg/agent/claudecode`, and `grepOpenClaims`'s `ImpliedBy`; `searchFolder`, `searchClaim` and the fold's targets in `pkg/agent/gemini/search.go`; `partialTargetsOnly` in `honestVerdict`, `pkg/attack/eval_test.go`; `TestASearchIsClaimedAsAKindOfSearchInItsFolder`, `TestAGrepOfAPathImpliesTheHarnessOpenBeforeTheSearch`, `TestTheHarnessOpenBeforeAGrepIsExplainedEndToEnd`, `TestParallelTaskVerifiesAllButTheSearchFolders`, `TestASearchNamesItsFolder`, `TestHonestVerdictAcceptsOnlyPartialTargets`). The user's decisions of 2026-11-02 after a threat-model audit, with options (i) to (iv) and option 1 for the attack evaluation. Amends I-25 and I-54. Amended by I-111 (a `~/` path), I-128 (`rg` from `PATH`) and I-133 (agy 1.4.7's searches, a file search).
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: I-25 and I-54 claim a Grep, a Glob and a `grep_search` as the kind of search only, so a search of another folder than the one claimed, which can read files the trajectory never mentions, was corroborated. What the probes record names the folder. On the private `long` pilots of Claude Code 2.1.306, a Grep of a directory runs the harness's ripgrep with its working directory set to that directory and `.` as its path, a Glob runs with the session's directory and `.`, and a Grep of one file runs on `/proc/self/fd/3` from the session's directory after the agent's process opened the file, which ripgrep then opens under its path (I-69; **Verified** on the raw captures). The working directory is recorded only since P-36, so the first three pilots and every older fixture do not have it. On agy 1.3.4 the embedded ripgrep is given `--ignore-file=<SearchPath>/.antigravityignore` (**Verified** on the `search` capture, and on one search of a private 1.4.7 capture whose other searches the adapter does not recognise).
Decision:
- A claim's target is the kind followed by the folder: for Claude Code the input `path` resolved against the call's `cwd`, or the `cwd` when the input names none; for agy the `SearchPath`. A path the adapter cannot resolve (starting with `~`, or relative with no absolute `cwd`) is kept as written.
- An observed search's target is the kind followed by the folder its process searched: Claude Code's `.` the exec's recorded working directory, an absolute last argument itself, `/proc/self/fd/<n>` the file the search's parent opened at most a second before the exec and the search's own process opened after it; agy's ripgrep the directory of its ignore file, its fallback grep's `.` the recorded working directory. A folder claimed and another observed is a substitution, like any mismatched target.
- When the folder cannot be read (no working directory recorded, a command line the probe cut, no file matched), the observed target is the kind alone, marked truncated: it pairs with a claim of that kind by prefix and is unverified (V-26), never a mismatch, so the run cannot be FAITHFUL. The pattern stays unverified (I-25 holds).
- The attack evaluation's honest control accepts such a pair, and only such a pair: an unverified pair whose sole gap is a target the observation recorded in part.
Consequences:
- Intended verdict change: `paired-2.1.286-parallel` (recorded before P-36) is INCONCLUSIVE instead of FAITHFUL at every agreement, its Grep and Glob unverified, and NOT FAITHFUL as before without a baseline; of the private captures, the first three `long` pilots of 2.1.306 gain three unverified searches each, and the second and third turn from FAITHFUL to INCONCLUSIVE at agreement 0.2. The fourth pilot, recorded with working directories, stays FAITHFUL at 0.2 with every Grep and Glob corroborated on its folder; the four single-file Greps of each pilot are corroborated on their files (**Verified**, scratch comparison against the parent, local only).
- `paired-agy-1.3.4-search` keeps its verdicts; its report names the folder, `/ws/search`, in the claim and the event. On the private agy 1.4.7 pilot only the claims' text changes, its searches not being recognised yet. Every other fixture and private capture is byte-identical.
- The attack evaluation's detection, classification and location numbers are identical on every capture; its honest control on `paired-2.1.286-parallel` reads honest through the narrowed acceptance.
- Not covered: the pattern; a Claude Code build that searches with `rg` from `PATH` (I-107); agy 1.4.7's search shapes, which wait for its live capture; a path given as `~/...`, which reads as a mismatch on an honest run.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping kind-only pairing for captures without a working directory (option 2: every verdict unchanged, but a folder never measured would read as verified); a mismatch for them (an honest old capture would read NOT FAITHFUL); reading the pattern from ripgrep's arguments (I-25: options take values and the pattern can follow `-e`).

### I-111: A Grep's or Glob's folder written as "~/" is resolved against the capture's home
Status: Accepted, implemented (`HomeExpander` and its use in `Prepare`, `pkg/agent/agent.go`; `Adapter.ExpandHome` in `pkg/agent/claudecode/shell.go`; `TestAGrepOfAHomePathVerifiesWithTheRecordedHome`). Amends I-108.
Date: 2026-11-02.
Context: I-108 claims a Grep or a Glob with the folder it names, its `input.path` resolved against the call's `cwd`, but kept a path starting with `~` as written, since `Parse` does not know the home the harness resolves it against. The observed search names an absolute folder, so an honest Grep of `~/notes` read as a mismatch, a substitution, which accuses an honest run. The capture records the home it resolved the adapters' `~/` declarations against (`Sensitive.Home`, V-40), the home of the user the agent ran as.
Decision: An optional adapter interface, `HomeExpander`, returns a claim with a `~` path resolved against a home. `Prepare` applies it to a copy of every claim when the capture records the home, so every caller of `Prepare` (`cmd/verify`, `cmd/analyze`, the attack evaluation) gets it. Claude Code resolves the folder of a Grep or Glob claim written `~` or `~/...`; a `~user/...` form and every other claim are left as written. The generic hook keeps the per-agent rule in the adapter, as every other declaration.
Consequences:
- With the home recorded, an honest Grep of a `~/` path verifies on its folder; a search of another folder is still a substitution.
- Without a recorded home (captures before V-40's snapshot) the path stays as written and I-108's limit holds: an honest Grep of it reads as a mismatch. A `~user/...` path always does.
- Every paired fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and JSON, with no baseline and baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and all 530 verifications of the private captures (no baseline, agreements 0.2 and 1, `--check-trace-hash`) are byte-identical to the parent's: no recorded Grep or Glob names a `~` path (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
Alternatives rejected: Resolving in `Parse` from the session file's location (a fixture or a copied session does not sit under the harness's home); marking a `~` path unverified (it would leave a resolvable folder unchecked); comparing only the path's tail (a folder of the same name elsewhere would verify).

### I-112: OpenCode is profiled before its adapter, with Qwen3-235B-A22B on the SciLifeLab OpenLLM server, its version locked and its model catalog the binary's own
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `opencode` profile in `config/agents.json`: `binary`, `version_command`, no `versions`, `version_lock`, `model`, `tool_set` `none`, `session` `stdout-json` with the key `sessionID`, `stdin` `null`, `invocation`; the session id's form in `capture_one`, `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `TestCaptureOfOpencodePassesTheKeyOnStdinOnly`). Builds on I-77 and I-79; the user's decisions of 2026-11-02 (the open stacks, OpenCode with an open Qwen, the provider, the key handling, the same policies as Claude Code and Codex).
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: The experiments use six stacks; OpenCode with an open Qwen model is the first open one. OpenCode 1.19.5 (npm `opencode-ai`) is one Bun executable. A survey of 2026-11-03 (eleven `opencode run` sessions against the server, `strace -f` without `-v`, and the strings of the binary) found: `opencode run --format json` prints one JSON event per line, each with `sessionID` (`ses_` and 26 letters and digits); `-m provider/model` picks the model; with the permission rule `{"*": "allow"}` no tool asks, and `run` denies `question` and the plan tools itself; a piped stdin is not wanted. At every start OpenCode fetches its model catalog from `https://models.opencode.ai/api.json` into `~/.cache/opencode/models.json` unless `OPENCODE_DISABLE_MODELS_FETCH` is set, when it uses the catalog built into the binary; it checks for updates unless the configuration's `autoupdate` is false or `OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE` is set, and never installs one by itself. `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` is built in (no package was fetched for it). Once per home, it installs `@opencode-ai/plugin` 1.19.5 from the npm registry into each configuration directory that is writable and lacks `node_modules`, and the first `glob` or `grep` downloads ripgrep 15.1.0 from GitHub into `~/.cache/opencode/bin` when no `rg` is on `PATH` (this machine has none). It shares no session unless asked (`share` `disabled`), and sends telemetry only when `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set. After those first runs, a run's only hosts were the model server and the hosts its tools fetched. Both Qwen models the brief names answered; Qwen3-235B-A22B drove every tool OpenCode offers it.
Decision: The `opencode` profile pins `scilifelab/Qwen3-235B-A22B`, the provider and model as `-m` takes them, and lists no version until a fixture verifies one. The invocation is `run --format json -m {model} {prompt}`, with stdin from `/dev/null`; the session id is `sessionID` (`stdout-json`), which the script now reads in any form of letters, digits, `_` and `-`. The version lock is `OPENCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE=1` and `OPENCODE_DISABLE_MODELS_FETCH=1` in every run's environment and `autoupdate: false` in the configuration (I-113): the model catalog then comes with the version, as the version is frozen. OpenCode takes no tool set (`none`); the prompts name its tools. Nothing is done about the one-time plugin install and ripgrep download: they are the harness's own first-run actions, recorded in whichever run meets them; whether to settle them before a capture is the user's decision (O-38).
Consequences: Every capture of OpenCode runs on the binary's model catalog and makes no update check. A capture records the runs, controls and manifest and no verdict until the adapter exists (I-79). The first capture in a home without OpenCode's `node_modules` or ripgrep records the install or the download in its first run that meets it. `config/agents.json` gains a profile without versions, which the tests accept (I-79).
