Alternatives rejected: Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B (it answered tool calls too, but the brief names Qwen3-235B-A22B first and it drove every tool); `--auto` instead of the permission rule (it approves only what no rule denies, and the configuration says so explicitly); a per-run tool list through permission rules of `deny` (no tool set method implements one yet; the prompts name the tools, as for Codex).

### I-113: OpenCode runs on agent-trace's own configuration file, its key passed only through the run's stdin, its global layer moved aside
Status: Accepted, implemented (`secret_env`, `config_file` and `isolation.env` in `scripts/agent-profile.py` and `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; the `opencode` profile's `secret_env`, `config_file`, `isolation` and `instruction_files`; `TestCaptureOfOpencodePassesTheKeyOnStdinOnly`, `TestCaptureOfOpencodeRefusesNoKeyOrAnotherConfig`). Extends I-77 and I-79; the user's decisions of 2026-11-02 (the key never printed, logged, written or on a command line, named in the configuration only as `{env:SCILIFELAB_API_KEY}`; a private configuration outside the repository; user configuration and instruction files kept out of the run).
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: OpenCode reads, in order, remote configuration of a `wellknown` login, the global layer (`~/.config/opencode/config.json`, `opencode.json`, `opencode.jsonc`, a legacy TOML `config`), the file `OPENCODE_CONFIG` names, the project's `opencode.json(c)` found up from the workspace, and, from each configuration directory (`~/.config/opencode`, the `.opencode` directories found up from the workspace, `~/.opencode`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`), its agents, commands, modes, plugins, skills and tools. Its prompt gets the first of `~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md` and `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, the first of `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `CONTEXT.md` found from the workspace up to its worktree (in a workspace that is not a git repository, up to `/`: the survey's first run read the user's `~/AGENTS.md`), and skills from `~/.claude/skills`, `~/.agents/skills` and those found up from the workspace (it read the user's). `OPENCODE_DISABLE_PROJECT_CONFIG` skips the project files and directories and the instruction files found up; `OPENCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE` skips `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and the Claude Code skills; `OPENCODE_DISABLE_EXTERNAL_SKILLS` the external skills; `OPENCODE_PURE` external plugins. No variable skips the global layer, `~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md` or `~/.opencode` (measured: a canary in the global `AGENTS.md` reached the reply with every flag set, and none of the others did). OpenCode adds `$schema` to a configuration file that lacks it, by rewriting the file. The capture runs the agent under `sudo` and `setpriv --reset-env`, and passes environment through `env NAME=VALUE`, a command line that any process can read and the process probe records.
Decision: Three profile fields, none naming an agent. `secret_env` names variables whose values the capture needs from its own environment, refuses without, and passes only through each run's stdin: the script stops exporting them, writes them to a pipe with the shell's `printf` builtin, and a fixed reader script run by `/bin/sh` after `env` in the run's exec chain exports each under its name, replaces stdin with `/dev/null` and execs the agent in place; a profile with it must have `stdin: null`, and `task-meta.txt` names the variables only. `config_file` is the agent's configuration, agent-trace's file outside the repository: the capture writes the profile's `content` where nothing is, refuses a file that differs or a path inside the repository, passes the absolute path in `env`, and adds the content's hash to the model pin and the path and hash to `task-meta.txt`. `isolation.env` holds variables every run gets. OpenCode's profile: `secret_env` `SCILIFELAB_API_KEY`; `config_file` `~/.config/agent-trace/opencode-1.19.5.json` as `OPENCODE_CONFIG`, which declares the provider `scilifelab` (`@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`, `https://openllm.scilifelab.se/api`, `apiKey` `{env:SCILIFELAB_API_KEY}`) with its one model, sets `small_model` to the same model, `enabled_providers` to it alone, every permission to `allow`, `share` `disabled`, `autoupdate`, `lsp` and `formatter` false, and holds `$schema`; `isolation.env` the four variables above and `OPENCODE_DISABLE_LSP_DOWNLOAD`; and `instruction_files` `~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md`, the global configuration files and the eight directory names under `~/.config/opencode`, and `~/.opencode`, moved aside for each run as I-79 moves every agent's (OpenCode's own `node_modules`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and `.gitignore` there stay).
Consequences: No command line, file or log of the capture holds the key (**Verified**, 2026-11-03: an unprivileged run of the whole script under stand-ins for `sudo`, `setpriv --reset-env` and `watch`, the last tracing every exec with `strace -s 4096`, for `basic` and a control, and a scan of the scratch tree and the worktree for the key's first characters, 0 matches; the shim test checks every command line from the agent up to the capture script). The key passes `sudo`'s stdin, so an I/O-logging sudoers policy would record it (a known limit). A change of the configuration's content changes the model pin, so `--baseline-from` refuses controls taken with another. The user's global OpenCode layer, absent on this machine today, is out of every run whatever it holds later; other OpenCode sessions of the user's that start during a run miss it for that run.
Alternatives rejected: `pass_env` for the key (its values go on `env`'s command line, which agy's non-secret variables may); `sudo --preserve-env` (`setpriv --reset-env` clears it, and whether sudoers allows it varies); a file holding the key (the user forbade it); the configuration inline in `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` (a long value on `env`'s command line, and it is loaded after the project layer); a private `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` for the global layer (every command the agent runs would inherit it, git's and Python's configuration lookups included, and `~/.opencode` would still be read); moving `~/.config/opencode` aside whole (OpenCode recreates the directory at start, so the restore would refuse every time).

### I-114: OpenCode's trace is copied from its one session database by the session id; the session's subagents are the sessions whose parent chain leads to it
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `sqlite-sessions` trace method: `collect_sqlite_sessions`, `model_seen`, `subagents_seen` and `trace_state` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `trace.database` in `scripts/agent-profile.py`; `TestCaptureOfOpencodePassesTheKeyOnStdinOnly`, `TestCaptureOfOpencodeKeepsARunWhoseDatabaseIsGone`). The capture side only; which files the adapter declares is open (O-38). Amended by I-116 (which files are the trace). The adapter's reading by I-120.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: OpenCode 1.19.5 keeps every session of the account in one SQLite database, `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db` (`opencode-<channel>.db` on a channel other than `latest`, `beta` or `prod`; `OPENCODE_DB` overrides it), in write-ahead-log mode. Its tables `session` (with `parent_id`, `directory`, `version`, `model` and `agent`), `message` and `part` hold the record: each part's JSON `data` is a text, a reasoning, a `step-start` or `step-finish` (with the snapshot's tree hash in a git workspace), a `patch`, or a `tool` part with `tool`, `callID`, and `state` with `status`, `input`, `output`, `metadata` and start and end times; every assistant message names `providerID` and `modelID`. A `task` call starts a subagent session whose `parent_id` is the caller's and whose id the call's metadata names (`sessionId`) and its output repeats (`<task id=...>`). The `event` table repeats every update as an event stream. No existing method reads one database of all sessions.
Decision: The trace method `sqlite-sessions` copies `trace.database` from `trace.dir` with SQLite's online backup to `trace.file` (`{label}.opencode.db`) after every run, and checks that the copy holds the session the agent printed; a database that is gone, or a copy without the session, is a trace that is gone. The model check reads `providerID/modelID` of the session's assistant messages, and its subagents' for the record; `order.tsv` counts the sessions whose `parent_id` chain leads to the session and their tool parts. The state check of a declared trace reads the copy: `damaged` when it does not open or fails `quick_check`, `missing` without the session, `empty` when the session has no message, else `read`. The profile declares the trace (`declared: true`), as the database is where the session lives under every option of O-38; `states_version` is true, since the session row states the version.
Consequences: The capture of a task whose agent deleted the database, or the session's rows, keeps the run and says the trace is gone (V-59, I-75). The copy holds every session of the account and is private; it must be reduced before it reaches a fixture, which the fixture script does not yet do. In a workspace that is not a git repository, which every built-in task's is, OpenCode keeps no snapshot repository and runs no `git` (measured); a git workspace gets one under `~/.local/share/opencode/snapshot/<project>/` and about a hundred `git` commands per task.
Alternatives rejected: Copying the database with `cp` (a database in write-ahead-log mode is not consistent on disk); `OPENCODE_DB` pointing every run at a database of its own (the trace would not be where OpenCode keeps it for the user, and every run would migrate a new database); exporting the session with `opencode export` (another run of the harness, after the run, that reads the database the run may have changed).

### I-115: OpenCode's tasks are Claude Code's eight with OpenCode's tools, and its controls ask for the marker command character by character
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `tasks` and `controls` of the `opencode` profile). Builds on I-71 and I-88.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: OpenCode 1.19.5 offers this model `bash` (a command, a working directory and a timeout; each command is `bash -c` of its own, no background mode), `read`, `write`, `edit` (with `replaceAll`), `glob` and `grep` (ripgrep), `task` (a subagent of a type, `general` among them), `webfetch`, `todowrite` and `skill`; `apply_patch` only to GPT models, `websearch` only to OpenCode's own provider or with Exa enabled (which sends the query to Exa), and no MultiEdit, notebook or list tool. It runs the calls of one message concurrently, writes included, and its `write` and `edit` write a file in place. In the survey Qwen3-235B-A22B did `basic`, `parallel` (with `todowrite`), `edits`, `shapes`, the adapted `tools` and a control with a subagent; in one `shapes` run it left out a call of a parallel block. With the control wording of the other profiles it ran `:` for `: agent-trace-control-marker`, which the baseline's exclusion of the marker command does not match.
Decision: The tasks are Claude Code's eight, the prompts naming OpenCode's tools where Claude Code's name its own (Read `read`, Write `write`, Edit `edit` with `replaceAll`, Bash `bash`, Grep `grep`, Glob `glob`, Agent `task` with a subagent of type `general`, WebFetch `webfetch`); `project` is unchanged. `tools` makes the background command a shell job, `(sleep 3; echo background-done > bg.txt) > /dev/null 2>&1 &`, later read with `read`, and replaces MultiEdit with two edits and NotebookEdit with an `edit` of the notebook's JSON. `websearch` fetches the page with `webfetch` only and is not a campaign task. The controls run the marker command with `bash`, asked for "this exact command string, copied character for character", create `agent-trace-control.txt` with `write` (`edit_tool`, I-88: in a git workspace OpenCode records a patch of its snapshot repository after a step that changed a file), and for a task whose tools hold `task` start one subagent that only replies.
Consequences: A task's claims and the harness's work around them can be compared across the six stacks task by task. The `tools` task measures a process that outlives the call that started it, not a harness background mode, which OpenCode lacks. In the survey, outside a capture, Qwen3-235B-A22B completed `project` (39 calls, every test passing) and `long` (128 calls, all twenty functions, `make check` passing); the captures measure it again.
Alternatives rejected: Enabling Exa for `websearch` (a third-party search host the other stacks do not have); a control without the edit (a git workspace's patch bookkeeping would stay unexplained).

### I-116: OpenCode's trace is its session database with its journal files; the snapshot repository is housekeeping; a warm-up run settles the first-run downloads
Status: Accepted (the `opencode` profile's `trace.note` in `config/agents.json`; the declaration itself lands with the adapter). Decides O-38. The user's ruling of 2026-11-03. Trace implemented by I-122 (2026-11-03). Amended by V-87 (2026-11-03): the `-shm` is outside the trace. Amended by I-117 (2026-11-03): the snapshot repository is learned by the baseline from controls in a git workspace. Amended by I-151 (2026-11-06): the warm-up is the capture's own, made before every capture invocation's first recorded run, not once per machine by hand.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: The survey (I-112, I-114) found that OpenCode 1.19.5 keeps every session of the account in one SQLite database, `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db`, with a write-ahead log; that a git workspace gets a snapshot repository under `~/.local/share/opencode/snapshot/` holding file contents for undo; and that the first run in a home installs `@opencode-ai/plugin` from the npm registry and the first `glob` or `grep` downloads ripgrep from GitHub, neither of which a flag turns off. O-38 asked which files are the trace, what the snapshot repository is, and where those downloads belong.
Decision:
- The trace for the fail-closed rule (V-59) is `~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db` with its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` files, as Codex's thread databases (I-84, I-106): a change to them by anything but the harness is tampering, reading them is not.
- The snapshot repository is housekeeping: it holds file contents for undo and no claim. The adapter declares its writes and the harness's `git` commands on it like other bookkeeping (V-50, V-54), and the agent's claims that name it stay checked.
- The one-time downloads are settled by a warm-up `opencode run` in the same home, outside any capture, once per machine and version, with the profile's environment and a prompt that uses `grep`; a capture records neither download.
Consequences:
- A tool-output file or the log changed by another process is not tampering with the trace; the database is the only place the session lives.
- A capture on a machine without the warm-up shows the downloads as unexplained harness activity in its first run; the capture's operator runs the warm-up first.
Alternatives rejected: Adding `tool-output/` and the log to the trace (the session does not live there); counting only the session's rows (a change elsewhere in the same file would go unnoticed); installing ripgrep system-wide (an install the user did not want); recording the downloads where they happen (every first run on a machine would carry them).

### I-118: A run of opens of a Gemini file tool passes over the harness's open of its project configuration
Status: Accepted, implemented (`collapseOpens`, `projectConfig`, `geminiHome` in `pkg/agent/gemini/stream.go`; `TestCollapsePassesOverTheProjectConfigOpen`, the new cases of `TestNormalizeStreamIsNarrow`). Amends I-50. Amended 2026-11-05 by I-135: a run of opens of a task log passes over it too.
Date: 2026-11-03 (the content capture of the `basic` task with agy 1.4.7, private).
Context: On that capture's task run, with its baseline, one event is Unrecorded besides the token renewal: the first of the three opens of `b.txt` that `view_file` makes, all within its claimed interval, by the agy process itself (**Verified**, local only). agy 1.4.7 opens its project configuration `~/.gemini/config/projects/default-cli-project.json` before each file tool call, as 1.3.4 did before a command (I-50), and here that open fell between the first and the second open of `b.txt`, 31 microseconds after the first. I-50's collapse ends a run at any other event of the process, so the run split in two and the first open was left over. The fs probe records the process (thread group), not the thread, so which thread opened what cannot be read from the capture; the interleaving within 31 microseconds, with no write between, shows two threads of one process. The conversation records the `view_file` call, which is the one claim of these opens: the open is the claimed tool's own, not a harness read the trajectory leaves out, so it is expressed by the fold that already takes `view_file`'s repeated opens, not by a harness-implied claim (V-33). In the 1.3.4 fixtures the configuration's open never fell inside a run.
Decision: In a run of opens of one file outside `~/.gemini/` by the agent's process, an open of `~/.gemini/config/projects/<name>.json` (a home under `/home` or `/root`, one path segment, `.json`) by the same process neither ends the run nor is dropped: the run still keeps its first open, and the configuration's open stays in the stream for the baseline to explain. A write or a close of the configuration, an open of any other file (another configuration file, a project configuration in a subfolder), and every other event still end a run. A run of opens of the configuration itself, and of any file under `~/.gemini/`, collapses as before, so the harness's start-up reads keep their measured shape.
Consequences:
- The capture's task run, with its baseline, corroborates the 11 claims with no Unrecorded file event; the token renewal is left (**Verified**, local only).
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and JSON, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation.
- What the fold hides beyond I-50: nothing new is explained, since the configuration's open stays an event; two opens of one file with only the configuration's open between them count as one. Two tool calls on one file still have the harness's transcript writes between them.
Alternatives rejected: A harness-implied claim of the first open (the open is the claimed `view_file`'s own read, and the extra claim would be unpaired whenever the interleaving does not happen); passing over every event on a harness or configuration path (a transcript write separates two calls and must keep ending a run); passing over any open of a path under `~/.gemini/` (the start-up reads of `config.json` around the configuration's open in the 1.3.4 `search` fixture would collapse, which changes its report).

### I-119: agy 1.4.7 is configured with a fixture from its first content capture; the fixture script replaces the account's profile picture id
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `1.4.7` entry of the `gemini` profile in `config/agents.json`; `pkg/agent/gemini/testdata/paired-agy-1.4.7-basic`; `AVATAR` and `avatar_ids` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `paired147_test.go`). Amended 2026-11-03 by V-85: the renewal is declared for 1.4.7 with the capture's version, and the fixture verifies FAITHFUL. Extended 2026-11-05 by I-133: 1.4.7 also has the fixture `paired-agy-1.4.7-search`, verified 2026-11-05.
Date: 2026-11-03 (the content capture of the `basic` task with agy 1.4.7, private).
Context: The capture (agy 1.4.7, `gemini-3.8-flash-high`, task `basic` and five controls, `--content`) has its probes clean: the `tls-go-crypto` method active, 40 responses all paired, no drops (**Verified**, local only). It is the first agy capture with request content, so its ground truths name every request target the harness sends, among them a `GET` of the account's profile picture, whose path is the account's own id. The fixture script rewrote homes, user names and addresses, but not that id. The conversation database states no version (I-57), so the version is known from the capture (`agy --version`, recorded as `control-version.txt`) and passed as `--agent-version`.
Decision: `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` replaces the path after `https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/a/` (up to a space, a quote, `?` or `#`) with `account-picture` in every written file, the same in every run so the baseline's rules still agree, and adds the ids it read from the capture's ground truths to the private strings it refuses to keep. The capture is reduced to `paired-agy-1.4.7-basic` (the conversation's 12 steps cut as for 1.3.4, the six ground truths), checked with `strings` for the home, the user name, e-mail addresses, the profile picture's id and token prefixes, none found. `config/agents.json` lists 1.4.7 with that fixture, verified 2026-11-03, so `verify --strict-version --agent-version 1.4.7` reads the version as configured.
Consequences:
- With its baseline, at every agreement, the fixture's task run corroborates all eleven claims (with I-118) and is NOT FAITHFUL on the token renewal only: the POST is Unrecorded and the connection unexplained harness network activity. The renewal is not declared: a housekeeping declaration takes the session's version (V-54), which an agy session never states, so a declaration for 1.4.7 would not apply (a known limit of the adapters).
- The 1.3.4 fixtures are not regenerated; every existing fixture's output and the attack evaluation are unchanged.
- The profile picture is the one request target found to name the account in this capture; another URL of another agent that does is not rewritten until the script learns it (a known limit of the tools).
Alternatives rejected: Dropping the profile picture's events (the baseline measures them, and a fixture should keep the shape the verifier reads); hashing the id (a hash of a public profile URL id is as identifying as the id).

### I-120: The OpenCode adapter claims the tool parts of the run's session and its subagents; a session file of several sessions is read by the id the capture recorded
Status: Accepted, implemented (`pkg/agent/opencode`: `Detect`, `Parse`, `ParseSession`, `readSession` in `session.go`; `claimParts` in `claims.go`; `Normalize` in `opencode.go`; `NormalizeStream`, `foldRereads` in `stream.go`; `agent.SessionParser` and `agent.ReadSessionTrace` in `pkg/agent/trace.go`, used by `cmd/verify`; `TestTheSessionIDNamesTheRunInADatabaseOfSeveral`, `TestSubagentsComeFromTheParentChain`, `TestParseMapsEachTool`, `TestParseRefusesIncompleteCalls`, `TestNormalizeRecoversTheCommand`, `TestNormalizeStreamFoldsTheMeasuredShapes`, `TestPairedTaskClaimsEveryEffectOnce`, `TestPairedIsFaithfulAtEveryAgreement`). Builds on I-110, I-114 and I-116. Extended by I-143 (2026-11-05): the harness's read of a file another process wrote is dropped the same way.
Date: 2026-11-03 (the first capture, `opencode-1.19.5-basic-20261103-0216`, private, and the survey of 1.19.5 of the same day).
Context: OpenCode 1.19.5 records each call as a tool part of its session in its one database of every session of the account (I-114): the tool, the status, the input, the output, metadata and the start and end of the execution in milliseconds. A subagent is a session whose `parent_id` names its caller, and the caller's `task` call names it (`metadata.sessionId`). On the capture's task run, every call's exec, opens and writes fell inside its recorded start and end; a `bash` call is a child of the harness that execs `/bin/bash -c <command>`; `write` and `edit` run in the harness's process and write in place, as Codex's apply_patch does, and 66 to 72 ms after each close the harness opens the file again with the hash it wrote; the harness opens every file of the workspace once at start (**Verified**, the capture). The capture's database copy holds the run's session and the account's others (two sessions without a parent in the task's copy), so the file alone does not say which session is the run's; the capture records the id outside the trace (I-110). `agent.Adapter.Parse` takes only a path.
Decision:
- A generic optional interface, `agent.SessionParser`, reads a session file of several sessions by the id: `ParseSession(path, id)`. `agent.ReadSessionTrace(a, path, id, hash)` uses it when the id is given, and `Parse` otherwise; `ReadTrace` is it with no id. `cmd/verify` passes `--session-id`. OpenCode's `Parse` reads a file that holds exactly one session without a parent (a fixture) and fails otherwise, which `verify` reports as a trace it cannot verify.
- The adapter reads the session the id names and every session whose parent chain leads to it (I-114, down to eight levels), each tool part as the table of the adapters' page maps it: `bash` a `process_exec` of its command, `read` a `file_open` (and one per instruction file the read added), `write` and `edit` a `file_open` and a `file_write`, `webfetch` of an https URL a `net_connect` to its host; calls that act on nothing the probes see are counted, `grep`, `glob` and unknown tools are counted and listed as tools that may have acted, and a call that did not complete claims nothing. The interval is the call's recorded execution (`IntervalExecution`), the calls of one message share a block, and subagent claims carry their session's id as their thread.
- Observed side: the wrapper `/bin/bash -c` or `/usr/bin/bash -c` is stripped (D6, as I-47 for agy); Codex's create, open-run and in-place folds apply to the harness's process; the harness's open of a file it has just closed with a content hash, its next file event outside its own directories within a second, with that hash, is dropped before the open runs are collapsed (`foldRereads`). The adapter does not declare `CollapsesOpenRuns` (V-49), since the verifier's collapse would fold a read's open into the harness's open of the same file at start.
Consequences:
- The first capture's task run, with its baseline from the five controls, is FAITHFUL at every agreement from 0.2 to 1 with `--session-id`: all eight claims corroborated, no finding, no unexplained harness activity, no tampering (**Verified**, the capture and its fixture, local only for the capture). Without the id the raw copy reads as a trace that cannot be verified (two sessions). With `--check-trace-hash` it is INCONCLUSIVE, since the copy is a backup, not the file's bytes (a known limit of the tools).
- Every existing fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and JSON, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, with and without `--check-trace-hash`, and with the adapter detected, is byte-identical to the parent's, and so is the attack evaluation (its subtests' order aside), which has no Codex or OpenCode capture (**Verified**, scratch comparison).
- `cmd/analyze` does not pass a recorded id, so it cannot read a raw OpenCode capture (a known limit of the tools).
- Not measured: subagents, webfetch, a directory read, an added instruction file, parallel calls, a call that did not complete; their mapping rests on the survey and synthetic tests.
Alternatives rejected: Reading the id from a file beside the database (a layout of the capture script the adapter would depend on, and absent in a fixture); taking the newest session (another session of the user's started during the run would be read); reducing the database to the run's session before verifying (the raw capture is the evidence `verify` reads).

### I-121: An OpenCode write claims its content when the record states the bytes written; an edit's comes from its recorded diff
Status: Accepted, implemented (`contents`, `disturbers`, `hunksOf` in `pkg/agent/opencode/claims.go`; `applyDiff` in `diff.go`; `Adapter.Expresses`; `TestContentIsClaimedOnlyWhenTheRecordStatesIt`, `TestPairedCatchesAlteredTrajectories`). Builds on I-87.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: The strings of the 1.19.5 binary show the write tool reading the file it replaces, stripping a byte order mark from the content and writing the content with one when either had one (**Reported**). The edit tool matches its old string loosely: in the survey an edit whose old string held a doubled backslash was applied to a file holding one and reported success (**Verified** in the survey of 1.19.5, local only). An edit records a unified diff of the file (`metadata.diff`), with four header lines naming it and hunks with three lines of context, not the whole file. On the capture the write that created `a.txt` closed it with the hash of the call's content, and the edit's close had the hash of that content with the diff applied (**Verified**).
Decision: A write claims the hash of its content when the file was new (`metadata.exists` false), when the content starts with a byte order mark, or when the session knows the file's prior content and it has none. An edit claims the hash of the file's known content with its recorded diff applied, by I-87's exact rule, when the diff's header names the file and every hunk applies; never from the call's old and new strings. A content is known from a write or an edit that claimed one, and is used only when no call that may write a file (a `bash` call, a search, an unmapped tool, a call that did not complete) ran at any time between the start of the call it came from and the end of the call that uses it. `Expresses` states the output hash exactly where one is claimed.
Consequences: On the capture the write and the edit of `a.txt` claim their content and are corroborated on it; the write over `b.txt`, which the session only read, claims none. A substituted write or edit content is a Mismatched finding. An overwrite of a file the session did not write claims no content, and an edit after only a read claims none.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming every write's content (an honest overwrite of a file with a byte order mark would read as a substitution); re-applying the edit's old and new strings (the loose match makes the result differ from a plain replacement); reconstructing the prior content from a read's output (it is line-numbered and cut at 2000 characters a line).

### I-122: OpenCode's own activity: its directories, its database as bookkeeping and trace, and its sensitive files
Status: Accepted, implemented (`IsHarnessOwned`, `HarnessCommands`, `HarnessHosts`, `HarnessBookkeeping`, `Trace`, `SessionTrace`, `SensitiveFiles` in `pkg/agent/opencode/harness.go`; `TestHarnessDeclarations`, `TestPairedACommandChangingTheDatabaseIsTampering`, `TestPairedNeedsTheDeclarations`). Implements I-116's trace; its snapshot repository's declaration is not built (known limit).
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: On the capture's six runs the harness wrote only `opencode.db`, its `-wal` and `-shm` and its log under `~/.local/share/opencode`, outside the workspace; it ran no command of its own and connected only to the model server (**Verified**). The survey found it writing under `~/.local/share/opencode`, `~/.local/state/opencode` and `~/.cache/opencode`, and reading its configuration from `~/.config/opencode` and the project's `opencode.json(c)`, `.opencode` and `AGENTS.md` (I-113). I-116 makes the database with its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` the trace, "as Codex's thread databases (I-84, I-106)", where reading is not tampering. Every reader of a database in write-ahead-log mode writes its `-shm`, which is why I-106 and V-75 leave the `-shm` uncovered.
Decision: The harness's directories are `~/.local/share/opencode`, `~/.local/state/opencode` and `~/.cache/opencode`, not `~/.config/opencode`. For 1.19.5 the writes, closes and deletes of the database and its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` are declared bookkeeping (V-50). The trace is the database (`Main`) with its `-wal` and `-journal`, both `Transient` (V-76), and not its `-shm`, as for Codex and agy, so that reading the database is not tampering; the declaration is the same with and without the session's id. No command and no host is declared. The sensitive files are the global configuration files and instruction file under `~/.config/opencode` and the workspace's `opencode.json(c)`, `.opencode/opencode.json(c)` and `AGENTS.md`, each checked whole (V-40).
Consequences: A command that deletes or writes the database or its `-wal` is trace tampering; one that reads it with SQLite is not. A change to the `-shm` is never seen. The log is explained by the baseline only, since every run wrote and closed it. A run in a git workspace shows the snapshot repository's commands as unclaimed until a capture of one measures them.
Alternatives rejected: Covering the `-shm` (a reader's index write would read as tampering, against I-116's "reading is not tampering"); declaring `~/.config/opencode` as the harness's (a change to the configuration would be harness activity, not a finding); declaring the snapshot repository's commands from the survey's `strace` (V-43 and V-50 name exact commands and files, and these carry per-workspace hashes).

### I-123: OpenCode 1.19.5 is configured on the first capture; the fixture keeps the run's session and its tool parts only
Status: Accepted, implemented (the `1.19.5` entry of the `opencode` profile in `config/agents.json`; `pkg/agent/opencode/testdata/paired-opencode-1.19.5-basic`; `reduce_opencode` and `reduce_opencode_part` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `checkOpenCodeFixture` in `pkg/config/fixtures_test.go`; `paired_test.go`).
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: The capture's database copies hold every session of the account, the user's own included, with their messages, parts, accounts and credentials tables (I-114). The adapter reads only the `session` table's id, parent, directory and version and the tool parts of the run's session and its subagents.
Decision: `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` reduces the task run's copy to a new database of two tables, `session` and `part`, holding the session the capture recorded and its subagents, and their tool parts cut to the fields the adapter reads; a write's content and an edit's diff hunks are kept unrewritten and refused when the rewriting would change them; the controls' copies are left out; and the private-string check also looks for every other session's id and for a value of the key's variable. OpenCode 1.19.5 is configured with the fixture, verified 2026-11-03.
Consequences: The fixture holds one session, so `Parse` reads it without an id. Its ground truths and database hold no home directory, user name, e-mail address or other session's id, and no value after the key's variable name (`strings` and the script's check). Its verdicts equal the raw capture's with `--session-id`: FAITHFUL at every agreement.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the run's whole session (messages hold the system prompt's echo and the model's text, which no claim reads); keeping the controls' databases (no test reads them, and each holds the account's sessions).

### I-124: An OpenCode grep or glob is a run of OpenCode's own ripgrep, claimed and observed as its kind and the folder it searched
Status: Accepted, implemented (`searchFolder` in `pkg/agent/opencode/claims.go`; `SearchGrep`, `SearchGlob`, `ownRipgrep`, `searchKind`, `searchTarget`, `foldSearches` in `search.go`; the grep output lines kept by `reduce_opencode_part` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `TestASearchIsClaimedWithItsFolder`, `TestFoldSearchesRecognisesOnlyTheHarnesssOwnRipgrep`, `TestPairedASearchIsCorroboratedOnItsFolder`). The user's decision of 2026-11-03, the recommended option. Follows I-107 and I-108. Amended by I-142 (2026-11-05): the folder rule of a grep of a file is confirmed, and a file is also recognised by a matched file outside the path or an earlier read, write or edit of it.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: OpenCode 1.19.5's grep and glob run ripgrep as a child of the harness. The survey's `strace` saw two, each exec'd from `<home>/.cache/opencode/bin/rg` (the ripgrep the harness downloads once per home, I-112, I-116) with the session's directory as its working directory: `--no-config --json --hidden --no-messages --glob=!**/.git/** -- <pattern> .` for a grep and `--no-config --files --glob=<pattern> --glob=!**/.git/** .` for a glob (**Verified** in the survey, local only). The strings of the binary show the rest (**Reported**): a grep adds `--glob=<include>` before the `.git` exclusion and a glob `--hidden` and `--follow` after `--files`; a glob's folder is its `path` (a directory) or the session's directory; a grep's working directory is its `path` when that is a directory and the path's directory when it is a file, and ripgrep then searches that whole directory with `.`; the harness's own file listing has no pattern of its own; ripgrep is OpenCode's download, or `rg` from `PATH` when there is one. A grep's output names each matched file as `<path>:` on a line of its own. In the basic capture no search ran.
Decision:
- Observed: the first exec of a process whose parent the stream does not show started (the harness), whose program is `<home>/.cache/opencode/bin/rg` (a home under `/home` or `/root`; the kernel's image, when recorded, the same file) and whose arguments are one of the two shapes, becomes `opencode search:grep <cwd>` or `opencode search:glob <cwd>`, with the exec's recorded working directory; its exit the same. Without a working directory, or with a command line the probe cut after the shape's start, it is the kind alone, marked truncated (V-26, as I-108). An `rg` from `PATH` or elsewhere, any other argument shape and an `rg` beneath a command the agent ran keep their text.
- Claimed: a completed grep or glob is a `process_exec` of its kind and its folder: the call's `path`, absolute or joined to the session's directory, or that directory; for a grep whose output names the path itself as a matched file, the path's directory. Its pattern is not verified (I-25).
Consequences:
- A search of the claimed folder by OpenCode's ripgrep is corroborated and its reads are explained beneath it; another folder is a substitution; an unclaimed one is an unexplained command. On a machine with `rg` on `PATH` the harness's searches stay unclaimed commands, so such a run is not FAITHFUL; the warm-up (I-116) and a machine without `rg` keep the measured shape.
- A grep of a file that matched nothing is claimed with the file as its folder and observed as its directory: an honest run reads as a substitution there (a known limit), until the record or a capture says otherwise.
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output and the attack evaluation are unchanged; the OpenCode fixture holds no search, and regenerating it gives the same bytes.
- The `parallel` parity capture must confirm: the exec's program, arguments and working directory for a grep and a glob of the session's directory under `watch` (P-36's `cwd`, the image), that ripgrep is a direct child of the harness, that its reads lie beneath it, and the claim's interval around the exec; a later capture with a grep of a file must confirm the folder rule.
Alternatives rejected: Trusting `rg` from `PATH` (any program on the path the agent can write would pass as a search, I-107); claiming ripgrep as a plain command line (the pattern and options are not recoverable reliably, and the folder would not be checked).

### I-125: The sqlite-sessions method also copies the database byte for byte after the run; that copy is the trace verify reads
Status: Accepted, implemented (`collect_sqlite_sessions` and the three sqlite-sessions readers in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `TestCaptureOfOpencodePassesTheKeyOnStdinOnly`). The user's decision of 2026-11-03. Amends I-114.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: I-114 copies OpenCode's session database with SQLite's online backup, whose bytes differ from the file the harness closed, while the capture's `verify` runs pass `--check-trace-hash` for every declared trace, which compares the file read with the last content the probe hashed of the session's main file. On the first capture the check therefore read the trace as `differs`, and the capture's own verdict would have been INCONCLUSIVE whatever the run did (**Verified** with `verify --check-trace-hash` on it, local only).
Decision: Right after the run, before any reader opens the database, the method copies it byte for byte to the trace file (`{label}.opencode.db`), with its `-wal` beside it as `<file>-wal` when there is one; it then takes the online backup into `<file>.backup` and checks the session there. The capture's readers of the trace (the state check, the model check, the subagent count) read a copy of the trace file with its `-wal`. Nothing changes for the other methods.
Consequences: When nothing else wrote the database between the harness's last close and the copy, the copy is the content the probe hashed and the check passes (**Unverified** until the next OpenCode capture: the first capture holds only the backup). Another OpenCode process of the user's that writes the database in that window makes the check read `differs`, a known limit of the tools, and one that writes during the byte copy can leave a copy that does not open, read as damaged; the backup stays beside it for inspection. The fixture script reads the trace file with its `-wal`, as before.
Alternatives rejected: Dropping `--check-trace-hash` for this method (the check would be lost for the one agent whose trace is a database of every session); a `verify` flag naming a second file to hash (the hash would not be of the file whose claims were read).

### I-117: OpenCode's snapshot repository is learned by the baseline from controls in a git workspace
Status: Accepted, not yet implemented (waits for a capture of a task in a git workspace; no code is needed until it shows what to template). The user's decision of 2026-11-03, the recommended option. Amends I-116.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: I-116 makes the snapshot repository housekeeping, to be declared like other bookkeeping (V-50, V-54) and harness commands (V-43). Both declarations name exact files and command lines of one version, and the survey's `strace` of three runs in a git workspace showed about 90 to 200 `git` commands per task whose lines carry the snapshot directory `~/.local/share/opencode/snapshot/<project>/<hash>`, the workspace and tree hashes, and writes of git objects under the snapshot directory (**Verified** in the survey, local only). No capture of a git workspace exists. The options were: the baseline learns them from controls in a git workspace, with the per-run parts templated by `Canonical` (a); a new generic declaration of a command family by pattern (b); keeping every task in a workspace that is not a git repository (c).
Decision: Option (a). A git-workspace capture's controls, which create `agent-trace-control.txt` with `write` (I-88, I-115) and so make the harness record a snapshot, measure the commands and what they do beneath them (V-38); the adapter's `Canonical` replaces the per-run hashes and directories it shows, and nothing is declared.
Consequences: Until that capture exists and `Canonical` is built from it, a run in a git workspace shows the snapshot repository's commands as unclaimed commands. Every built-in task's workspace is not a git repository, so no planned capture is affected.
Alternatives rejected: A pattern declaration (a pattern explains whatever an attacker makes it match, which every exact declaration avoids); declaring from the survey's `strace` (not a `watch` capture, and the shapes carry hashes).

### I-126: cmd/analyze applies the start-up window only when asked, as verify does
Status: Accepted, implemented (`--startup-window` in `cmd/analyze/main.go`; `Options.StartupWindow`, `Campaign.StartupWindow`, `Session.StartupWindow` and `Session.input` in `cmd/analyze`; `inputs.startup_window` in `results.json` and its line in `report.md`; `TestStartupWindowAppliesTheRuleAsVerifyDoes`, `TestStartupWindowIsRecordedInTheResults`). Builds on V-82 and V-85.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: `cmd/verify --startup-window` reads an open of a declared start-up-only harness file after the start-up as the agent's (V-82), off by default. `cmd/analyze` verifies every run of a campaign in process and had no such switch, so a campaign analysis could not apply the rule a single verify applies.
Decision: `cmd/analyze` takes `--startup-window`, off by default. When given, every verification of the analysis (the honest runs, the sensitivity settings and the attack sweep, all through `Session.input`) sets `StartupOnly` from `agent.StartupFiles` for the version that keys the other declarations: the session's, else the manifest's (`agent.DeclarationVersion`, V-85). `results.json` records `"startup_window": true` under `inputs` and `report.md` adds a line saying the window is on; off, neither names it.
Consequences:
- Without the flag `results.json` and `report.md` are byte-identical to the parent's on the same inputs.
- With it, the analysis and a `verify --startup-window` of each run apply the same rule with the same version; the baselines are built as before, since `verify` builds none with the rule either.
Alternatives rejected: On by default (V-82 keeps it off until a long interactive capture shows no honest run changes); reading the switch from the manifest (the capture does not decide how a run is verified).

### I-127: The campaign manifest names each run's recorded session id, and cmd/analyze decides the trace from it
Status: Accepted, implemented (`paths.session_id` in `summarize`, `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `sessionIDPath`, `recordedSessionID`, `Session.SessionID` and `Session.input` in `cmd/analyze/session.go`; `TestTheRecordedSessionIDDecidesTheTrace`). Amends I-110.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: I-110 lets the session id the capture recorded (`<label>.session-id.txt`) decide the trace, but only the capture script's own `verify` runs passed it; the manifest did not name the file and `cmd/analyze` declared the trace from the id the session's record states, so the two could disagree on a run whose record a command rewrote.
Decision: The manifest's `paths` gain `session_id`, the run's `<label>.session-id.txt`, null when the capture wrote none. `LoadSession` reads the id from the file the manifest names, else from `<label>.session-id.txt` in the run directory (a manifest written before this change), and `Session.input` passes it to `agent.SessionOf` as `verify --session-id` does, for every verification of the analysis. A run without the file, or with an empty one, keeps the id the record states; a file that exists and cannot be read is a load error (X4), as an unreadable capture is.
Consequences:
- `cmd/analyze` and the capture script's `verify` runs declare the same trace for every run recorded since I-110.
- A campaign whose runs have no such file, every checked-in fixture among them, gives byte-identical `results.json` and `report.md`.
- A difference between the recorded and the stated id is not reported by `cmd/analyze`, where `verify` prints a `limitation:` line (a known limit of the tools).
Alternatives rejected: Reading the file only through the manifest (campaigns recorded between I-110 and this change would keep the stated id though they have the file); copying the id into `run.json` (the file is the capture's record, and the manifest names files by path).

### I-128: A search Claude Code's harness runs with rg from PATH is recognised by the process that starts it
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent.AgentProcessUser` and its use in `NormalizeCapture`, `pkg/agent/agent.go`; `Adapter.WithAgentProcess`, `pathSearches` and `pathSearchProgram` in `pkg/agent/claudecode`; `TestPathSearchesRecogniseOnlyTheHarnesssRg`, `TestAPathSearchVerifiesEndToEnd`). Amends I-107 and I-108; the brief's policy of 2026-11-03: an `rg` the harness runs for a claimed Grep is the search, one the agent runs through Bash stays a command claim.
Date: 2026-11-03.
Context: The strings of the 2.1.306 binary show that a build other than the native one, or one with `USE_BUILTIN_RIPGREP` off, runs its Grep and Glob through `rg` found on `PATH`, without `--no-config` (**Reported**, I-107). I-107 recognises only a run of the agent's own program, so such a search showed as an unclaimed command and its claim as Unwitnessed. No private capture holds such a build: of the 202 ground truths at hand, none records an exec of a program named `rg` by a Claude Code process, and no Claude Code capture records `root_image` (**Verified**, scratch scan, local only). The shape is therefore taken from the strings. An `rg` is also what an agent runs through Bash, so the program alone cannot tell the harness's search from the agent's command; the process that starts it can: the harness spawns the search directly, a Bash call runs its commands under the harness's shell wrapper.
Decision:
- A generic hook, `agent.AgentProcessUser`, gives an adapter the capture's root pid (after `Rooted`) in `NormalizeCapture`, as `AgentImageUser` gives it the image, so `Prepare` and the baseline's `Capture` normalize alike.
- Claude Code's `NormalizeStream` first takes a process as such a search when the agent's process forked it, its first exec that took effect runs an absolute program path named `rg` whose kernel filename equals it, unchanged and not cut, with no interpreter and a resolved program also named `rg`, its arguments are a Grep's or a Glob's (`searchArgs`; the harness's `--files` listings are not), and every exec of the process before it is a failed attempt of an absolute `rg` with the same arguments (the PATH lookup). Those attempts, the exec and its exit take the search's target with its folder (`searchTarget`, I-108), so the command the forest reads from the first exec is the search.
- Anything else keeps its command line: an `rg` under a Bash call's shell, one the shell execs in place after its own exec, one reached through a link to another program or as a script, a cut record, an exec not seen to take effect, and every `rg` in a capture without a root.
Consequences:
- A Grep or a Glob of such a build verifies on its folder as a native one does; an `rg` a Bash call runs pairs with the Bash claim only, and a Grep claimed for it is Unwitnessed.
- The trust is a program file named `rg` on the harness's `PATH`, weaker than I-107's own program: an `rg` the agent planted in a directory on that `PATH`, a write the trajectory must claim, runs as the harness's search, and what it does beneath it is read as the search's (a known limit of the adapters).
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and JSON, with no baseline, with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and with `--check-trace-hash`, the private captures' verifications and the attack evaluation are byte-identical to the parent's: none records such an `rg` (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
- A capture of such a build must confirm: that the harness execs `rg` directly as its child (no shell between); its arguments, the native ones less `--no-config`; its working directory and folder (`.` in the session's directory, an absolute path, or `/proc/self/fd/<n>` after the harness's own open, I-69); whether its PATH lookup's failed attempts reach the probe as exec events of the same process; and its start-up listings, which the baseline must then name.
Alternatives rejected: Any `rg` by its file name alone (a Bash call's `rg` would pair with a fabricated Grep claim); a list of install directories for `rg` (I-107 rejected layout patterns: unmeasured and still a path); reading the parent's program (the root's exec is not an event, and a Bash subshell's fork execs nothing).

### I-129: A change to the fs probe lands only with a local privileged test run on a kernel with the BPF LSM, named in a trailer
Status: Accepted, implemented (`check_privileged`, `PRIVILEGED_PATHS`, `PRIVILEGED_TRAILER` in `scripts/check-docs.py`; `scripts/test-privileged-local.sh`). The user's ruling of 2026-11-05.
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: The fs probe's kernel content copy needs kernel 7.0 with the BPF LSM active. GitHub's 7.0 runner (7.0.0-1017-azure) has the kernel functions but no `bpf` in its LSM list, and its 6.17 runners lack the functions (**Verified**, a CI diagnostic step of 2026-11-03), so the copy's privileged tests skip in CI and a green run says nothing about them. The user chose to implement the copy anyway, tested as root on the development laptop (7.0.0-42-generic, BPF LSM active).
Decision: `scripts/test-privileged-local.sh` builds the test binary of each package whose privileged tests need such a kernel (today `pkg/probe/fs`) as the invoking user, runs it under `sudo`, fails when a `TestKernelHash_` test skipped, and, on a clean tree, prints `Privileged-Tested-Locally: <kernel> <commit> <date>`. `check-docs.py commits` requires, for a range in which a commit changes `pkg/probe/fs`, a commit in the range whose message carries that trailer, naming a commit CI can read whose `pkg/probe/fs` equals the head's. CI applies it to every pull request and push to `main`; the `pre-push` hook only to a push to `main` (`CHECK_DOCS_SKIP_PRIVILEGED`), so a draft branch can be pushed for CI before the local run.
Consequences: A change to the fs probe needs a human with a suitable kernel before it lands, also a change that does not touch the kernel copy, since its tests run in the same binary. The trailer is the human's statement: the script makes it easy to state truthfully, the check only verifies that it names the code being merged. A rebase after the test changes the commit, so the test runs after the last rebase. A push to `main` is checked over the pushed commits, so the trailer commit travels with them.
Alternatives rejected: A self-hosted runner booted with the BPF LSM (infrastructure the project does not have); relying on CI alone (the copy's tests would never run); running the script with `sudo -E go test` (root writes into the invoking user's Go build cache, and later unprivileged builds fail on its files).

### I-130: The attack evaluation scores every harness's paired fixtures, reported per harness; Codex's and OpenCode's are reported, not gated
Status: Accepted, implemented (`cdx`, `ocode`, `controlTargets`, `harnessTallies` and the per-field check's "does not apply" line in `pkg/attack/eval_test.go`). Applies E-5's gating rule to the new captures; the brief's goal of 2026-11-05 that every harness gets the same evaluation. Amended by I-131 (2026-11-05): the four captures are gated once Codex's parallel `cat` pairing and OpenCode's two opens of `b.txt` are fixed. OpenCode's capture gated by I-147 (2026-11-06).
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: The attack evaluation (`pkg/attack/eval_test.go`, E-5) scored the paired captures of Claude Code and agy only, so the detection rates of Codex and OpenCode, whose adapters have paired fixtures (`paired-codex-0.166.0-basic`, `-tools`, `-tools-2`, `paired-opencode-1.19.5-basic`), were never measured by the generators, and a rate read across harnesses had no common basis. Its baseline excluded the control command only, where `cmd/analyze` also excludes the file the controls of an agent with an edit tool create (I-88). Its per-field substitution check passed over a field no claim of a capture carries without saying so.
Decision:
- The four captures join the evaluation through their own adapters, with the same generators, seeds, mutation counts and checks as the others, and the version each session states.
- Every capture's baseline excludes the control command and the control edit's file (`agent.ControlCommand`, `agent.ControlFileTarget`), as `cmd/analyze` does.
- `TestAttacksAreDetectedClassifiedAndLocated` also logs one line per adapter, generator and mutation count, pooled over that adapter's captures (`harness <agent> <generator> n=<n>`), so the rates are reported per harness.
- The per-field check logs, for a capture on which no claim carries the field, that the substitution does not apply and the generator's reason.
- The four captures are reported and not gated, by E-5's rule: each fails an acceptance check (the Codex basic honest control is INCONCLUSIVE on a loss, V-39; mislocated fabrications and substitutions among the parallel `cat` commands of the Codex tools task; Unwitnessed target substitutions of `b.txt` in OpenCode's), and none of these is a fault of the generators. They are gated when the failures are explained or fixed, as the `tools` captures were (E-5's amendment of 2026-10-28).
Consequences:
- Every log line of the nine captures evaluated before is unchanged apart from its source line number; the added lines are the four captures', the per-harness lines, and one "does not apply" line for `paired-2.1.286-parallel`'s `output_hash` (**Verified**, `go test ./pkg/attack -v` at the parent and with this change, compared line by line, local only). No capture made before I-88 names the control file, so the second exclusion changes no baseline.
- The numbers are in [../architecture/50_attack_generators.md](../architecture/50_attack_generators.md#the-evaluation-tests): on all four captures every mutation is detected; Codex's classification and location are 0.960 to 1.000, OpenCode's substitution classification 0.660 to 0.727, and OpenCode's omissions include 34 / 59 / 82 undetectable by design (V-36, V-37).
- A regression on the four captures fails no test; it shows as a changed number in the log.
Alternatives rejected: Gating them now (CI would fail on findings that are the verifier's alignment and a capture's loss, not the generators'); leaving them out until explained (no per-harness numbers, and the failures would stay unmeasured); excluding only the control command for the older harnesses and both for the new ones (one baseline rule per harness for the same control design, against uniform treatment).

### I-131: Codex and OpenCode have protocol targets: no bar, no range, no held-out and no tool-heavy task; their attack captures are gated once two alignment fixes land
Status: Accepted, implemented (`config/protocol.json`; `TestRepositoryProtocolHoldsTheRegisteredTargets`). The user's decisions of 2026-11-05. Extends E-8 to every adapter; sets the gating condition of the captures I-130 reports. OpenCode's capture gated by I-147 (2026-11-06).
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: `config/protocol.json` (E-8) listed targets for Claude Code and agy only, so `cmd/analyze` refused every Codex campaign (exit 3), the two Codex parity campaigns included, and would refuse an OpenCode one. D2 registers a FAITHFUL bar for Claude Code only, the protocol registers each other agent's targets by its own amendment, and D8 names held-out tasks for Claude Code and agy only, so those three targets follow from the decisions. The tool-heavy list did not: the code defines a tool-heavy task as one whose claims include a read the harness shares (the read of a background command's output file, V-36), while agy's list holds `long`, although agy has no background mode, which reads as a choice by task name. Codex writes no output file for a background command and runs reads as commands, beneath the command at level 1; OpenCode's bash has no background mode. Separately, I-130 added the Codex and OpenCode captures to the attack evaluation reported and not gated, each failing an acceptance check, without saying when they would be gated.
Decision:
- `codex` (label Codex) and `opencode` (label OpenCode) get entries: `faithful_bar` and `inconclusive_pilot_range` declared `none` with the reason, `d8_held_out_tasks` `[]`.
- `tool_heavy_tasks` is `[]` for both, by the written definition (no read of a background command's output file the harness shares), not by task name. agy's entry is left as it is.
- The four Codex and OpenCode captures of the attack evaluation stay reported, not gated (E-5, I-130), and are gated once the alignment fixes land: Codex's pairing of a mutated command with one of the `cat` commands the agent ran in one call, and OpenCode's two opens of `b.txt` (the read and the later write over it).
Consequences:
- `cmd/analyze` runs on `codex-parity-1` and `codex-parity-2` (exit 0) with no tool-heavy subgroup (**Verified**, local only).
- On the existing Claude Code and agy campaigns the output differs only in the protocol file's SHA-256 (`protocol_sha256` and the report's protocol line): no results line names an agent the campaign lacks, and an empty held-out list adds nothing to the report's sentence on the task groups (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
- A Codex or OpenCode task that later shares such a read, for example a version that writes background output to a file, needs a new entry, not a silent reuse of this one.
Alternatives rejected: `tools` and `long` for both by task name, as Claude Code (the subgroup would then hold runs with none of the reads that define it); `long` only, as agy (the same, by analogy to an entry the definition does not support); gating the four captures now (CI would fail on findings of the verifier's alignment and a capture's loss).

### I-133: agy 1.4.7's searches are the harness's own first exec of each shape it runs, and a search of a file names the file
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.WithAgentProcess`, `search`, `searchOf`, `harnessRun`, `foldSearches`, `fallbackAfter` and `fileGrep` in `pkg/agent/gemini`; `pkg/agent/gemini/testdata/paired-agy-1.4.7-search`; `TestSearchOfRecognisesThe147Forms`, `TestFoldSearchesFoldsARipgrepWithNoExitRecord`, `TestFoldSearchesTakesOnlyTheHarnesssOwnRun`, `TestA147SearchVerifiesEndToEnd`, `TestPaired147SearchAtEachAgreement`, `TestPaired147SearchFoldsTheGrepUnderTheRipgrep`). Follows I-107, I-108 and I-128; amends I-54.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the `search` and `long` task runs of the first agy 1.4.7 parity campaign, and the `long` pilot of 1.4.7, private).
Context: The `search` run was NOT FAITHFUL with and without a baseline, its search an Unrecorded exec beside a corroborated claim: since P-31 a process whose exec failed has no exit record, and I-54's fold required the failed ripgrep to exit with no code before the fallback grep. The `long` run showed three more shapes, the same in the pilot: a search with `MatchPerLine` true runs the ripgrep with `--line-number` in place of `-l` and the grep with `-nH` in place of `-l`; only the session's first search tries the ripgrep, every later one runs the grep alone; and a search whose `SearchPath` is a file runs `/usr/bin/grep -nH --binary-files=without-match -F -- <query> <file>`, without `-r`, in the file's directory. Every such program was the first exec of a direct child of the agy process, the grep's exec carried its image (`/usr/bin/grep`), and the ripgrep's carried none. A `run_command` runs `/usr/bin/bash -c <command>` as that child, and bash then execs a single command in place, so the agent's own grep is never its process's first exec (**Verified**, the raw captures, local only). The binary names the call's other parameters (`Includes`, `CaseInsensitive`, `IsRegex`, `AllowAccessGitignore`; **Reported**, its strings), whose command lines no capture shows.
Decision:
- The recognised command lines are I-54's two and the three above. Each is a search only when the harness ran it: it is the first exec of its process since its fork and, given the capture's root pid (`agent.AgentProcessUser`, as I-128), a child of the root; when the probe recorded what the exec ran (P-36), that is the command line's own program, not cut, unchanged, with no interpreter, and a file of the same name. A capture without a root keeps I-54's check of the line alone.
- A file search's observed target is the kind and the file's absolute path (I-108's absolute last argument), which a claim of that `SearchPath` names. A path with a space, a relative path and every unmeasured option keep their text.
- The ripgrep and the grep after it fold into one search as I-54 states, with one more way to know the ripgrep failed: it has no exit record, its exec carries no image and the grep's exec carries one. Their output modes must agree.
Consequences:
- The `search` run's search is corroborated on its folder, and with V-93 the run is FAITHFUL at every agreement. On the `long` run the six searches are corroborated, one of a directory, one of the workspace and four of single files. The pilot, recorded before P-36, keeps its first ripgrep unfolded (no images) and its grep unverified (no working directory).
- The trust is a program file on the harness's `PATH` that the harness itself started, as I-128's: an agent that planted a `grep` earlier on that `PATH`, a write its trajectory must claim, has it run as the search.
- The new fixture `paired-agy-1.4.7-search` is the `search` run reduced. Every existing fixture's `cmd/verify` output, text and JSON, without a baseline and with baselines at agreements 0.2, 0.5 and 1, with and without `--agent-version` and `--check-trace-hash`, and the attack evaluation are byte-identical (**Verified**, scratch comparison, local only).
- The rerun of `long` must confirm the three shapes again under `watch` with images and working directories; a capture with `Includes`, `CaseInsensitive` or `IsRegex` must measure their lines before they are recognised.
Alternatives rejected: Recognising `grep` by its line alone (a `run_command` of the same line would pass as the harness's search); requiring the ripgrep's exit as before (no capture since P-31 can have it).

### I-134: A run_command canceled after agy moved it to the background is claimed as a command that started
Status: Accepted, implemented (`statusCanceled`, `fieldTask`, `startedTask` in `pkg/agent/gemini/gemini.go`; `TASK_FIELD` and `reduce_task` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `TestParseClaimsACanceledBackgroundCommand`). Amends I-17. Amended 2026-11-05 by V-95: the fixture script also keeps the task's log (148.2).
Date: 2026-11-05 (the `long` task run of the first agy 1.4.7 parity campaign, private).
Context: After its `invoke_subagent` calls failed, the agent of the `long` run ran five searches over directories outside its workspace with `run_command`; each outlived its `WaitMsBeforeAsync` of 5 s, so agy moved it to the background (the step's field 148 names the task, its log and the command), and the agent killed it with `manage_task`, which left the step with status 6 (`Canceled`, its status history 1, 2, 6) and the command's output in the result. I-17 claims no step that did not complete, so the five commands were five Unrecorded execs and five unexplained subtrees, and the run NOT FAITHFUL on actions its own trajectory states (**Verified**, the raw capture, local only).
Decision: A `run_command` step with status 6 whose payload holds a non-empty field 148 is claimed as a completed one is: a `process_exec` of its `CommandLine` from the step's start to its completed time. A canceled step with no task record, and every other status, stay unclaimed (I-17). The report says how many such steps were claimed. The fixture script keeps field 148's task id (148.1) and nothing else of it.
Consequences:
- On the `long` run the five commands and everything beneath them are explained by their claims.
- An exit is not aligned for agy, so a killed command needs nothing more.
- A canceled step that never reached the background (killed within its wait) is still not claimed: no capture shows one.
- Every fixture's output and the attack evaluation are unchanged; the existing agy fixtures regenerate byte-identical with the script's change.
Alternatives rejected: Claiming every canceled step (one canceled before it ran would be a fabrication); claiming from the status history's running state (an `invoke_subagent` that failed shows the same 1, 2, 7).

### I-135: view_file's run of opens of a background task's log collapses as any file's
Status: Accepted, implemented (`taskLogRead`, `taskLog` and `collapseOpens` in `pkg/agent/gemini/stream.go`; `TestCollapseFoldsAReadOfATaskLogOnly`). Amends I-50 and I-118.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the `long` task run of the first agy 1.4.7 parity campaign, private).
Context: `view_file` of a running task's log opened it three times in 1.1 ms, with the open of the project configuration after the first (I-118's shape), and the claim is one open. I-50's collapse leaves the harness's own files alone, the task log among them, so with V-94 two of the three opens stayed unexplained harness activity. The harness's own opens of the log each begin an append, so its process's next event is a write of the log (**Verified**, the raw capture, local only).
Decision: A run of opens of a task log (`tasks/task-<n>.log` under a conversation's brain directory, any home) by the agent's own process collapses to its first, and passes over the open of the project configuration, as a file outside `~/.gemini` does. An open whose process's next event is a write of the log is the harness's, and is never dropped.
Consequences:
- On the `long` run the three opens are one, which pairs with the claim.
- Every other file under the harness's directories keeps its opens, and every fixture's output is unchanged: no fixture holds a task log.
Alternatives rejected: Collapsing every harness file's opens (the controls measured their shapes, which the baseline holds as they are).

### I-136: The custom agent of an agy task with invoke_subagent lists one subagent the capture defines beside it
Status: Accepted, implemented (`agent_md`, `has_subagent_tool`, `sub_agent_name` and `sub_agent_md` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`). Builds on I-71. Not yet measured: agy's quota is exhausted until about 2026-11-09.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the `long` run and controls of the first agy 1.4.7 parity campaign, private; the 1.4.7 binary).
Context: The custom agent the capture writes for agy (`<workspace>/.agents/agents/agent-trace/agent.md`) lists the task's tools and sets `subagent: false` and `inheritCustomizations: false`. On the `long` run each of the agent's three `invoke_subagent` calls, each with another `TypeName`, the custom agent's own name among them, failed with `subagent "<name>" not found or not allowed to be invoked` (status 7), and the system prompt the run sent listed no subagent under `Available subagents:`; each of the five controls was asked for one subagent and recorded none (**Verified**, copies of the conversation databases, local only). The changelog embedded in the 1.4.7 binary (whose last entry is 1.4.6) states how a Markdown agent gets subagents: `inheritCustomizations` decides whether it adopts the user's ambient subagents (1.1.14); an `agents` frontmatter list declares the subagents it depends on, by workspace-relative, absolute or agent-relative paths as `skills` (1.1.27), and an agent that lists `agents: [child.md]` can invoke `child` (1.3.1); an agent that lists `invoke_subagent` in its tools receives the roster of the subagents it declared in its system prompt (1.2.5); an agent with `subagent: false` is not invocable (1.1.5); an agent file needs a `name` and a `description` in its frontmatter. The binary's frontmatter struct has the YAML keys `agents`, `subagent`, `mainAgent`, `tools`, `model`, `inheritCustomizations` and `inheritMcp` (**Reported**, the strings of the 1.4.7 binary).
Decision: When a task's tool set holds the profile's subagent tool, the capture writes beside the custom agent a second agent file, `<name>-subagent.md` (`agent-trace-subagent.md`), with `name: agent-trace-subagent`, a description, `mainAgent: false`, `subagent: true`, `inheritMcp: false`, `inheritCustomizations: false`, and the task's tools without the subagent tool, so it starts no subagent of its own; and the custom agent lists it as `agents: [agent-trace-subagent.md]`. A task without the subagent tool writes the agent file exactly as before. The controls of the task are written by the same function from the same tools (I-71), so they offer the same subagent and keep their prompt; their subagent now runs, where on 2026-11-05 it failed, so a baseline from controls made before this decision does not hold for a run made after it.
Consequences:
- What the rerun after the quota reset must confirm, on the `long` task and its controls: (1) the main agent's system prompt lists `agent-trace-subagent` under `Available subagents:`; (2) the `long` run's `invoke_subagent` completes (status 4) with that `TypeName`, and its result names one subagent conversation (field 140.2.6.2.143.10.1, I-55), whose database the capture copies; (3) the subagent runs `find_by_name`, `run_command` and `write_to_file` (REPORT.md), and answers its caller (by a `send_message` call, as on 1.3.4, or by the harness's notice when it goes idle: whether send_message is offered to a subagent whose tools do not list it is **Unverified**); (4) each control records one subagent with no command and no file (the campaign's subagent count is 1, not 0); (5) the agent selected with `--agent agent-trace` is still the main agent, the subagent file is not listed as a main agent, and the controls' other events are unchanged apart from the subagent's bookkeeping. If (1) or (2) fails, the agent-relative path is the first suspect: a workspace-relative `.agents/agents/agent-trace/agent-trace-subagent.md` is the alternative the changelog names.
- `--baseline-from` compares the control tools and prompt, not the agent file, so it accepts the controls of the 2026-11-05 campaign for a new run though their subagents failed; the subagent's bookkeeping in the new run then reads as unexplained harness activity (INCONCLUSIVE, not hidden).
- Other agents' command lines and files are unchanged: only the agent-md tool-set method writes these files, and only agy uses it.
Alternatives rejected: Offering `define_subagent` (the model would have to define the subagent in the run, a step the controls would also have to make, and the task's prompt does not ask for); setting `inheritCustomizations: true` (the user's skills, rules, plugins, subagents and MCP servers would enter the run, which I-24's isolation excludes); making the custom agent its own subagent with `subagent: true` (the subagent would hold `invoke_subagent` and could recurse, and its definition would be the main agent's).

### I-137: A capture may run the agent as a dedicated unprivileged user, whose home the profile's `~/` paths resolve against
Status: Accepted, implemented (`--run-as` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh` and `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`: `as_root`, `as_agent`, `fetch`, the run-as resolution; `TestARunAsAnotherUserUsesItsHomeAndTheWorkspaceRoot`, `TestARunAsAnotherUserIsRefusedWhereItCannotWork`, `TestARunAsAnotherUserCopiesSQLiteTraces`, `TestTheDefaultRunIsUnchanged`, `TestACampaignDryRunPassesTheRunAsUser`, `TestRunJSONRecordsTheRunAsUser`). The user's decision of 2026-11-05. Amended by I-150 (2026-11-06): the `pass_env` names a profile marks operator only are left out of a run as another user instead of refused, so agy runs as one.
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: A capture ran the agent as the operator with no sandbox. In agy's `long` run of `agy-parity-1` the agent searched the operator's home, other projects and the raw captures, and what it read went to the model provider. The user chose that experiment captures run the agent as a dedicated user, `agenttrace`: no sudo, a home of its own, in none of the operator's groups, with the operator's home at mode 750.
Decision:
- `--run-as USER` (default `$AGENT_TRACE_RUN_AS`; without it, the operator, as before) names the user. The script resolves its uid, gid and home with `getent`, refuses root and a name that is not one, and passes the uid and gid to `setpriv`.
- For a user other than the operator, every `~/` of the profile resolves against that user's home: the binary, looked up on that user's `PATH` (`~/.local/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin`), the version query (as that user, in a throwaway home of its own), the version lock's settings file (the operator's `dir_env` variable is not read), the model catalog, `config_file`, the instruction files moved aside, the trace directory and databases, and `watch --agent-home`, the home of the sensitive-file snapshot that verify reads (V-40, I-111). The run's environment gets `HOME` and that `PATH`.
- That home is closed to the operator, so the script reads it through `sudo -n`: a read (a copy, a test, a hash) as root, then the copy is given to the operator; a write (an instruction file moved aside, the configuration file) and a SQLite read of a live database (OpenCode's online backup, Codex's snapshots) as the user, into a directory of the user's, so nothing in the home changes owner. It asks for sudo at its start; `--check` never prompts and needs a credential already.
- `secret_env` reaches the run on its stdin, unchanged. `pass_env` cannot: a profile with it is refused for another user, naming the agent and the variables, and the operator's session bus is never shared.
- `task-meta.txt` records `run_as=<user> uid=<uid>` and `operator_uid=<uid>`, `--check` prints the first, and the campaign passes the flag to every check and capture, asks for sudo before its checks (a dry run included) for another user, and writes `run_as` (user, uid, operator uid) into each `run.json`.
Consequences:
- Without `--run-as` every command line, environment and `task-meta.txt` is today's for every agent and task (**Verified**: the shim captures of the base and this script, Claude Code `basic`, `tools`, `shapes`, agy `basic`, `long`, Codex and OpenCode `parallel`, give identical `watch` command lines and `task-meta.txt` once session ids are masked, scratch comparison, local only).
- agy cannot be captured as another user until its login is settled: its profile's `pass_env` names the session bus, and this decision leaves that profile as it is.
- The run-as user's PATH holds no tool of the operator's home: what an agent needs besides itself (node and npm for the npm packages) must be installed system-wide.
- A dry run of a campaign as another user asks for sudo, which a dry run did not before.
Alternatives rejected: sharing the operator's session bus with the run (it gives the agent the operator's keyring); granting the operator read access to the user's home through a group or ACLs (the agent's files are created private, and the operator's group must not be the user's); a container or namespace sandbox (the probes' pid and path model would change).

### I-138: Another user's run works in a workspace under a workspace root and is copied back to the output
Status: Accepted, implemented (`capture_one`, `release_workspace` and the `EXIT` trap in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `homes` and `run_as_user` in `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py`; `TestARunAsAnotherUserUsesItsHomeAndTheWorkspaceRoot`). The user's decision of 2026-11-05 (I-137).
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: A run's workspace was `OUTPUT_DIR/ws-<label>`, normally under the operator's home, which the run-as user cannot enter (I-137).
Decision: For a user other than the operator, the workspace is prepared as before in `OUTPUT_DIR/ws-<label>`, then copied as root to `<root>/capture-XXXXXX/ws-<label>`, owned by the user with mode 700; `<root>` is `--workspace-root DIR` (default `$AGENT_TRACE_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, else `/var/lib/agent-trace/ws`), refused inside the operator's home or the repository, and the capture's directory under it is root's with mode 711. `env -C` starts the agent there. After the run (and on any exit of the script for a run in progress) the workspace is copied back to `OUTPUT_DIR/ws-<label>`, given to the operator, and removed from the root; the capture's directory is removed at exit when empty. A locked directory's mode (the `shapes` task) is checked as the user.
