The ground truth and the transcripts name the root's path, which `verify` and `baseline` take as recorded (the ground truth's `workspace`; nothing reads the files there). `scripts/make-capture-fixture.py` takes `<root>/capture-XXXXXX` as the directory the capture was recorded in, and rewrites the user's home (from each ground truth's sensitive-file snapshot) to `/home/user` and the user's name (from `task-meta.txt`) to `user`, and checks for both.
Consequences:
- Every existing raw capture tried gives a byte-identical fixture with the changed fixture script (**Verified**: Claude Code `shapes` of 2026-10-13, Codex `basic` of 2026-11-01, OpenCode `basic` of 2026-11-03, agy `basic` with content of 2026-11-03, scratch comparison, local only).
- The copy back is a second copy of the workspace per run; a run that ends with the machine (no `EXIT` trap) leaves its workspace under the root.
- The operator's home and the user's are both written `/home/user` in a fixture.
Alternatives rejected: a workspace inside the user's home (the agent would work beside its own configuration, and the fanotify mark would cover the home); moving the workspace back with `mv` (another filesystem than the output's is the normal case).

### I-140: A setup script creates the capture user and the workspace root, and prints the agents' install and login steps
Status: Accepted, implemented (`scripts/setup-capture-user.sh`; `TestTheSetupScriptPrintsThePinnedVersionsAndNeedsRoot`). The user's decision of 2026-11-05 (I-137). Amended by I-152 (2026-11-06): agy's install is a copy the operator makes, and its sign-in leaves out the operator-only environment.
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: The user and the workspace root need root once; the agents must be installed and logged in as that user, which runs the agents and is the user's to do.
Decision: `sudo scripts/setup-capture-user.sh [--user NAME] [--workspace-root DIR]` creates `NAME` (default `agenttrace`) with its own group, `/home/NAME` at mode 700, a locked password and the comment `agent-trace capture user (scripts/setup-capture-user.sh)`, which marks it as the script's; it refuses an existing user without that comment, and a group of that name without the user. It refuses when the user is in `sudo`, `admin`, `wheel` or the operator's primary group, or the operator in the user's. It creates the workspace root (root's, mode 711) and warns, without changing it, when the operator's home lets others in. Run again it changes nothing that is as it should be. It runs no agent: it prints, for each profiled agent with a version, the install command of the version `config/agents.json` lists last, the version lock (the profile's `version_lock.env` on the login and version commands, and its `require_setting` file), and the login; `--steps-only` prints them alone, without root. The install commands are a table in the script keyed by the profile's binary (**Reported** from the vendors' instructions, not run).
Consequences:
- The install commands are not in the profiles; an agent the table lacks gets a generic line.
- Whether the printed steps install and log in each agent is checked only when the user follows them, with `capture-agent.sh --check --run-as NAME`.
Alternatives rejected: installing the agents in the script (it would run the agents' installers as root, and log in needs the user); an `install` field in the profiles (left for the profiles' owner).

### I-139: An experiment campaign requires every run to have run the agent as a user other than the operator
Status: Accepted, implemented (`capture_user` in `config/protocol.json`; `CaptureUser`, `RequireCaptureUser` in `cmd/analyze/targets.go`; `RunRecord.RunAs`; `TestACampaignWithoutTheCaptureUserStopsTheAnalysis`, `TestLoadProtocolRefusesWhatItCannotRead`, `TestRepositoryProtocolHoldsTheRegisteredTargets`). The user's decision of 2026-11-05 (I-137). Extends E-8.
Date: 2026-11-05.
Context: The protocol file states what the analysis requires (E-8): every field is required, a requirement the protocol does not set is declared absent with a reason, and a campaign that does not meet one (an agent without targets) stops the analysis with exit 3 and a message naming the file.
Decision: The protocol file gains `capture_user`, required: `{"require_other_user": true, "basis": ...}` or `{"none": reason}`. The repository's file requires it (basis I-139). With it required, `analyze` refuses (exit 3) a campaign any of whose manifest runs lacks `run_as`, lacks the operator's uid, or ran as the operator's uid, naming up to five runs, the count and the file, and saying how to analyse a campaign that is not an experiment (a protocol file whose `capture_user` is `none`).
Consequences:
- Every campaign recorded before I-137 (the pilots and the parity campaigns) is refused under the repository's protocol file; analysing one needs a protocol file that declares `capture_user` `none`.
- The protocol file's schema stays 1; a file without the field is refused as any file without a required target.
Alternatives rejected: a halt (exit 1, results written): the requirement is about what may be analysed as an experiment, as the per-agent targets are; an exclusion rule per run (a protocol change the user did not make).

### I-141: OpenCode's adjacent open claims of one file are one claim, as Claude Code's
Status: Accepted, implemented (`collapseOpenClaims` in `pkg/agent/opencode/claims.go`; `TestAdjacentOpensOfAFileAreOneClaim`). Applies I-32's claim-side rule to OpenCode. `CollapsesOpenRuns` stays false (V-49), for the reason the adapters' page gives.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private).
Context: The OpenCode adapter's `collapseOpens` keeps one open of a run of opens of one file by the harness's process that no other file event of it breaks, across calls, as Codex's and Claude Code's do, but its claims kept one open per call. A read of a file followed by an edit or a write of it, with no other file call between, is two open claims and one observed open. On the parity campaign this left four Unwitnessed opens on the `project` run and nine on the `long` run, each a read followed by an edit of the same file; on the `shapes` run the read of `locked/data.txt` and the write over it shifted the alignment of that file's claims by one, so three claims paired with earlier opens, the harness's open at start among them, and read as outside their interval (**Verified**, `cmd/verify --baseline` on copies of the runs, local only).
Decision: After the calls are claimed, adjacent opens of one file among the file claims become one, as Claude Code's `collapseOpenClaims` (I-32): an open whose previous file claim is an open of the same target (and the same harness-implied marker) is dropped, and the first one's end is widened to the latest end. Claims of other kinds do not break a run, since the observed side only sees the harness's own file events. Subagent sessions are included: they run in the harness's process.
Consequences:
- On the parity campaign, with each run's own baseline: `project` becomes FAITHFUL (42 corroborated, no finding); `shapes` loses its three interval findings and `long` its nine Unwitnessed opens and three interval findings (**Verified**, local only). `edits`, which writes before every edit, is unchanged.
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output, with and without baselines at 0.2, 0.5 and 1, and the attack evaluation's log are byte-identical: the basic fixture's read of `b.txt` and the write over it are separated by the calls on `a.txt`.
- An unclaimed second read of a file the agent claimed reading, with no other file event of the harness's process between, is not reported, as I-32 states for Claude Code; the observed side already did not report it.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring `CollapsesOpenRuns` as well (V-49 would fold a read's open into the harness's open of every workspace file at start, which the baseline keeps for a claim on its path, so the read would pair outside its interval); a time bound on the observed collapse (the opens of one write or edit lie up to 31 ms apart on the campaign, and two calls of one message on one file can be closer still, so a bound decides by timing what the claims decide by order).

### I-142: An OpenCode grep of a file searched the file's directory, which the output or an earlier file call shows
Status: Accepted, implemented (`searchFolder` and `knownFiles` in `pkg/agent/opencode/claims.go`; `TestASearchIsClaimedWithItsFolder`). Amends I-124.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private).
Context: I-124 asked a capture to confirm the program, arguments and folder of OpenCode's searches. On the campaign every grep and glob ran `<home>/.cache/opencode/bin/rg` as a direct child of the harness, in I-124's argument shapes, with the search's folder as its recorded working directory: one grep and one glob of the session's directory on `parallel`, a glob on `project`, two globs and five greps on `long` (**Verified**, the probe's exec events on copies of the runs, local only). Four of `long`'s greps named a file (`textkit/<module>.py`): each ran ripgrep with `.` in the file's directory, so it searched the whole directory, and three of their outputs listed matches in the directory's other files and none in the file; the fourth matched nothing. The adapter read a grep's path as a file only when its output named the path itself, so all four were claimed on the file and read as Mismatched against the directory.
Decision: A grep's folder is its path's directory when the path was a file, which the record shows in two ways: the output names a matched file (`<path>:` on a line of its own) that is the path itself or lies outside it, which a search of the path as a directory cannot produce; or an earlier completed `read` (not of a directory), `write` or `edit` of exactly that path, in the session or its subagents, in execution order. Otherwise the path is the folder, as before.
Consequences:
- On `long` the four greps are corroborated on `textkit`, three by their output and the one that matched nothing by the edits of the file before it; with I-141, `long`'s alignment keeps no Mismatched, Unwitnessed or interval finding (**Verified**, local only).
- A grep of a file that matched nothing and that no earlier call touched is still claimed on the file (a known limit). A path that an earlier call showed to be a file and that a command then replaced with a directory is claimed on its parent: a substitution on an honest run, not a hidden search.
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output and the attack evaluation are byte-identical; no fixture holds a grep of a file.
Alternatives rejected: Reading a path as a file by its extension (a directory may have one); claiming such a grep with no folder, as truncated (a search of any folder would pair with it).

### I-143: OpenCode's read of a file a command has just written is dropped, as its read of what it wrote
Status: Accepted, implemented (`foldRereads` in `pkg/agent/opencode/stream.go`; `TestNormalizeStreamDropsTheReadOfAFileACommandWrote`). Extends I-120's fold.
Date: 2026-11-05 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private).
Context: I-120 drops the harness's open of a file 66 to 72 ms after its own write or edit closed it, with the hash it wrote. The campaign shows the same open after other processes' writes: on `shapes` 56 and 68 ms after the commands `echo two > two.txt` and `sleep 2; echo one > one.txt` closed their files, on `tools` 61 ms after the background job of step 1 wrote `bg.txt`, between two calls, and on `long` 67 ms after `make dist` closed `dist/textkit.pyz` and its seven build files, each open with the content just written where the probe hashed it (**Verified**, copies of the runs, local only). No call names these opens, so they were Unrecorded, and the harness's process made them without a call running. It is the same behaviour as I-120's: the harness reads each file of the workspace that changed.
Decision: The fold also drops, for a close with a content hash by any other process, of a path that is not under the harness's own directories and not ambiguous, the harness's first open of that path after the close, when it falls within `rereadSpan` (one second) of the close, its input hash equals the close's hash, and no write, close, rename or delete of the path by any process lies between. The rule for the harness's own close is unchanged.
Consequences:
- `shapes` becomes FAITHFUL; on `tools` the open of `bg.txt` and on `long` the open of `dist/textkit.pyz` are dropped (**Verified**, local only).
- After a close without a hash nothing is dropped: the probe published `make dist`'s seven build-file closes without one, and the harness's opens of them carry none, so they stay Unrecorded on `long`. Whether to drop such an open on the shape alone is left to the user (it would rest on order and timing only, with no content to compare).
- An unreported in-process read of a file a command wrote, within a second of the write, with the content written, is dropped with it, as I-120 states for the harness's own writes.
- Every fixture's `cmd/verify` output and the attack evaluation are byte-identical: in the basic fixture only the harness writes.
Alternatives rejected: A harness-implied open claimed for each command (V-33's witness is the command's open of the file, which a command that only writes does not make, and the background job's write falls in no call); a baseline rule (the paths are the task's).

### I-146: OpenCode 1.19.5 declares glibc's /etc/gai.conf as read while serving a webfetch
Status: Accepted, implemented (`Adapter.HarnessServedReads` and `servedReadVersions` in `pkg/agent/opencode/harness.go`; `TestHarnessServedReadsDeclareTheResolversConfigurationForAWebfetch`, `TestParityRunsLeaveOnlyTheKnownFindings`). The user's decision (a) of 2026-11-06; the first declaration of V-100.
Date: 2026-11-06 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private).
Context: The `parallel` run's one finding was the harness's open of `/etc/gai.conf` 5 ms into its webfetch of `https://example.com`, after the DNS query for `example.com` and before the connection, inside the call's interval (V-100's context). No other run and no control fetched a host of several addresses.
Decision: For 1.19.5, a `net_connect` claim of a `webfetch` call (the only claim the adapter derives from one) is served by `/etc/gai.conf`. Nothing else is declared, and no other version.
Consequences:
- `paired-opencode-1.19.5-parallel` verifies FAITHFUL at agreement 0.2, 0.6 and 1, with the read listed as a declared harness read; the other OpenCode fixtures are unchanged (**Verified**).
- Other files the resolver may read for a webfetch (`/etc/hosts`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf`, `/etc/host.conf`, the stub resolver's configuration) are not declared: the harness reads them for its model connections too, so the baseline explains them, and no capture shows a webfetch reading them unexplained.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the file for every connection of the harness (its model connections never read it on the campaign, so a read beside them would be hidden for nothing).

### I-147: OpenCode's opens of the workspace before its first model response are dropped, and its attack capture is gated
Status: Accepted, implemented (`dropStartupOpens`, `startupAfterConnect`, `firstOf` in `pkg/agent/opencode/stream.go`; `Adapter.WithAgentProcess` and `Adapter.WithWorkspace` in `opencode.go`; `agent.WorkspaceUser` and its call in `agent.NormalizeCapture`; `ocode(..., true)` in `pkg/attack/eval_test.go`; `TestNormalizeCaptureDropsTheStartupOpensOfTheWorkspace`, `TestParityRunsLeaveOnlyTheKnownFindings`, `TestAttacksAreDetectedClassifiedAndLocated`). The user's decisions (a) of 2026-11-06 and, for a capture without request content, option 1 of the same day. Gates the capture I-130 reported, as I-131 set out.
Date: 2026-11-06 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private, and its seven reduced fixtures).
Context: OpenCode 1.19.5 opens every file of the workspace once as it starts, while its first model request is under way: on the 42 captures of the fixtures (seven task runs, 35 controls) between 32 ms before and 61 ms after the harness's first connection to the model server, and 366 ms to 1.97 s before its first model response where the capture has request content. The baseline learned an open rule per seeded file, and a later open of such a file was a baseline event that pairs only with a claim on its path (V-55): in the attack evaluation a substituted read of `b.txt` read as a fabrication (0.660 to 0.727 of the substitutions classified and located) and an omitted one was undetectable by design (34, 59 and 82 of 100, 200 and 300). The previous lane's prototype dropped these opens before the first response, else before the first connection, and showed two more things: the `parallel` task run had no content and its opens came 61 ms after the first connection, so they stayed and the controls', which had content, did not; and the rules had hidden two shapes of the harness's opens, which I-148 folds.
Decision: The adapter, given the capture's root and workspace (`agent.AgentProcessUser`, and the new `agent.WorkspaceUser`, which `agent.NormalizeCapture` gives the capture's `Workspace`), drops every `file_open` by the root of an exact, unambiguous, untruncated path under the workspace before the bound: the root's first `net_response`, or, in a capture without one, its first `net_connect` plus 200 ms (`startupAfterConnect`). Its other opens before the bound stay: the system files it reads at start are opened again later in varying number, and dropping them shifted baseline counts between runs (tried, and it made four runs worse). A capture without a root, a workspace or either network event is left as it is. No call can run before the model's first response names it, so nothing dropped is the agent's, except, without content, within the 200 ms allowance.
Consequences:
- With I-148, on the seven fixtures at agreement 0.2, 0.6 and 1: `basic`, `edits`, `shapes`, `project` and `parallel` stay FAITHFUL; `tools` and `long` keep exactly their known findings (Bun's library extraction, I-151's warm-up; the seven reads of hashless closes, I-154's kernel hashing). Every honest alignment that was ambiguous because of the start-up rules no longer is, and each corroborated read pairs with its first open. Every fixture of the other adapters is byte-identical, with and without baselines at 0.2, 0.5 and 1 (**Verified**).
- The attack evaluation on `paired-opencode-1.19.5-basic`: omissions, fabrications and substitutions detected, classified and located 1.000 at n=1, 2 and 3; no omission undetectable by design (from 34, 59 and 82); ambiguous runs 11/24/35, 0/0/0 and 0/10/36 (from 81/73/68, 100/100/100 and 81/78/81); the per-field, widened-neighbour and retimed checks all pass. The capture now meets every acceptance check and is gated. Every log line of the other twelve captures is unchanged apart from its source line number (**Verified**).
- Risk of the 200 ms allowance: it is a timing assumption, not an observation. In a capture without request content, an unclaimed read of a workspace file by the harness's process within 200 ms of its first model connection is dropped, which needs a model that answers and a call that runs within that window. The measured margin is 139 ms above the latest start-up open and 166 ms below the earliest first response. A capture with content is bound by the response itself.
- A workspace file the harness opens before the bound for any other reason is dropped too; none was seen.
Alternatives rejected: Bounding a capture without content at the first model connection (the `parallel` run's start-up opens, 61 ms later, became three Unrecorded findings while its controls, with content, dropped theirs); the second connection to the model host (one control of `tools` made only one); a fixed allowance alone without the response when content exists (the response is the observed bound); scoring these substitutions undetectable by design, or count-limited baseline open rules in the verifier (options (b) and (c) of the previous lane's report, not chosen).

### I-148: OpenCode's interleaved opens of parallel reads, and its reread after an edit across another file event, are one access
Status: Accepted, implemented (`pairInterleavedOpens` and `foldRereads` in `pkg/agent/opencode/stream.go`; `TestNormalizeStreamPairsTheInterleavedOpensOfParallelReads`, `TestNormalizeStreamDropsTheRereadAfterAnotherFileEvent`, `TestNormalizeStreamDropsTheReadOfAFileACommandWrote`). Amends I-120's fold, as I-143 extended it. Needed by I-147.
Date: 2026-11-06 (the first OpenCode 1.19.5 parity campaign, `opencode-parity-1`, private, and its reduced fixtures).
Context: With I-147's drop, the start-up open rules no longer absorbed two shapes of the harness's opens, so the prototype left `tools`, `project` and `long` with new Unrecorded opens. On `tools` three reads of one message opened `notes.txt`, `numbers.txt` and `analysis.ipynb` in the order n, u, a, n, u, a within 10 ms: each read opens its file twice, and `collapseOpens` folds only adjacent opens, so six accesses stood for three claims. On `project` and `long`, after four edits the harness opened `/etc/hosts` for its next model request 24 to 43 ms after the edit's close and before its reread of the edited file, 54 to 72 ms after it, so I-120's rule, which required the reread to be the process's next file event, kept it (**Verified**, the reduced fixtures).
Decision:
- Interleaved reads (`pairInterleavedOpens`, before `collapseOpens`): an open of a file by the harness's process is dropped when the process's previous event on that file is an open of it with the same input hash, that open was not itself dropped as the second of a pair, and every file event of the process between them is an open of another file. Events on the harness's own files are skipped. An open without a hash is never paired.
- Rereads (`foldRereads`): after a close with a content hash by the harness's process, its first open of the file within a second, with the close's hash and no write, close, rename or delete of the file by any process between, is dropped, whatever other file events of the process come first, as I-143 already reads a close by another process.
Consequences:
- On the fixtures, with I-147, as I-147 states; without I-147 neither rule changes a verdict.
- Two sequential reads of one file stay two accesses, since the first open of the second read follows an open already paired. Two reads of one file in parallel calls of one message, whose four opens interleave, are one access, as I-141 makes two adjacent read claims of one file one claim; when another claim lies between those claims, the second is Unwitnessed on an honest run (a known limit, not seen).
- An unclaimed read of a file interleaved, open for open, with a claimed read of another file is not hidden by the pairing: its first open stays, and only a second open of the same content is dropped. An unreported read of a just-edited file within a second, with the content written, was already dropped by I-120 when nothing came between; it now is also when other files' events come between.
Alternatives rejected: A time bound on the pairing (the reads' two opens lay 4 to 10 ms apart, and the rule decides by order and content, as I-141 does); declaring `/etc/hosts` transparent to the reread rule alone (other files may come between as well, and the content hash already ties the reread to the close).

### I-150: A profile names the `pass_env` variables that apply only to a run as the operator; agy runs as another user without a session bus
Status: Accepted, implemented (`pass_env.operator_only` read by `scripts/agent-profile.py`; `pass_env_dropped` and `pass_env_not_passed` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; agy's profile in `config/agents.json`; `TestAgyRunsAsAnotherUserWithoutTheSessionBus`, `TestARunAsAnotherUserIsRefusedWhereItCannotWork`). The user's decision of 2026-11-06. Amends I-137.
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: I-137 refuses a profile with `pass_env` for a run as another user, so agy, whose profile passes the operator's session bus (`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`) for its login, could not be captured as `agenttrace`. agy 1.4.7 has a file token store beside its keyring one: its binary holds `cliFileTokenStorage` with `SaveToken`, `LoadToken` and `CLITokenFilePath`, a `compositeTokenStorage` that records keyring errors and timeouts, and the messages `Using file-based token storage because %s detected`, `... because the caller requested it` and `... because a keyring timeout was recorded within the last %v`; its embedded changelog states that the CLI bypasses the keyring when no D-Bus session bus is present (headless hosts and containers) (**Reported**, the strings of the 1.4.7 binary). The user chose that agy, run as another user, signs in once as that user and keeps its token in that file.
Decision:
- `pass_env.operator_only` lists names of `pass_env.names` that apply only when the agent runs as the operator, with `operator_only_why`; a name it lists that `names` lacks is refused by the profile reader. A run as another user gets none of them and is not refused for them; any other `pass_env` name is still refused as in I-137. A run as the operator gets every name as before.
- agy's profile lists both of its names as operator only, so its run as another user starts with no session bus variable.
- `task-meta.txt` of a run as another user records `pass_env_not_passed=<names> (operator only: <why>)`, and `--check` prints `pass_env_not_passed=<names>`. The token store a run used cannot be read without running agy: agy states it only in its own log under `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/log/`, as `ChainedAuth: authenticated via <method> (effective: <store>)` (the operator's logs show `keyring`, **Verified**) and, for the file store, `Using file-based token storage because <reason> detected`.
Consequences:
- What the first run-as capture of agy must confirm, in that user's `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/log/cli-*.log` of the run: (1) a `ChainedAuth` line whose effective store is not `keyring`, and the line `Using file-based token storage because ...`; (2) no sign-in prompt and no `not logged in` error in the run's `watch.log`; (3) no `/run/user/<uid>/bus` for that user (a lingering systemd session would give agy a bus without the variable, since its binary names that path; whether it would use it is **Unverified**); (4) that agy, without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, still runs its language server and tools as in the operator's captures (the harness activity the controls learn).
- The token file is that user's, under its home, which the run can read: the agent can read its own credentials, as Codex's and Claude Code's runs can theirs.
- Without `--run-as`, and for every other agent, the command lines and `task-meta.txt` are unchanged (`TestTheDefaultRunIsUnchanged`, and the operator half of `TestAgyRunsAsAnotherUserWithoutTheSessionBus`).
Alternatives rejected: Sharing the operator's bus (I-137 rejects it: the operator's keyring); a keyring daemon in the user's session (a second login service to run and lock; the file store is agy's own fallback); naming agy in the capture script (I-77: the profile says it).

### I-152: The setup script prints agy's install as the operator's copy and its sign-in without the operator-only environment
Status: Accepted, implemented (`steps` in `scripts/setup-capture-user.sh`; `TestTheSetupScriptPrintsThePinnedVersionsAndNeedsRoot`). The user's decision of 2026-11-06 (I-150). Amends I-140.
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: I-140's printed steps installed agy from `/path/to/agy-<version>` and said that `--run-as` refuses its profile; the operator's agy is in the operator's home, which the run-as user cannot read, and I-150 lets agy run as that user.
Decision: The install table marks a step run by the operator. agy's steps are, as the operator, `agy --version` (it must print the version `config/agents.json` lists last) and a pipe of the resolved executable into `sudo -u USER sh -c` that writes `/home/USER/.local/bin/agy` (the user's own home, from `getent`, else `/home/USER`) with mode 755, so the user owns the file and its directories. A profile with `pass_env.operator_only` gets its login and version commands under `env -u NAME ...` for those names, with a line that checks the user has no bus at `/run/user/UID/bus`. The other agents' steps are unchanged; every agent's install still names the version the profile lists last.
Consequences: The copy is of the operator's binary; that it is the pinned version rests on the `--version` line, and the capture checks it again as the user.
Alternatives rejected: `install -D` as root into the user's home (the directories it creates would be root's); a vendor download of a pinned agy (no pinned download is known; agy installs itself).

### I-151: A profile may name a warm-up run, made once per capture invocation and once per campaign before its first slot
Status: Accepted, implemented (`warm_up` read by `scripts/agent-profile.py`; `warm_up`, `--warm-up-only` and `--warm-up-from` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `warm_agents`, `warm_from`, `warm_ups` and the run's `warm_up` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; OpenCode's profile in `config/agents.json`; `TestTheWarmUpRunsOnceBeforeTheFirstRecordedRun`, `TestAFailedWarmUpStopsTheCapture`, `TestAWarmUpRecordReplacesTheCapturesOwn`, `TestTheWarmUpRunsAsTheRunAsUser`, `TestACampaignWarmsUpOnceBeforeItsFirstSlot`). The user's decision of 2026-11-06 (option b). Amends I-116. Amended by I-155 (every profile names a warm-up).
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: I-116 settles OpenCode's one-time downloads (its plugin from npm, ripgrep from GitHub) by a warm-up the operator runs by hand once per machine and version. The first OpenCode parity campaign showed a first-run effect that a reboot brings back: on its `tools` run, the first after a reboot, Bun extracted two native libraries into `/tmp/.bun-<uid>-*`, which later runs only opened (**Verified**, the campaign `opencode-parity-1`, private). A manual warm-up once per machine does not cover it, and a capture as another user (I-137) needs the warm-up in that user's home.
Decision:
- A profile's `warm_up` names a `prompt` and optional `tools` (else the task's, for a tool set method that passes some). Before the first recorded run of every capture invocation, the capture makes one run of the profile's invocation with that prompt: in a throwaway workspace with the three one-line files, removed after it (under the workspace root, owned by the run-as user, for another user); with the runs' exec chain without `watch` (`sudo setpriv` as the run's user with `--reset-env`, `env` with the runs' environment, the secret reader, the pinned model) and with the instruction files moved aside.
- It is recorded in `warm-up.log`, `warm-up.txt` (agent, version, run uid and user, model, prompt hash, start, end, exit) and `task-meta.txt` (`warm_up=`). A non-zero exit stops the capture before any recorded run.
- A campaign makes it once per invocation and agent, before its first slot, by `capture-agent.sh --warm-up-only` into `OUTPUT_DIR/warm-up/`, stops on its failure, and passes the record to each capture of that agent with `--warm-up-from`, which records it instead of making one and refuses a record of another agent, version, uid or prompt, or one that failed. The manifest lists the warm-ups (`warm_ups`) and each `run.json` carries `warm_up`.
- OpenCode's profile names I-116's warm-up: a `grep` over the workspace. No other profile names one, so other agents' runs, command lines and `task-meta.txt` are unchanged.
Consequences:
- No recorded OpenCode run carries the extraction, the plugin install or the ripgrep download, as long as nothing between the warm-up and the run removes them (a reboot during a campaign brings the extraction back; a campaign resumed after one makes a new warm-up, since it makes one per invocation).
- Each capture invocation spends one more model call; a campaign one per agent and invocation.
- The warm-up's session is in the agent's session store before the recorded runs (OpenCode's database holds it); no trace method reads a session other than the run's.
- Whether the warm-up settled everything is measured by the recorded runs, not checked by the capture: an effect the warm-up's prompt does not reach still shows in the first recorded run as unexplained harness activity.
Alternatives rejected: Keeping the manual warm-up (it misses the reboot effect and needs the run-as user's environment by hand); recording the first run's extraction as harness activity (every first run after a reboot would carry it, and the controls of other runs would not); a warm-up under `watch` (its capture would be one more run nobody reads).

### I-154: A capture setting turns on the fs probe's kernel hashing for every run of a capture or campaign
Status: Accepted, implemented (`--fs-kernel-hash` and `kernel_hash_note` in `scripts/capture-agent.sh`; `--fs-kernel-hash`, `fs_kernel_hash` and the scale record's `file_hashes` in `scripts/capture-campaign.sh`; `ScaleRecord.FileHashes`, `SessionCost.FileHashes` and `reportFileHashes` in `cmd/analyze`; `TestTheFsKernelHashSettingReachesWatch`, `TestRunJSONRecordsTheHashSources`, `TestACampaignWarmsUpOnceBeforeItsFirstSlot`, `TestTheReportListsHashedClosesBySource`). The user's decision of 2026-11-06. Builds on P-48 to P-50.
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: `watch --fs-kernel-hash` (P-48) hashes a written file's content as the kernel copies it at its last close, where the kernel supports it, and the coverage records where each close's hash came from (P-50). It is off by default. The user decided that captures on this laptop, whose kernel supports it, turn it on.
Decision:
- `capture-agent.sh` and `capture-campaign.sh` take `--fs-kernel-hash`, default on when `AGENT_TRACE_FS_KERNEL_HASH` is `1`, else off; no profile field, since the setting is the machine's, not the agent's. When on, `watch` gets `--fs-kernel-hash` for the task run and every control; when off, the command lines are unchanged.
- `task-meta.txt` records `fs_kernel_hash=on` or `off`; each `run.json` records `fs_kernel_hash` from it, and its scale record and each control's carry the coverage's `file_hashes` when the ground truth has it; `invocations.jsonl` records the campaign's setting.
- When the kernel copy did not run, the probe hashes in userspace as without the flag; the capture prints a note naming the coverage's mode after each such run and goes on. It does not refuse.
- `cmd/analyze`'s report lists, after H4b, each task run's hashed closes by source when some run has them; a campaign without them gets no table.
Consequences:
- `--baseline-from` does not compare the setting: the kernel copy changes the source of a close's hash, not the events the controls record. A campaign mixing runs with and without it is visible per run in the manifest.
- The instrument validation (`--validate`) does not pass the flag.
- Every `task-meta.txt` gains the `fs_kernel_hash` line, `off` by default; nothing reads `task-meta.txt` by line position.
Alternatives rejected: Refusing the flag on a kernel without support (the probe already falls back and says why, and the user asked to continue); a profile field (the setting does not depend on the agent).

### I-155: Every agent's profile names a warm-up run
Status: Accepted, implemented (`warm_up` of the `claude-code`, `codex` and `gemini` profiles in `config/agents.json`; `TestTheWarmUpRunsOnceBeforeTheFirstRecordedRun` and `TestAWarmUpRecordReplacesTheCapturesOwn` remove one to check a profile without it). Amends I-151. The user's ruling of 2026-11-06.
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: I-151 gave only OpenCode a warm-up. The first captures run as a new user (I-137, 2026-11-06: Claude Code 2.1.306 and Codex 0.166.0, `basic`, `--run-as agenttrace`) were NOT FAITHFUL with their baselines while every claim was corroborated: in the task run, the first of the agent in that home, Claude Code wrote `policy-limits.json`, `remote-settings.json`, a first `.claude.json` backup and `.last-cleanup` and ran its first shell snapshot, and Codex created its sqlite databases and journals, installed its built-in skills under `~/.codex/skills/.system`, unpacked its arg0 helpers and ran its first shell snapshot. The controls, made after it, never repeat that setup, so no baseline explains it.
Decision: Every profile names a warm-up, so all agents are captured alike (the uniform design): Claude Code runs `true` with `Bash` and reads `a.txt` with `Read`; Codex runs `true` and `cat a.txt`; agy runs `true` with `run_command` and reads `a.txt` with `view_file`; OpenCode keeps I-116's `grep`. Each exercises the shell and a file read, the setup the first run does.
Consequences:
- Every capture invocation (and every campaign, once per agent) spends one more model call per agent.
- A first run in a fresh home, or the first after a reboot, no longer leaves its setup in the task run; setup an agent does only later in a session (a first web fetch, a first subagent) is not covered and stays to be measured.
Alternatives rejected: Declaring the first-run files as harness bookkeeping (they are writes the harness makes once, which a baseline cannot learn and a declaration would hide whenever they recur); a warm-up for the new user only (a reboot or an update gives the operator's home the same first run).

### I-156: The pids of the host's im-config login-shell log line are one token where runs are compared, for every adapter
Status: Accepted, implemented (`canonicalHost` and `imConfigPIDs` in `pkg/agent/agent.go`, applied by `canonical` after the adapter's `Canonical`; `TestTheIMConfigLinesPIDsAreOneTokenAcrossRuns`). Extends I-41. The user's ruling of 2026-11-06.
Date: 2026-11-06.
Context: The first captures run as a dedicated user after the warm-ups (I-155: Claude Code 2.1.306 and Codex 0.166.0, `basic`, `--run-as agenttrace`) corroborated every claim and were NOT FAITHFUL with their baselines on one finding each: the harness's shell-snapshot command (`bash -c -l` for Claude Code, `bash -lc` for Codex) had four and two events beneath it that no control's same command had. They are the debug line of im-config 0.62, which the host's package upgrade of 2026-11-02 installed: `/etc/profile.d/im-config_wayland.sh` sources `/usr/share/im-config/initializer`, which in every login shell runs `systemd-cat -p 6 -t im-config echo @ IM_CONFIG_ENTRY='profile' XDG_SESSION_TYPE ='' UID=<uid> PID=$$ PPID=$PPID` (its debug switch defaults to the non-empty "false", which logs). Beneath Claude Code that is the `systemd-cat` exec and its PATH search for `echo` (two failed attempts and `/usr/bin/echo`); beneath Codex, whose adapter drops failed attempts, the `systemd-cat` exec and `/usr/bin/echo`. Every control ran the same four or two execs, each naming its own pids, so the baseline held five literal lines and the task run's sixth matched none. A login shell reads `/etc/profile` before any file of the home, so no home setup avoids it; the operator's captures predate the upgrade. OpenCode starts no login shell.
Decision: Where runs are compared (I-41: `Capture` and `Prepare`'s match against the rules and the subtree records), after the adapter's own `Canonical`, the shared code replaces the two pids of exactly that line, `( @ IM_CONFIG_ENTRY='…' XDG_SESSION_TYPE ='…' UID=\d+ PID=)\d+( PPID=)\d+$` at the end of a `process_exec` or `process_exit` target, by `N`, for every adapter alike.
Consequences: Re-verified with the controls they hold, both captures are FAITHFUL; every fixture's output and the attack evaluation are unchanged (no fixture holds the line). A line that differs in anything but the two pids (the program, the tag, the entry, the session type, the uid, any text after the pids) stays distinct. Another host script that names a per-run id beneath a harness command reads the same way until it is measured (Known limits of the adapters' page).
Alternatives rejected: A quieter home for the capture user (the line comes from `/etc/profile`, read before the home); removing or diverting im-config on the host (needs root, and every other Ubuntu host with the package would read the same); the rule in the Claude Code and Codex adapters only (the host is not the harness, and the uniform design gives every adapter the same comparison); replacing every number equal to a pid of the run's process tree (wider than the one measured line).
